L42: ## 1 Executive Summary
L43: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L44: Tivozanib is a kinase inhibitor (KI) that inhibits phosphorylation of vascular endothelial growth factor receptor (VEGFR) 1, VEGFR 2 and VEGFR 3 and inhibits other kinases including c-kit and PDGFR \beta at clinically relevant concentrations, as demonstrated via in vitro cellular kinase assays.
L45: The proposed dosing schedule is 1.34 mg taken orally once daily for 21 days on treatment followed by 7 days off treatment for a 28-day cycle until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L46: Tivozanib is a new molecular entity that was not approved in the U.S. for any indication at the time of submission of this application.
L47: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L48: The review team recommends approval of tivozanib, according to 21 Code of Federal Regulations (CFR), Part 314.50, Subpart B, for the following indication:
L49: “FOTIVDA is indicated for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory advanced renal cell carcinoma (RCC) following two or more prior systemic therapies”
L50: The basis for the recommendation is a favorable benefit-risk profile for tivozanib in patients with advanced RCC. In a Phase 3 trial, TIVO-3, 350 patients with advanced renal cell carcinoma were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to tivozanib at a dose of 1.34 mg once daily for 21 consecutive days followed by 7 days off or to sorafenib 400 mg twice daily. Randomization was stratified by International Metastatic RCC Database Consortium (IMDC) risk category and prior therapy (2 prior tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI) vs. 1 prior TKI + prior checkpoint inhibitors (CPI) vs. 1 prior TKI + any other agent). To be eligible, patients were required to have had previous treatment with 2 prior lines of systemic therapy and to have an ECOG PS \le1. Baseline characteristics were balanced between arms on TIVO-3; approximately half of patients on both arms received 2 prior TKIs, approximately one quarter of patients on both arms received 1 prior TKI + prior CPI, and approximately one quarter of patients on both arms received 1 prior TKI and another systemic agent.
L51: The major efficacy outcome measure was progression free survival (PFS) in all randomized patients per Blinded Independent Review Committee (BIRC) review of imaging. A statistically significant improvement in PFS was observed for patients randomized to tivozanib (n=175) vs. sorafenib (n=175). The estimate of the hazard ratio (HR) for PFS was 0.73 (95% CI: 0.56 to 0.95, p-value of 0.016). Median PFS estimate was 5.59 months (95% CI: 4.83, 7.33) in the tivozanib
L52: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L53: arm vs. 3.88 months (95% CI: 3.71 to 5.55) in the sorafenib arm. Consistent results were observed across the pre-specified subgroups. The final median overall survival (OS) estimate was 16.39 months for the tivozanib arm vs. 19.15 months for the sorafenib arm; however, the HR was 0.97 (95% CI: 0.75 to 1.24). The objective response rate (ORR) was 18% (95% CI: 12%, 24%) in the tivozanib arm vs. 8% (95% CI: 4%, 13%) in the sorafenib arm; these results were supportive of the overall efficacy of tivozanib vs. sorafenib.
L54: Progression-free survival is considered by FDA to be an acceptable endpoint to demonstrate the effectiveness of new therapeutics across many oncology indications and has been the primary basis for approval of therapies indicated for the treatment of advanced renal cell carcinoma. Although the overall difference in median PFS between arms was 1.71 months, which is shorter than the 8 week scanning interval on the trial, a post-hoc interval censoring analysis conducted by FDA to explore the robustness of these results with respect to the imaging assessment interval was consistent with the primary analysis. Because the study was a replacement trial design compared to standard of care therapy, the benefit in PFS was deemed appropriate to support substantial evidence of effectiveness.
L55: The OS HR estimate was &gt;1 favoring sorafenib [i.e. HR of 1.12 (0.4-1.51)] at an interim analysis of OS and thus FDA recommended further follow up. The final OS analysis showed a HR of 0.97 (95% CI: 0.75 to 1.24), which reflected a more mature timepoint in the overall survival curve than the earlier analysis. The estimate of median OS was longer on the sorafenib arm, but the median OS is thought to represent only one timepoint on the survival curve and not the overall curve. With that, the FDA conducted a post-hoc exploratory analysis in which the restricted mean survival time (RMST) difference between the two arms was calculated using different cutoff timepoints. Considering the small difference observed between the two treatment arms in the RMST analysis, together with the overlapped survival curves and the HR estimate of 0.97 in favor of the tivozanib arm, the review team concluded that overall, no decrement in overall survival was observed for patients on the tivozanib arm.
L56: The PFS and OS results were also interpreted in the setting of an active control arm of sorafenib, which had previously demonstrated a PFS and OS advantage in patients with advanced renal cell carcinoma compared to placebo. Any efficacy observed in the tivozanib arm in terms of a PFS advantage was thus considered additive to the effect that sorafenib had on its own in this setting. Consistent efficacy was also demonstrated in the ~25% of patients who were pretreated with a CPI, which would be more similar to a contemporary US population in the later-line setting with the recent approval of multiple CPI-containing combinations in the first-line metastatic setting.
L57: The safety profile of tivozanib was considered within range for this patient population and was similar to that observed with other kinase inhibitors. For patients receiving tivozanib on TIVO-3, deaths due to TEAEs occurred in 7.5% including pneumonia, hepatobiliary disorders, respiratory failure, myocardial infarction, cerebrovascular accident, and subdural hematoma. Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 67%, and treatment discontinuations occurred in 21%. The most common TEAEs observed for patients on the tivozanib arm included fatigue, hypertension, diarrhea,
L58: decreased appetite, nausea, dysphonia, hypothyroidism, cough, and stomatitis. Hypertension and hypertensive crisis, cardiac failure, cardiac ischemia and arterial thromboembolic events, venous thromboembolic events, hemorrhagic events, proteinuria, thyroid dysfunction, risk of impaired wound healing, reversible posterior leukoencephalopathy syndrome, embryo-fetal toxicity, and allergic reactions to tartrazine are included as Warnings and Precautions in the tivozanob labeling.
L59: Overall, patients receiving tivozanib on TIVO-3 had lower rates of dose reduction, interruption, or permanent discontinuation than those receiving sorafenib. Although no Patient-Reported Outcome data (PROs) were collected on TIVO-3, analysis of PROs from TIVO-1 which also randomized patients with advanced RCC to tivozanib vs sorafenib (in the first-line setting) suggested an improvement in the analysis of overall treatment bother results. This suggests an overall improved tolerability over an accepted standard-of-care according to several clinically relevant metrics. The safety profile of tivozanib was further supported by review of safety data from an additional 835 patients in other clinical trials who received the dose of tivozanib under current review.
L60: Tivozanib had previously been evaluated in TIVO-1, which randomized patients with treatment-naïve advanced RCC to receive tivozanib versus sorafenib. Although TIVO-1 met its primary endpoint (PFS HR = 0.80 [95% CI: 0.64, 0.99], p = 0.04), a trend towards a detrimental effect on survival was observed with tivozanib (OS HR = 1.25 [95% CI: 0.95, 1.62], p = 0.11). The FDA team convened a meeting of the Oncologic Drug Advisory Committee (ODAC) on May 2, 2013 to discuss this application. The Applicant presented their perspective that the reason for the overall survival results was the one-way crossover aspect of the study design. However, ODAC voted 13:1, "No," in response to the question "Has the Applicant demonstrated a favorable benefit to risk evaluation for the treatment of renal cell carcinoma in an adequate and well-controlled trial?", citing concerns that included the observed decrement in the HR estimate for OS and confounding factors in the trial design.
L61: Despite the negative previous ODAC vote for tivozanib and the subsequent CR letter issued on June 6, 2013 for tivozanib based on review of TIVO-1, the review team felt that the totality of the TIVO-3 data warranted an approval in this case given the PFS advantage, supportive ORR, detailed safety analysis, and the HR for OS that ultimately did not demonstrate a decrement in patients who were required to have had previous treatment with 2 prior lines of therapy. Although the Applicant had requested an indication that included all patients with advanced renal cell carcinoma, the FDA review team granted an indication that reflected the TIVO-3 enrolled population and not the TIVO-1 population, i.e. patients who progressed on two prior lines of therapy.
L62: Labeling was drafted with the input and the agreement of all discipline-specific review teams and the Applicant. All disciplines were in favor of a regular approval. There are no postmarketing requirements or postmarketing commitments for this application.
L63: ## 1.3. **Benefit-Risk Assessment**
L64: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L65: Patients with relapsed or refractory advanced renal cell carcinoma following two or more prior systemic therapies may receive a TKI (different than one received previously) or an mTOR inhibitor on progression; however there is no therapy specifically approved in this line of treatment. Thus patients with advanced RCC who progressed on two prior lines of therapy, including a TKI, were evaluated in TIVO-3, which randomized 350 patients to tivozanib or sorafenib with a primary endpoint of PFS per BIRC assessment.
L66: The primary efficacy analysis for TIVO-3 demonstrated a PFS benefit per BIRC assessment for treatment with tivozanib (n=175) vs. sorafenib (n=175). The estimate of the hazard ratio for PFS was 0.73 (95% CI: 0.56 to 0.95, p-value of 0.016). Median PFS estimate was 5.59 months (95% CI: 4.83, 7.33) in the tivozanib arm vs. 3.88 months (95% CI: 3.71 to 5.55) in the sorafenib arm. Subgroup analyses were consistent across the prespecified stratification factors of IMDC risk category and prior therapy (2 prior TKIs vs. 1 prior TKI + prior CPI vs. 1 prior TKI + any other agent). The final median OS estimate was 16.39 months for the tivozanib arm vs. 19.15 months for the sorafenib arm, with a HR of 0.97 (95% CI: 0.75 to 1.24) for tivozanib. The ORR was 18% (95% CI: 12%, 24%) in the tivozanib arm vs. 8% (95% CI: 4%, 13%) in the sorafenib arm; these results were supportive of the overall efficacy of tivozanib vs. sorafenib.
L67: The safety profile of tivozanib for patients on TIVO-3 was in line with the known safety profile described with other TKIs. Deaths due to an adverse event (excluding disease progression) occurred in 7.5% of tivozanib-treated patients on TIVO-3 and included pneumonia, hepatobiliary disorders, respiratory failure, myocardial infarction, cerebrovascular accident, and subdural hematoma. Permanent treatment discontinuation due to adverse events occurred in 21%. Grade 3-4 adverse events occurred in 67% of patients and the most common (&gt;20%) all-grade adverse events were fatigue, hypertension, diarrhea, decreased appetite, nausea, dysphonia, hypothyroidism, cough, and stomatitis. Warnings and Precautions for tivozanib include hypertension and hypertensive crisis, cardiac failure, cardiac ischemia and arterial thromboembolic events, venous thromboembolic events, hemorrhagic events, proteinuria, thyroid dysfunction, risk of impaired wound healing, reversible posterior leukoencephalopathy syndrome, embryo-fetal toxicity, and allergic reactions to tartrazine.
L68: According to 21 CFR, Part 314.50, Subpart B a regular approval for tivozanib is recommended. All review disciplines are in agreement.
L69: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L70: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	Patients with advanced RCC whose disease has progressed on two prior lines of therapy represent a group of patients with an unmet medical need whose disease is considered incurable.
Advanced RCC is a serious and life-threatening condition
Current Treatment Options	There is no therapy specifically approved in the 3rd line of treatment, however there are several approved TKIs for the treatment of advanced RCC which are active and can be used in the third-line setting (including sorafenib).
Combination therapies which include CPIs are approved in the first-line setting, and in the case of nivolumab, in the second-line setting. Most patients have therefore been treated and have progressed or are intolerant to these therapies by the time they have reached the later lines of therapy.
Other treatment options for patients with advanced RCC in the later lines include mTOR inhibitors and bevacizumab, although these are used infrequently.
Although treatment options exist, none are curative and none is approved specifically for patients whose disease has progressed after treatment with 2 prior lines of therapy. Therefore, there is an unmet medical need for new effective treatments for patients with advanced RCC who have received 2 prior lines of therapy.
Benefit	In the analysis of TIVO-3, the estimate for the HR for PFS was 0.73 (95% CI: 0.56 to 0.95, p-value of 0.016). Median PFS estimate was 5.59 months (95% CI: 4.83, 7.33) in the tivozanib arm vs. 3.88 months (95% CI: 3.71 to 5.55) in the sorafenib arm. Subgroup analyses were supportive.
ORR was 18% (95% CI: 12%, 24%) in the tivozanib arm vs. 8% (95% CI: 4%, 13%) in the sorafenib arm.
The final median OS estimate was 16.39 months for the tivozanib arm vs. 19.15 months for the sorafenib arm, with a HR estimate of 0.97 (95% CI: 0.75 to 1.24).
Tivozanib has demonstrated a PFS advantage over sorafenib in patients with advanced RCC whose disease has progressed after treatment with 2 prior lines of therapy. The submitted evidence meets the statutory evidentiary standard for regular approval of tivozanib for this indication.
L71: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L72: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Risk and Risk Management	The safety profile of tivozanib was similar to the known safety profile for TKIs.
When compared to patients on the sorafenib arm of TIVO-3, patients on the tivozanib arm had lower rates of dose reduction, interruption, or permanent discontinuation.
Although no PROs were collected on TIVO-3, analysis of PROs from TIVO-1 suggested an improvement in the analysis of overall treatment bother results. These metrics suggest an overall improved tolerability over sorafenib which is an accepted standard-of-care.
The safety profile of tivozanib is acceptable in this advanced setting and is manageable with dose modifications and supportive care as needed.
The significant and potentially serious adverse reactions of tivozanib are adequately addressed in the Warnings and Precautions section and the dose modification recommendations included in product labeling.
There were no safety concerns identified during NDA review requiring risk management beyond labeling or warranting consideration for Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy (REMS).

L78: ## 2 Therapeutic Context
L79: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L80: Renal cell carcinoma (RCC) is a serious and life-threatening condition. In 2021, an estimated 76,080 new diagnoses of kidney cancer will be made and 13,780 deaths will occur in the U.S.[1] Approximately 80-90% of kidney cancers are RCC, and 16% of kidney cancers are metastatic at diagnosis. Kidney cancer deaths comprise 2.4% of cancer deaths in the U.S. The 5-year survival of newly diagnosed metastatic kidney cancer is 13%.[2] Clear cell RCC is the most common histologic subtype in adults, comprising approximately 80% of RCC cases.[3]
L81: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L82: Advanced RCC is a malignancy that was historically difficult to treat with systemic therapy due to its chemoresistant nature. The approval of sorafenib and sunitinib in 2005 and 2006 offered patients the first kinase inhibitors for advanced RCC. This was followed by subsequent approval of other kinase inhibitors for treatment of patients with advanced RCC including pazopanib, axitinib, cabozantinib, and lenvatinib, and kinase inhibitors have become a mainstay of treatment for patients with advanced RCC.
L83: Other treatment options available in the U.S., although used less frequently, include bevacizumab, a monoclonal antibody against VEGFR, and mammalian target of rapamycin (mTOR) inhibitors such as everolimus.
L84: Nivolumab was the first CPI approved for the second line treatment of advanced RCC in 2016. Since that time, the treatment landscape in RCC has continued to evolve with the approval of several CPI-containing combination regimens in frontline setting beginning in 2018. These combinations include nivolumab plus ipilimumab, pembrolizumab plus axitinib, avelumab plus axitinib, and nivolumab plus cabozatinib. The standard second-line therapy has not been established since these frontline approvals occurred. However, since there are multiple options for frontline therapy, guidelines generally suggest considering any one of a number of regimens as reasonable choices, including any of the frontline options to which the patient has not been exposed, as well as kinase inhibitors that could be used as monotherapy or in combination. These include cabozantinib with or without nivolumab, nivolumab with or without ipilimumab, axitinib with or without avelumab, and lenvatinib with everolimus.
L85: There is currently no therapy specifically approved in the third-line setting for treatment of advanced RCC, however several KIs have a line-agnostic approval for treatment of advanced RCC.

L95: ## 3 Regulatory Background
L96: ## 3.1. U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
L97: Tivozanib is not approved in the U.S. It has been approved by regulatory agencies in Europe (European Medicines Agency [EMA]) and New Zealand.
L98: ## 3.2. Summary of Presubmission/Submission Regulatory Activity
L99: **September 27, 2012** (NDA 204408): The Applicant submitted an NDA for tivozanib for the proposed indication of adults with unresectable or metastatic RCC based on the results of TIVO-1. TIVO-1 was a multicenter trial that randomized patients with KI-naïve advanced RCC to receive tivozanib versus sorafenib. See also Section 8.1.3.
L100: The study met its primary endpoint (PFS HR = 0.80 [95% CI: 0.64, 0.99], p = 0.04). However, a trend towards a detrimental effect on survival with tivozanib (OS HR = 1.25 [95% CI: 0.95, 1.62], p = 0.11) was observed. Therefore, the FDA team decided to discuss the application at an Oncologic Drug Advisory Committee (ODAC).
L101: **May 2, 2013:** An ODAC was convened to discuss TIVO-1 results and considerations for approval. The Applicant presented their perspective that the reason for the overall survival results was the one-way crossover aspect of the study design. ODAC voted 13:1, “No,” in response to the question “Has the Applicant demonstrated a favorable benefit to risk evaluation for the treatment of renal cell carcinoma in an adequate and well-controlled trial?” Committee members summarized the main concerns as being primarily a lack of confidence in the benefit-risk evaluation due to the decrement in the overall survival trend, trial design with confounding factors that limited interpretation of the results, and low enrollment from the U.S. that might impact the generalizability of results.[16]
L102: **June 6, 2013:** FDA issued a Complete Response letter for NDA 204408. The letter cited one Clinical issue and one Product Quality issue:
L103: **Clinical Deficiency:** The inconsistent PFS and OS results and imbalance in post study treatments make the trial's results uninterpretable and inconclusive when making a risk-benefit assessment necessary for drug approval. FDA recommended that the sponsor perform an adequate and well-controlled randomized trial(s) of tivozanib using PFS as the primary endpoint and OS as a secondary endpoint. The sponsor should ensure the applicability of the results from this trial to the US population. The trial should be powered to detect a difference in PFS and adequately sized to reassure us that there is no adverse effect on OS. The design, conduct, and results of this trial will determine whether one additional trial will be sufficient for approval purposes.
L104: **Product Quality Deficiency:** The proposed dissolution acceptance criterion (Q=(b)/(4)% at (b)/(4) minutes) is not supported by the provided dissolution data and is not acceptable. The data support a dissolution acceptance criterion of Q=(b)/(4)% at (b)/(4) minutes for your product. Implement this criterion at release and on stability. When the NDA is
L105: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L106: resubmitted, the sponsor was instructed to include the revised specifications table for the drug product with the updated dissolution acceptance criterion.
L107: **May 2015 (IND 075547):** A Pre-Phase 3 Meeting was held to obtain feedback on the design of a new multinational phase 3 study (TIVO-3) comparing tivozanib to sorafenib in the third-line setting for patients with advanced RCC who progressed on/after at least two regimens, including at least 1 VEGFR-TKIs (excluding sorafenib). The proposed design included: primary endpoint of PFS by independent radiological review; first secondary endpoint of OS; statistical plan to enroll 314 patients, assuming a PFS difference of two months (4 months vs. 6 months, HR 0.5); stratification factors of number of prior treatments (2 or \ge 2) and number of metastatic sites/organs involved (1 or \ge 2).
L108: FDA agreed generally that the proposed study could support the proposed indication in the third-line setting. FDA's additional feedback was that the sponsor should provide justification for the statistical assumptions around PFS, should state assumptions for OS, and should justify the decision to allow patients to continue study therapy after receiving radiation therapy for progressive disease.
L109: **November 6, 2015:** The sponsor submitted the statistical analysis plan (SAP) for TIVO-3, which stated that: "The interim OS analysis will be done at the time of the final PFS analysis. Assuming a median OS for sorafenib of 12 months and a median OS for tivozanib of 16 months, 245 OS events at the time of final OS analysis and 149 OS events at the time of final PFS analysis will yield approximately 61% power to demonstrate longer OS for tivozanib using the log-rank test, given 2.5% type I error (one-sided). The final OS analysis will be performed when all subjects have been lost to follow-up, have withdrawn consent, or have died, or when all subjects in follow-up have been on-study for at least 2 years, whichever occurs first."
L110: **November 8, 2018:** The sponsor requested a Pre-NDA Meeting after conducting the first interim analysis (IA), which showed a statistically improved PFS with HR 0.73 (0.56, 0.94), p=0.0165 and OS HR 1.12 (0.4-1.51) based on 183 OS events (70% information fraction).
L111: **January 24, 2019:** Pre-NDA meeting. FDA's preliminary comments discouraged the sponsor from submitting an NDA at the time due to the overall survival results, which showed a trend towards a detriment in OS in both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3. The sponsor agreed to not submit an NDA. The sponsor stated that they were considering conducting a formal interim analysis at a later date with a Type I error allocation based on the percentage information.
L112: **February 21, 2019:** The SAP version 4 submitted to FDA, where the final OS analysis was changed to the second IA, to be done when all patients had been on study for 2 years. An additional final OS analysis was added which would occur when 75% of patients would experience an event, at 263 OS events. See Table 5 in Section 8.1 for details.
L113: **October 23, 2019:** Type B Pre-NDA meeting to discuss the second IA OS results: OS HR 0.99

## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L115: (0.76-1.29). The median overall survival for tivozanib was still worse than that of sorafenib (16.4 vs. 19.7 months). FDA stated: "Given the totality of evidence presented to date we recommend that you do not submit this application at this time." The sponsor's rationale was that TIVO-3 was the only trial to demonstrate a TKI with superior PFS over another in the post-immune checkpoint setting and that they were confident OS would not worsen with further follow-up. L132: ## 5 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L133: ## 5.1. Executive Summary
L134: The nonclinical studies for tivozanib hydrochloride were reviewed on May 24, 2013 under NDA # 204408 (Applicant-owned) for the treatment of patients with advanced renal cell carcinoma (RCC). See Appendix 19.3. In the current executive summary, risk estimates are calculated based on the maximum recommended human dose of 1.34 mg/day for adult patients with *relapsed or refractory* advanced RCC.
L135: Tivozanib hydrochloride is an anticancer agent of the Vascular Endothelial Growth Factor Receptor (VEGFR) Tyrosine Kinase inhibitor class and is being developed for the treatment of advanced renal cell carcinoma. *In vitro* cellular kinase assays demonstrated that tivozanib is an inhibitor of VEGF receptors -1, -2, and -3 (IC_{50} 0.107, 0.081, and 0.122 ng/mL, respectively) and other kinases such as c-Kit and PDGFR\beta at higher IC_{50}'s (0.83 and 0.88 ng/mL, respectively). The IC_{50} of these kinases is well below the C_{min} value achieved in RCC patients (16 to 30.9 ng/mL) following repeat dosing at the recommended dose at 1.34 mg/day, suggesting continuous inhibition of these kinases throughout the treatment period.
L136: *In vivo* pharmacodynamic studies in mice and rats bearing human or murine xenograft tumors demonstrated that tivozanib inhibited tumor growth at a dose of 0.2 mg/kg in rats, via inhibition of angiogenesis and vascular permeability. This dose is associated with plasma levels of tivozanib in rodents (C_{max} of 70 - 108 ng/mL; AUC of 1640 ng*hr/mL), which were within range of the C_{max} and AUC achieved in patients with solid tumors following repeat dosing at the recommended dose of 1.34 mg/day (C_{max} was -86.9 ng/mL; AUC_{0-24} was 1510 ng*hr/mL).
L137: The selection of animal models to be used in toxicology and safety pharmacology studies (rats and monkeys) was based on PK and/or metabolic profiles of tivozanib. The oral route was utilized in nonclinical studies as this is the intended clinical route of exposure. The tivozanib toxicokinetic profile following repeat oral administration was approximately linear in rats and monkeys, with C_{max} and AUC increasing in an approximately dose proportional manner. No gender differences were shown in the rat or monkey. Interspecies differences in the rate of absorption (T_{max}) and elimination half-life (t_{1/2}) were reported. The elimination t_{1/2} in rats (9-45 hours) was greater than that reported in monkeys (8-12 hours), but was considerably shorter than that reported for humans (4.5-5.1 days). To account for these differences, the dosing regimen in the nonclinical toxicology studies was daily as compared to the clinical regimen (once daily, 3 weeks on and 1 week off). In *in vitro* distribution studies conducted using animal or human plasma, it was shown that tivozanib bound to plasma proteins at &gt; 97.6%, and protein binding was predominantly to albumin, with low binding to \alpha-1-acid glycoprotein. Tivozanib mainly partitioned into the plasma/serum component of blood. *In vivo* metabolism studies demonstrated that the parent compound (tivozanib) was the major component in serum and feces of rats and monkeys. Tivozanib appeared to be metabolized mainly by
L138: oxidation, demethylation and glucuronidation. All prominent human metabolites (M1 through M24) were present in both rat and monkey, with the exception of M11, which was unique to human hepatocytes (minor &gt;0.5%), and M10, which was not observed in rat hepatocytes. M14 was present at &gt;10% and was designated as a major metabolite in human, monkey and rat. Comparison of the metabolic profile of tivozanib in liver microsomes showed that monkey most closely resembled the human, followed by the rat. These data provided support for species selection. Excretion studies in rats and monkeys showed that tivozanib was mainly excreted in feces in both species, and through the bile in rats.
L139: In safety pharmacology studies, no effects on respiration rate, tidal volume, neurobehavioral endpoints or physiological parameters were reported in rat studies. In single and repeat-dose cardiovascular studies in telemetered cynomolgus monkeys, a dose of 3 mg/kg (HED= 58 mg/day; 43-fold of the recommended human dose) resulted in increases in systolic, diastolic and mean arterial blood pressure. At doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day in the monkey (HED=19.5 mg/day; 15-fold of the recommended human dose), decreases in heart rate, cardiac output, renal arterial blood flow and cardiac arterial blood flow were reported. A decrease in vascular relaxation response and decrease in sensitivity to acetylcholine were also reported at doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day, which may have played a role in the reported hypertension response in monkeys.
L140: Using the core battery of genetic toxicology studies recommended in ICH guidances, tivozanib was not shown to be mutagenic, aneugenic or clastogenic; however, tivozanib produced changes in chromosomal number (polyploidy, hyperdiploidy) at \ge 2 \mug/mL (23-fold times the plasma level at recommended human dose).
L141: In a male and female fertility study in rats, doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day resulted in a decrease in animal mating (HED=0.97 mg/day; 0.72-fold of the recommended human dose) and doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day produced infertility (HED=2.9 mg/day; 2-fold of the recommended human dose). In an embryo-fetal developmental study in rats, tivozanib was administered during the period of organogenesis. Tivozanib at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED=0.29 mg/day; 0.2-fold of the recommended human dose) resulted in maternal toxicity, increases in early and late resorptions, an increase in fetal external malformations (body edema, short tail and kinked tail) and an increase in skeletal developmental delays. In an embryo-fetal developmental study in rabbits, tivozanib at 1 mg/kg/day (HED=19.5 mg/day; 14.6-fold of the recommended human dose) administered orally during the period of organogenesis resulted in fetal malformations including ventricular septal defects, constricted pulmonary artery vessels and distended aorta. No maternal toxicity was reported.
L142: Based on findings in animal studies (embryo-fetal development studies) and its mechanism of action, FOTIVDA can cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. It is recommended to advise females of reproductive potential to use effective contraception during treatment with FOTIVDA and for 1 month after the last dose. The duration of contraception use is consistent with the FDA oncology reproductive toxicology guidance for non-genotoxic
L143: and teratogenic pharmaceuticals, where it recommends a contraception period of 5 half-lives after completion of therapy (111 hours (t_{1/2}) X 5 = 23.1 days, rounded to one month).
L144: In repeat-dose general toxicology studies, the toxicity profiles of tivozanib in the rat and monkey showed some overlap. The predominant toxicities in both species were associated with the following systems/organs: adrenal gland, gastrointestinal system (stomach, duodenum, and pancreas), kidney, bone (femur and tibia), liver, brain, pituitary gland, heart, incisors and reproductive system (ovaries and uterus). Clinical pathology changes and body weight decreases were also reported in both species.
L145: Oral administration of tivozanib to rats for 13 weeks resulted in mortalities and severe clinical observations (clinical signs typically associated with morbidity, malocclusion, missing teeth and white teeth) at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=0.97 mg/day; 0.72-fold of the recommended human dose). The observed dental toxicity correlated with microscopic findings in the incisors of the rats which may have contributed to the reported decreases in body weights and food consumption. The effect on incisors is unlikely to be relevant to targeted patient population (adults) due to the difference in incisor growth patterns in rats and humans. Clinical pathology changes were reported at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED=0.29; 0.21-fold the recommended human dose) and included changes in hematology (increases in RBC and WBC parameters), clinical chemistry (increases in iron metabolism, liver parameters, LDH/creatinine, triglycerides and renal parameters and decrease in protein parameters) and urine parameters (increases in protein, urobilinogen, bilirubin and hyaline casts). At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=0.97; 0.72-fold the recommended human dose) the target organs with significant toxicity were the adrenal glands, kidney, bone-femur, liver/bile duct, brain, pituitary, incisors, pancreas and duodenum. Less pronounced (lower incidence and/or severity) microscopic findings were reported in the heart, reproductive organs, bone marrow, adrenal medulla and stomach. Partial recovery of most effects was reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, although renal effects were not recoverable at these doses. Toxicities at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day overlapped with those at the lower doses, but were more severe, occurred at a higher incidence and were for the most part not recoverable.
L146: Oral administration of tivozanib in rats for 26 weeks produced non-adverse decreases in body weights, abnormal whitening of the teeth and changes in hematology/clinical chemistry at 0.08 mg/kg/day. No test article-related findings were reported by the end of the treatment or the recovery periods. The doses assessed in this study were low, and given the lack of appreciable toxicity, were considered insufficient to enable an adequate determination of dose-response relationships.
L147: Oral administration of tivozanib in monkeys for 13 weeks produced microscopic findings in the brain and adrenal cortex at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=1.9 mg/day; 1.4-fold of the recommended human dose). At doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day (HED=5.8 mg/day; 4.3-fold of the recommended human dose), adverse clinical signs, decrease in body weight and changes in hematology (increases in RBC, decreases and in WBC parameters), clinical and urine chemistry
L148: parameters (increases in CK, SELA-\alpha &amp; \beta, iron, phospholipids; decreases in ALP, SDH, total bile acid, amylase, UIBC, U LDH and U creatinine) were reported. The predominant test article-related microscopic findings evident at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day included bone (femur and tibia), gastric system (stomach, duodenum and pancreas), reproductive system (ovary and corpora lutea) and lymphoid organs (lymphoid depletion of thymus, lymph nodes and spleen). The majority of the findings were recoverable after a 2- or 5-week recovery period.
L149: In a 39-week oral toxicity study in monkeys, the highest dose tested 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=1.95 g/day; 1.5-fold of the recommended human dose) was well tolerated. As with the 26-week rat study, the doses assessed in the 39-week monkey study were low, and given the lack of appreciable toxicity, were considered insufficient to enable an adequate determination of dose-response relationships.
L150: Ocular and cutaneous phototoxicity studies with tivozanib in rats did not show skin or ocular phototoxicity.
L151: The nonclinical data submitted in this NDA are adequate to support approval of FOTIVDA for the proposed indication.

L158: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L159: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L160: Tivozanib hydrochloride is a tyrosine kinase inhibitor of vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGFR)-1, -2, and -3. In the current original NDA submission, the applicant seeks approval of tivozanib for the treatment of patients with relapsed or refractory RCC. The Applicant proposed dosing regimen is 1.34 mg oral once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with or without food.
L161: The primary evidence of efficacy and safety is provided by a pivotal randomized, open-label, controlled trial (TIVO-3) in patients with relapsed or refractory advanced RCC, who received prior systemic treatments (2 TKIs, a TKI plus a checkpoint inhibitor or a TKI plus other agent). Safety data included 1008 patients with RCC treated with tivozanib who were enrolled in 6 monotherapy studies that included patients from study TIVO-3 comparing tivozanib to sorafenib.
L162: The clinical pharmacology review focused on dose selection and the acceptability of tivozanib dosing recommendations for general population, for patients with organ dysfunction, and for drug-drug interactions.
L163: ## Recommendations
L164: The office of clinical pharmacology has reviewed the information contained in NDA 212904. This NDA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized below:
L165: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and Supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from a pivotal phase 3, randomized, open-label, controlled trial (TIVO-3)
General dosing instructions	The proposed dosing regimen of 1.34 mg oral once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with or without food is acceptable and is supported by the improvement in PFS, compared to the active control, observed in patients with relapsed or refractory RCC.
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	Patients with hepatic impairment:Mild: No dose adjustment
Moderate: Reduce dose to 0.89 mg oral once daily for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with or without food
Severe: Avoid use

Patients with renal impairment:No dose adjustment
L166: Drug-Drug interactions	CYP3A Inducers: Tivozanib is primarily metabolized by CYP3A4.Avoid coadministration of tivozanib with strong CYP3A4 inducer.

Labeling	Generally acceptable, upon the Applicant's agreement to the FDA revisions to the label.
L167: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L168: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L169: Tivozanib demonstrated linear pharmacokinetics (PK) in the dose range of 0.89 -1.78 mg following both single dose and repeated doses. At the recommended dosage of 1.34 mg QD, the steady state Cmax (%CV) of Tivozanib was 86.9 (66%) ng/mL and the steady state area under the concentration-time curve (AUC0-24h) (%CV) was 1510 (67%) ng*hr/mL. The plasma elimination half-life (T1/2) is 111 hours. Following repeated 1.34 mg QD administration, steady-state was achieved by 14 days after the first dose and accumulation at steady-state is approximately 6- to 7- fold the exposure observed at single-dose levels.
L170: ## ADME properties
L171: **Absorption:** The median time to peak concentration (T_{max}) ranged from 3 to 24 hours in most studies. This is most likely due to enterohepatic recirculation of tivozanib, which was also seen in non-clinical mouse, rat and monkey studies. The C_{max} of tivozanib decreased by 23% (C_{max} GMR 0.77, 95%CI: 0.729-0.824) and the AUC_{0-\infty} did not change (AUC_{0-\infty} GMR 1.074, 95%CI: 1.028-1.123) when tivozanib was taken with a high-calorie, high-fat meal (approximately 500-600 fat calories, 250 carbohydrate calories and 150 protein calories) compared to those in the fasted state.
L172: **Distribution:** The typical apparent volume of distribution of tivozanib is 121 L, volume of distribution was found to increase nearly proportionally with body weight. In vitro protein binding of tivozanib is &gt;99%. The blood-to-plasma ratio is 0.495 to 0.615.
L173: **Metabolism:** Tivozanib can be metabolized by CYP3A4 and CYP1A1 in vitro. As CYP1A1 is mainly expressed in extrahepatic tissues such as the lung and intestine, it is unlikely that this isoform would be extensively involved in generating the metabolites observed following incubations with human liver microsomes. Following a single oral dose of 1.34 mg of (160 \mu Ci) of [^{14}C]-tivozanib, unchanged tivozanib was the predominant circulating component in serum, ranging from approximately 77.6% to 95.1% (median = 90.1%) of the total radioactivity. Of the minor unknown metabolites detected, each represented less than or equal to a mean of ~4% of the total radioactivity.
L174: **Elimination:** The T_{1/2} of tivozanib was 111 hours. Typical CL/F was 0.75 L/h.
L175: **Excretion:** Following a single oral dose of 1.34 mg of (160 \muCi) of [^{14}C]-tivozanib to healthy male patients, 79.3% of the radioactive dose was recovered in feces as both unchanged tivozanib (7.82% to 46.1%, median = 26.2%) and metabolites (the predominant metabolites together represented a total of 6.35% to 34.3%, median=20.5%) and 11.8% in urine (unchanged tivozanib not detected).
L176: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L177: ## General Dosing
L178: The proposed dosing regimen is 1.34 mg oral once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with or without food.
L179: ## *Dose Selection Rationale and Dose- and Exposure-Response Relationships*
L180: The doses of 0.89, 1.34, and 1.78 mg were evaluated in the first-in-human dose escalation study (Study AV-951-03-B01). The selection of the proposed dose was supported by dose-dependent effect of tivozanib on dose-limiting toxicity and hypertension in the first-in-human study, and also supported by pharmacodynamic analysis, which demonstrated dose-dependent increases in serum VEGF-A levels, dose-dependent decreases in serum soluble VEGFR-2 levels and a dose-dependent reduction in tumor perfusion. Due to the incidence of dose-limiting toxicities (DLTs) at the highest dose level tested (1.78 mg), the 1.34 mg dose of tivozanib was identified as the highest dose level with an acceptable safety/tolerability profile.
L181: With regard to the on/off period, the first-in-human study was initiated with a 4 weeks on/2 weeks off schedule, and then altered to a 3 weeks on/1 week off schedule due to the following reasons: 1) Adverse events in FIH Study AV-951-03-B01 were rapidly reversible upon stopping treatment, supporting a shorter rest period; 2) Nonclinical studies showed that tumors regrew after cessation of tivozanib.
L182: The proposed dosing regimen (1.34 mg oral once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with or without food) was subsequently studied in four studies: AV-951-07-201, AV-951-10-202, AV-951-09-301 (TIVO-1), AV-951-15-303 (TIVO-3). The primary evidence of efficacy and safety is provided by pivotal, randomized, open-label, controlled trial (TIVO-3) in patients with relapsed or refractory advanced RCC, who received prior systemic treatments (2 TKIs, a TKI plus a checkpoint inhibitor or a TKI plus other agent). Safety data included 1008 RCC patients treated with FOTIVDA who were enrolled in 6 monotherapy studies that included patients from the pivotal randomized study TIVO-3 comparing tivozanib to sorafenib. The proposed dosage is effective and has a manageable safety profile.
L183: Dose-response relationship was not established, the exposure-response analysis was based on only one dose level, which is the proposed dosage 1.34 mg. Exposure-response analysis demonstrated that increased tivozanib exposure by C_{avg} quartile was associated with a trend
L184: towards increased PFS, the highest tivozanib exposure quartile (Q4) an estimated relative risk reduction of approximately 33% compared to the lowest tivozanib exposure quartile (Q1). In addition, exposure-dependent increases in rates of hand-foot syndrome (HFS, a.k.a., palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia [PPE]) was also demonstrated.
L185: The above data supports the selection of the highest dose level with an acceptable safety/tolerability profile, which is the proposed dosing regimen 1.34 mg oral once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with or without food.
L186: ## Therapeutic Individualization
L187: No clinically significant differences in the PK of tivozanib were observed based on gender, race (93% Caucasian, 3% African American, 2% Asian, 2% others), age (18-84 yrs), body weight (39-158 kg), mild to severe renal impairment, or mild hepatic impairment.
L188: Patients with moderate hepatic impairment eliminated Tivozanib more slowly, mean tivozanib exposure (AUCt) increased 1.62-fold. Reduce the recommended dosage of tivozanib to 0.89 mg capsule taken orally once daily for 3 weeks on treatment followed by 1 week off treatment for a 28-day cycle for patients with moderate hepatic impairment.
L189: The effect of severe hepatic impairment on the pharmacokinetics of tivozanib is unknown. For patients with severe hepatic impairment, tivozanib is not recommended.
L190: ## Outstanding Issues
L191: None.
L192: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L193: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L194: **Table 3. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetics**
L195: Pharmacology
Mechanism of Action	Tivozanib is a kinase inhibitor that targets 3 vascular endothelial growth factor receptors (VEGFR1, 2 and 3)
Active Moieties	Unchanged tivozanib is the predominant circulating component in serum, ranging from approximately 77.6% to 95.1% (median = 90.1%) of the total radioactivity. Of the minor unknown metabolites detected, each represented less than or equal to a mean of ~4% of the total radioactivity.
QT prolongation	No large (>20 msec) QTc prolongation effect of tivozanib was detected in this QT assessment.
The effect of tivozanib was evaluated in an open-label, multiple dose, single arm study in patients with advanced solid tumors (Study
L196: # AV-951-10-112). The highest dose evaluated was 1.34 mg (1.34 mg tivozanib free base is equivalent to 1.5 mg tivozanib hydrochloride as cited in table below) once daily for 21 days, which covers the therapeutic exposures of tivozanib. The data were analyzed using the exposure-response analysis as the primary analysis, which did not suggest that tivozanib is associated with large mean increases in the QTc interval.
The results from this analysis are supported by the available nonclinical data, categorical analysis, and by-timepoint analysis. Refer to QT-IRT review for more information.

General Information
Bioanalysis	Tivozanib in serum was measured using validated LC-MS/MS methods. A summary of the method validation is included as an appendix (19.4.1)
Healthy Volunteers vs. Patients	PopPK analysis demonstrated no effect of disease status (healthy, solid tumor, RCC) on the PK of tivozanib.
Drug exposure at steady state following the therapeutic dosing regimen	When 1.34 mg was administered daily for 3 weeks on/1 week off in patients with RCC, C_{max} was 86.9 (66%) ng/mL and AUC_{0-24} was 1510 (67%) ng*hr/mL at steady state.
Minimal effective dose or exposure	Not available.
Maximal tolerated dose or exposure	1.34 mg once daily as a monotherapy
Dose proportionality	Exposure (AUC and C_{max}) of tivozanib generally increased in a dose-proportional manner across the dose range evaluated (0.89 mg to 1.78 mg).
Accumulation	Accumulation at steady-state is approximately 6- to 7- fold the exposure observed at single-dose levels
Variability	Population PK modelling showed high variability (82.5%) in mean transit time (MTT) for absorption, likely due to enterohepatic recirculation of tivozanib, but moderate variability in distribution and elimination parameters (40.5% and 28.1% for CL/F and V1/F, respectively).
Absorption
Oral	Absolute oral bioavailability has not been determined.
Bioequivalent	The proposed to-be-marketed formulation is 0.89 mg and 1.34 mg strength capsules.
L197: the capsule formulation is used throughout the clinical studies and is the intended commercial formulation.
Oral Tmax	The pharmacokinetics (PK) of tivozanib in humans are characterized by a variable absorption period with a median time to peak serum concentration (Tmax) ranging from approximately 3 to 24 hours in most studies.
Food Effect for 1.34 mg capsule fed/fasted GMR (90% CI)	Negative food effect

0.775 (0.729, 0.824) 1.051 (0.998, 1.106) 1.074 (1.028, 1.123)
Substrate transporter systems [in vitro]	Tivozanib is neither a substrate nor inhibitor of the transporter proteins MDR1 (P-gp), OCT1, OATP1B1, OATP1B3 and BSEP. Furthermore, tivozanib is not an in vitro inhibitor of OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2-K nor substrate of MRP2 and BCRP. Tivozanib inhibits the transporter BCRP in vitro, however, the theoretical GI concentration (1.05 µM) is < 2-fold greater than the in vitro IC50 (0.68 µM) for BCRP, therefore inhibition of BCRP is likely to be incomplete, also, because of the low solubility and low permeability of tivozanib, the GI concentration is likely lower than the theoretical GI concentration. Therefore, the inhibition of BCRP is considered clinically insignificant.
Distribution
Volume of Distribution	Volume of distribution was found to increase nearly proportionally with body weight. Typical value (95% CI) is 123 (121-126) L.
Plasma Protein Binding	>99%
Blood to Plasma Ratio	0.495 - 0.615
Elimination
Half-life	The half life of tivozanib is 111 hours. Females had a longer estimated T1/2 (129 hours) compared to males (110 hours).
Clearance	The apparent clearance (CL/F) is 0.75 L/h. Females had a 24.5% lower clearance (typical value 0.651 L/h) versus males (typical value 0.862 L/h).
Metabolism
Primary metabolic pathway(s)	Tivozanib is primarily metabolized by CYP3A4. UGT-mediated metabolism of tivozanib required an initial oxidative (Phase 1) step catalyzed by CYP450.
Inhibitor/Inducer	Tivozanib does not inhibit CYP isoforms 1A2, 2A6, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6 and 3A4 in a clinical meaningful way. Tivozanib was not found to be a strong inhibitor of UGT activities, UGT isoforms not identified.
L198: Tivozanib does not induce CYP1A, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, CYP2C19 or CYP3A.
Excretion	
Primary excretion pathways	Following a single oral dose of 1.34 mg radiolabeled tivozanib to healthy male patients, the majority (mean 79.3%) of [^{14}C]-tivozanib was excreted in the feces as both unchanged tivozanib and metabolites, while the remainder (mean 11.8%) was excreted as metabolites in urine.
L199: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L200: ## Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L201: Yes, the primary evidence of effectiveness was obtained from a pivotal, randomized, open-label, controlled trial (TIVO-3). A summary of the clinical effectiveness and safety results based on Study TIVO-3 are provided in Section 7.
L202: Exposure-response analyses for efficacy demonstrated exposure-dependent increases in progression-free survival (PFS), providing supportive evidence of effectiveness for tivozanib. Exposure-response analyses for safety demonstrated exposure-dependent increases in rates of hand-foot syndrome (HFS, a.k.a., palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia [PPE]). In addition, the proposed dose reduction strategy (1.34 mg to 0.89 mg) to manage tolerable \legrade 3 AEs was proposed and it appears reasonable.
L203: ## Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L204: Yes, the proposed dosing regimen is considered appropriate from the following aspects:
L205: - The dose-dependent, dose-limiting toxicities (DLTs) and hypertension in the first-in-human study, as well as the dose-dependent increases in serum VEGF-A levels, dose-dependent decreases in serum soluble VEGFR-2 levels, and dose-dependent reduction in tumor perfusion supported the selection of a highest dose level (1.34 mg) with an acceptable safety/tolerability profile. Due to the incidence of DLTs at the highest dose level tested (1.78 mg) in the dose-escalation study, and the acceptable safety profile in patients with RCC in the pivotal TIVO-3 study, the 1.34 mg dose of tivozanib was considered appropriate.
- With regard to the on/off period, the first-in-human study was initiated with a 4 weeks on/2 weeks off schedule, and then altered to a 3 weeks on/1 week off schedule due to the following reasons: a) Adverse events in FIH Study AV-951-03-B01 were rapidly reversible upon stopping treatment, supporting a shorter rest period; b) Nonclinical
L206: studies showed that tumors regrew after cessation of tivozanib. The proposed treatment schedule was considered appropriate due to the manageable safety profile.
L207: 3) The proposed dosage of 1.34 mg QD, 3 weeks on, 1 week off is supported by the improvement in PFS, compared to the active control in the pivotal TIVO-3 study. The high relative dose intensity (88.3%) despite the longer mean exposure than the active control, suggests that tivozanib is well-tolerated.
L208: ## Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L209: ## Gender
L210: Although gender has an effect on the clearance of tivozanib, females had a 24.5% lower clearance (typical value 0.651 L/h) versus males (typical value 0.862 L/h). The effect of gender on PFS was not clinically meaningful, therefore, a dose adjustment is not warranted.
L211: ## Hepatic Impairment
L212: Yes, for patients with moderate hepatic impairment, the dosage should be reduced to 0.89 mg oral once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with or without food; for patients with severe hepatic impairment, tivozanib is not recommended.
L213: The effect of hepatic impairment on the PK of tivozanib was assessed in a dedicated hepatic impairment study and by population PK analysis.
L214: In the dedicated study, the patients were originally classified according to Child-Pugh Scores, there were 8 patients with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A), 8 patients with moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B) and 6 patients with severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh C). Results showed that the exposure to tivozanib in patients with mild hepatic impairment was similar to that in patients with normal hepatic function (GMR of AUC_{0-\infty}: 1.1). However, tivozanib was eliminated more slowly in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment, mean tivozanib exposure (AUC_{0-\infty}) increased 2.2-fold in patients with moderate (Child-Pugh B) hepatic impairment and 3.4-fold in patients with severe (Child-Pugh C) hepatic impairment.
L215: After re-classifying the patients in the dedicated study by NCI-ODWG criteria, there were 12 patients with mild hepatic impairment, 5 patients with moderate hepatic impairment, 0 patients with severe hepatic impairment. Mean tivozanib exposure (AUC_{0-\infty}) increased 1.79-fold in patients with mild hepatic impairment and 3.49-fold in patients with moderate hepatic impairment. The effect of hepatic impairment on the clearance of tivozanib was larger when patients were classified by NCI-ODWG criteria compared to Child-Pugh criteria in the dedicated study.
L216: Therefore, in the PopPK analysis, NCI-ODWG criteria was used to classify the patients, not only because that dosing recommendation based on NCI-ODWG criteria is more conservative, given
L217: the larger fold changes in AUC observed, but also that the majority of the patients were cancer patients. In the whole PopPK dataset, there were 570 patients with normal hepatic function, 83 patients with mild hepatic impairment, 10 patients with moderate hepatic impairment, 0 patients with severe hepatic impairment. The PopPK analysis indicated that mild hepatic impairment did not have an effect on the tivozanib CL/F, while patients with moderate hepatic impairment were found to have a 38.9% lower tivozanib CL/F compared to patients with normal hepatic function or mild hepatic impairment.
L218: The PopPK analysis, using all data with more patients for both mild hepatic impairment and moderate hepatic impairment, provided a reliable estimate for the hepatic impairment effect, given disease status was not found to be a significant covariate. Therefore, simulations were conducted based on the PK metrics generated from PopPK analysis. Simulation results indicated that 0.89 mg once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off in patients with moderate hepatic impairment is estimated to result in tivozanib exposure comparable to that in patients with normal hepatic function treated with 1.34 mg QD tivozanib for 3 weeks on and 1 week off. Therefore, based on the safety profile in the clinical trials, 0.89 mg once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off is recommended for patients with moderate hepatic impairment.
L219: ## Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L220: There is no clinically relevant food-drug interactions.
L221: Tivozanib is primarily metabolized by CYP3A4. Coadministration of tivozanib with ketoconazole did not have an effect on tivozanib exposure. Coadministration of tivozanib with rifampin resulted in decreased tivozanib exposure (AUC_{0-\infty}) by approximately 50%. Avoid coadministration of tivozanib with strong CYP3A4 inducers.
L222: ## Effect of Food on Tivozanib
L223: The C_{max} of tivozanib decreased by 23% (C_{max} GMR 0.77, 95%CI: 0.729-0.824) and the AUC_{0-\infty} did not change (AUC_{0-\infty} GMR 1.074, 95%CI: 1.028-1.123) when tivozanib was taken with a high-calorie, high-fat meal (approximately 500-600 fat calories, 250 carbohydrate calories and 150 protein calories) compared to those in the fasted state. Tivozanib is recommended to be administered with or without food.
L224: ## Effect of CYP3A Inhibitors on Tivozanib
L225: Coadministration of tivozanib 1.34 mg single dose with ketoconazole 400 mg once daily (a strong CYP3A inhibitor) did not have an effect on tivozanib exposure. C_{max} GMR 0.966, 95%CI: 0.905-1.031, AUC_{0-\infty} GMR 1.049, 95%CI: 1.005-1.095.
L226: ## Effect of CYP3A Inducers on Tivozanib
L227: Coadministration of tivozanib 1.34 mg single dose with rifampin 600 mg once daily (a strong CYP3A inducer) decreased tivozanib exposure (AUC_{0-\infty}) by approximately 50%. C_{max} GMR 1.084, 95%CI: 0.998-1.178, AUC_{0-\infty} GMR 0.480, 95%CI: 0.448-0.516.

L243: ## 7.2. Review Strategy
L244: Dr. Elaine Chang conducted the clinical review and Dr. Lijun Zhang conducted the statistical review.
L245: The clinical and statistical review included the following:
L246: - Literature review of advanced relapsed and refractory RCC.
- Research of the FDA database for the regulatory history of the tivozanib IND 075547 and review of the ODAC materials and transcript for the TIVO-1 review, and meeting minutes conducted during drug development.
- Review of applicant submitted CSR, protocol, protocol amendments, statistical analysis plan, and selected datasets for TIVO-3 and TIVO-1.

- Review of selected patient narratives for serious adverse events and deaths in TIVO-3.

L247: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical and Evaluation
L248: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L249: ## 8.1.1. TIVO-3 (AV-951-15-303)
L250: ## Trial Design
L251: Study TIVO-3 was an open-label, randomized, controlled, multi-national, parallel-arm study to compare treatment with tivozanib to treatment with sorafenib in adult patients with histologically or cytologically confirmed RCC with a clear cell component who failed 2 or 3 prior systemic regimens, including a TKI other than sorafenib or tivozanib.
L252: Patients were randomized 1:1 to receive either tivozanib or sorafenib using the Interactive Web Response System (IWRS). Randomization was stratified by the following factors:
L253: - IMDC prognostic category (favorable vs. intermediate vs. poor)
- Prior therapy (2 prior TKIs vs. a prior checkpoint inhibitor plus a TKI vs. a prior TKI plus any other systemic agent)
L254: Treatment began within 14 days of randomization. Tivozanib was administered 1.5 mg orally daily 3 weeks on/ 1 week off in each 4-week cycle and sorafenib 400 mg BID was administered orally continuously throughout each cycle. Note that 1.5 mg in the protocol refers to the dose of tivozanib hydrochloride, which is equivalent to the recommended dosage of 1.34 mg, referring to tivozanib free base.
L255: Eligible patients were required to have recurrent metastatic RCC with a clear cell component that had progressed after 2 or 3 prior systemic regimens, one of which included a VEGFR TKI other than sorafenib or tivozanib. Postoperative or adjuvant systemic therapy was not counted as a prior therapy unless a recurrence was detected within 6 months of completion of adjuvant treatment. Patients were also required to have measurable disease per RECIST criteria and ECOG performance status of 0 or 1.
L256: Key exclusion criteria included untreated brain metastases, significant cardiovascular disease (symptomatic left ventricular failure, severe angina or acute coronary syndrome within 6 months prior to trial enrollment, uncontrolled hypertension defined as systolic blood pressure [sBP] &gt;150 mmHg or diastolic blood pressure [dBP] &gt;100 mmHg on 2 or more anti-hypertensive medications, documented on 2 consecutive measurements taken at least 2 hours apart, or cardiac arrhythmias requiring anti-arrhythmic medications, except for well-controlled atrial fibrillation), grade \ge 2 bleeding within 6 months prior, non-healing wound, inflammatory bowel disease or risk/history of gastrointestinal perforation or fistula, thromboembolic event or bleeding disorder within 6 months prior, and patients with HIV infection or taking immune suppressive therapy for organ transplant.
L257: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L258: Patients were treated until a discontinuation criterion was met. Discontinuation criteria included documented disease progression of significant clinical deterioration indicative of progressive disease, unacceptable toxicity considered by the investigator to require removal of the patient from the trial, and treatment interruption for &gt; 4 weeks. Patients with stable disease or with objective response at the time of treatment discontinuation, who discontinued for reasons other than radiological evidence of progressive disease, continued to have periodic tumor assessments.
L259: Radiology assessments (CT scan or MRI) were performed every 8 weeks following Cycle 1, Day 1 to assess disease status until documented radiographic disease progression. Scans were evaluated by a blinded independent radiology review committee (BIRC).
L260: ## Study Endpoints
L261: The primary efficacy endpoint of the study was progression-free survival (PFS) per BIRC assessment based on RECIST v 1.1. PFS was defined as the time from randomization to the first date of radiological disease progression or death date (due to any reason), whichever occurred earlier.
L262: Secondary endpoints included overall survival (OS) and objective response rate (ORR).
L263: OS was defined as the time from randomization to the date of death due to any reason.
L264: ORR was defined as the proportion of patients with a best overall response of confirmed complete response or a confirmed partial response, according to the RECIST v1.1 criteria per BIRC assessment.
L265: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L266: ## Sample Size Consideration
L267: The study was designed to have 90% power to detect a hazard ratio (HR) of 0.67 for PFS at a two-sided significance level of 5%. Based on an assumed median PFS of 4 months in the sorafenib arm, this targeted treatment effect represents an increase in the median PFS of approximately 2 months (4 months vs. 6 months) in the tivozanib arm. With that, approximately 255 PFS events were required for the primary analysis. A total of 322 patients were planned to be randomized.
L268: Per the statistical analysis plan (version 5), the final OS analysis was to be conducted using a cutoff date of May 1st, 2020. Based on an estimate of approximately 4 OS events occurring per month, approximately 263 OS events would occur at that time. That would have 64% power to detect a hazard ratio of 0.75 at a two-sided significance level of 0.05 for OS.
L269: ## Analysis Populations
L270: The primary analysis population for all efficacy endpoints was the intent-to-treat (ITT) population. All randomized patients were to be included in the ITT population. Patients were to be grouped according to the treatment assigned at randomization.
L271: A sensitivity analysis population to be used for the primary efficacy endpoint was the per protocol (PP) population. This population was defined as all randomized patients who received at least 8 weeks of protocol treatment, had no major protocol deviations that would confound the effects of treatment, and met all eligibility criteria. Patients were to be analyzed as treated.
L272: The primary analysis population for safety was the safety analysis population. This population included all patients who received at least one dose of study drug, with treatment assignment designated according to actual study treatment received.
L273: ## Efficacy Analysis Methods
L274: The primary efficacy endpoint, PFS per BIRC, was to be summarized using the Kaplan-Meier method, and compared between the two treatment arms using a log-rank test stratified by the randomization stratification factors, i.e., IMDC risk category and prior therapy, as collected in the IWRS system. The treatment effect size was to be estimated by hazard ratio with a two-sided 95% confidence interval derived from a stratified Cox proportional hazards model with the same two stratification factors used in the stratified log-rank test.
L275: PFS was to be censored following the rules below in the primary analysis:
L276: - If there were no tumor assessments after randomization, PFS would be censored on the date of randomization.
- If a patient was known to be alive and progression-free, PFS would be censored on the day of the last tumor assessment.
- If a patient was given anti-tumor treatment (anti-cancer surgery/procedure, anti-cancer radiotherapy, and anti-cancer systemic therapy other than the study treatment) prior to disease progression or death, PFS would be censored on the date of the last tumor assessment prior to the start date of the anti-tumor treatment.
- If treatment follow-up ceased prior to objective disease progression, PFS would be censored on the date of the last tumor assessment.
- If a patient missed 2 or more consecutive post-baseline tumor assessments and the following assessment was disease progression, PFS would be censored on the date of the last tumor assessment.
L277: Secondary endpoints included OS, ORR, and duration of response (DOR). OS was to be analyzed in the same manner as for PFS; i.e., the two treatment arms were compared using the stratified log-rank test and the hazard ratio was estimated using the stratified Cox proportional hazards model. The number and proportion of patients achieving confirmed response (i.e., confirmed complete response or partial response) were to be summarized and compared between treatment arms using a Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) test with the same stratification factors used for the PFS analysis. Duration of response was to be summarized for patients who have achieved a confirmed response.
L278: ## Interim Analysis
L279: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L280: An interim analysis was to be conducted after 128 PFS events were observed for futility evaluation. A Lan-DeMets spending-type function with an O'Brien-Fleming stopping boundary was applied at this interim look. There was no interim efficacy analysis for PFS.
L281: Two interim analyses were planned for OS in the final version of the study SAP. The first interim analysis was to be conducted at the time of the primary PFS analysis and the 2nd interim analysis was to be conducted after all patients were followed for at least 2 year. The final analysis was to be conducted at a pre-specified cutoff date of May 1st, 2020. A Lan-DeMets spending-type function with an O'Brien-Fleming stopping boundary was used for OS interim analyses.
L282: ## **Multiplicity Adjustment**
L283: There was no multiplicity adjustment for secondary endpoints. PFS was the only primary endpoint and it was tested at 2-sided alpha of 0.05. The secondary endpoints, including overall survival, were intended to provide supportive evidence. All secondary endpoints were assessed without adjustment for multiple comparison.
L284: ## **Reviewer comments:**
L285: *The sample size calculation and analysis plan for the primary endpoint of PFS is acceptable.*
L286: *The analysis plan for OS has been revised several times when the study was ongoing. In the initial SAP, the study only planned one interim OS analysis at the time of PFS analysis. Due to the observed negative trend of OS at the time of the first interim analysis, the applicant revised the statistical analysis plan by considering the original planned final OS analysis as the second interim analysis and adding a new final OS analysis with longer follow-up. See the SAP amendment summary table for details. The change of analysis plan for OS could inflate the overall type I error rate if the study had intended to make any efficacy claims on OS. Per the statistical design, the study did not allocate any alpha for OS tests and was not adequately powered for OS analyses; therefore, no statistical inference could be made for OS. Results of all secondary endpoints are considered exploratory. Overall, the statistical analysis plan appears acceptable.*
L287: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L288: A summary of important changes implemented under each amendment are provided in the table below. The first distributed protocol was Version 2 (October 2015). A total of 161 patients were randomized during the time that Version 2 was in use, including 3 patients enrolled under France-specific protocol amendment 2.1 and 13 patients enrolled under UK-specific protocol amendment 2.2. Subsequently, a total of 189 patients were randomized during the time that Version 3 was in use, including 10 patients enrolled under France-specific protocol amendment 3.1 and 16 patients under UK-specific protocol amendment 3.2. No patients were enrolled under Version 4.

Performing the analysis now will reduce the power of the study by approximately 2% (99 vs 90%)." See SAP Version 3 below.

L298: ## Reviewer Comments
L299: The FDA noted that the OS analysis plan was revised after the first interim analysis results were available. In general, the FDA does not recommend revising analysis plan of major endpoints based on unblinded data if not pre-specified. However, given that in this study analyses of OS were to provide supportive evidence on efficacy and safety, and no efficacy claims were to be made on OS, and an OS analysis based on more mature data is expected to have a more accurate overall estimate of survival for the totality of the trial, the revision appeared acceptable in this application.
L300: In the SAP Amendment 6, the Applicant revised censoring rules for the primary PFS analysis by adding a censoring rule for missing 2 or more visits per FDA's recommendation. As this revision occurred after unblinding PFS data in 2018, the applicant conducted a sensitivity PFS analysis, in which this censoring rule was removed, to evaluate its impact and results are shown in Table 14. This approach appeared acceptable.
L301: ## 8.1.2. Study Results
L302: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L303: The sponsor made a formal statement that study was conducted in compliance with ICH E6 current Good Clinical Practice and the principles of the Declaration of Helsinki or the laws and regulations of the country in which the research was conducted, whichever afforded the greater protection to the individual. Informed consent was obtained from all patients prior to initiation of the study.
L304: ## Financial Disclosure
L305: There was no financial information (equity interest, significant payments of other sorts, other compensation by the sponsor and propriety) to disclose for the 802 clinical investigators who participated in TIVO-3, as reported on FDA Form 3454. See Section 19.2.
L306: OSI's inspections include review of financial disclosures to verify documentation and ensure processes are in place to update financial disclosures/interests as needed.
L307: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L308: The overall data quality and integrity are acceptable to the reviewers. The submitted datasets are generally consistent, and variables are clearly labeled and/or explained. Based on the submitted data and reports, the reviewers believe that the analyses and results are reliable for regulatory decision making.
L309: The electronic submission including Protocols, Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP), Clinical Study Reports (CSRs) and SAS transport datasets for the NDA submission are located in the network path: \CDSESUB1\evsprod\NDA212904\0001.
L310: These sources were utilized to perform the clinical and statistical review of this application.
L311: FDA inquired the sponsor about the impact of the communication failure between [REDACTED] (b) (4) software systems used to facilitate imaging measurements and import the measurement data into eCRFs. [REDACTED] (b) (4) had several discussions with FDA regarding this issue, [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L312: For TIVO-3, the number of measurement discrepancies identified was 6 of 21,811 measurements (discrepancy incident rate percentage: 0.03%). Per [REDACTED] (b) (4) and sponsor review, none of the discrepancies required action to be taken for the PFS analysis, and thus the communication failure had no impact on the PFS analysis in TIVO-3.
L313: ## Patient Disposition
L314: Between May 2016 and August 2017, 457 patients (456 unique patients) were screened for TIVO-3 and 351 patients (350 unique patients) were randomized. For the 106 patients who were screened but not enrolled, reasons for screen failure were not captured by the electronic data capture system and therefore not included in any dataset. However, because FDA inquired about these details via an Information Request, the sponsor provided a listing of screened patients, date of screening, and reason for screen failure. Reasons that patients were ineligible for enrollment are summarized below.
L315: - General conditions: 40/106 (38%)
- Lab abnormalities: 25/106 (24%)
- Systemic disorder: 16/106 (15%)
- Prior therapy: 10/106 (10%)
- ECOG PS &gt; 1: 9/106 (8%)
- No measurable disease per RECIST 1.1: 4/106 (4%)
- Inadequate recovery from prior surgery: 1/106 (1%)
L316: **Reviewer comment:** *Significant regional variability was apparent in reasons for screen failure.*
L317: For example, “general conditions” comprised reason for screen failure in half of patients, while in other regions, “systemic disorder” comprised reason for screen failure in half of patients, suggesting that these reasons were not well-defined and may be interchangeable depending on the interpretation of the investigator.
L318: Of the 350 patients enrolled, 175 patients were randomized to the tivozanib arm and 175 patients were randomized to the sorafenib arm. Eight (8) patients (2 in the tivozanib arm and 6 in the sorafenib arm) were randomized but not treated. Twenty patients (11%) in the tivozanib arm and 2 patients (1.1%) in the sorafenib arm remained on study drug treatment as of the August 15, 2019 data cutoff date. Patient disposition is shown in the figure.
L319: Figure 1. Patient Disposition (as of August 15, 2019), TIVO-3

L321: bPatients continued until progressive disease or discontinued for other reasons.
L322: As of the May 1, 2020 cutoff, 14 patients (13 in the tivozanib arm and 1 in the sorafenib arm) were receiving ongoing treatment with the study drug.

L346: **Reviewer Comment:** The FDA notes the relatively low overall use (~25%) of prior checkpoint inhibitors in the TIVO-3 patient population, which is likely a function of the fact that enrollment began in 2016, several years prior to initial FDA approval of nivolumab in the second-line setting, and certainly prior to the subsequent approval of several combination regimens involving checkpoint inhibitors in the first-line metastatic setting. However, the proportion of patients who had received prior checkpoint inhibitor is still higher than that of other recent randomized trials demonstrating clinical benefit of agents in refractory RCC, including axitinib,[17] cabozantinib,[18] and nivolumab.[19] The FDA performed an exploratory analysis of efficacy of tivozanib specifically in those patients who received prior checkpoint inhibitor therapy as those patients might be more similar to the patient receiving their 3rd line of treatment in the contemporary treatment setting, and these results are discussed in the efficacy results section below.

L354: ## Treatment compliance
L355: Treatment compliance was documented by (b) (4) capsule count beginning with Cycle 2. Compliance rates are reflected in Table 21 (Study Drug Exposure in TIVO-3).
L356: ## Concomitant medications
L357: Any medication/treatment considered necessary for the patient's welfare, which was not expected to interfere with the evaluation of the study drug, was given at the discretion of the investigator. Prohibited medication/treatment included anti-neoplastic therapy including radiotherapy, herbal preparations/supplements, strong CYP3A4 inducers or inhibitors, and full-dose oral anticoagulants (unless the patient was on a stable dose &gt;2 weeks prior to enrollment).
L358: Generally, the use of concomitant medications was similar between treatment arms. The most frequently (\ge 40\%) used concomitant medications in both treatment arms were drugs for peptic ulcer and gastroesophageal reflux disease (60%), opioids (51%), thyroid preparations (48%), other analgesics and antipyretics (48%), antithrombotic agents (43%), and selective calcium channel blocker anti-hypertensives (42%).
L359: ## Use of subsequent therapies
L360: Administration of subsequent anti-cancer therapy was summarized in Table 11. Overall, the percentage of patients receiving any subsequent anti-cancer therapy was similar between the two arms (49% vs. 51%). A higher proportion of patients (23%) who were randomized to receive sorafenib received subsequent checkpoint inhibitor compared to patients randomized to receive tivozanib (16%). As this was an open-label study, patients and investigators were aware of the study therapy received by the patient during the trial and subsequent decisions regarding therapy were presumably made with this knowledge.
