L366: *inhibitors have shown an OS advantage in several lines of therapy in advanced RCC.*
L367: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L368: Progression-free survival by BIRC was the primary efficacy endpoint of study TIVO-3. PFS was defined as the time from randomization to the first evidence of disease progression or death due to any cause. At the clinical cutoff date of 04 October 2018, 246 PFS events had occurred (123 in each arm), with a median follow-up time for PFS of approximately 18 months on the tivozanib arm and 16 months on the sorafenib arm.
L369: Table 12 presents the results of the primary analysis of PFS by BIRC assessment in the ITT population. A statistically significant improvement in PFS was observed in the tivozanib arm compared to the sorafenib arm with a hazard ratio estimate of 0.73 (95% CI: 0.56, 0.95). The estimate of median PFS was 5.59 months for the tivozanib arm and 3.88 months for the sorafenib arm. The Kaplan-Meier curves of PFS are shown in Figure 2.
L370: **Table 12. Progression-Free Survival Analysis per BIRC Assessment in the ITT Population, TIVO-3**

(N=175)

L373: **Reviewer Comment:** Figure 2 shows that separation of the two PFS curves started around the 2nd scheduled tumor assessment time point, and the proportional hazards assumption may not hold. A Schoenfeld residual test for non-proportional hazards had a p-value of 0.01 which suggested a departure from the proportional hazards assumption. With that, the review team also estimated PFS rates at 6 months and 12 months as supplementary: the estimates of 6-month PFS rates were 45% vs. 37%, and 12-month PFS rates were 28% vs. 11% for tivozanib arm and sorafenib arm, respectively.
L374: The FDA notes that although the absolute magnitude of improvement in the median PFS between arms was somewhat modest (&lt;2 months), the fact that this improvement was against an active control rather than a placebo or best supportive care arm was reassuring in terms of the efficacy benefit attributed to tivozanib.
L375: In the primary analysis of PFS, 52 patients in each arm have been censored. The reasons for censoring are summarized in Table 13.

L380: **Reviewer Comment:** It was noted that 31 patients (5 on the tivozanib arm vs. 26 on the sorafenib arm) were censored within 8-weeks post randomization (the first tumor assessment timepoint). Of the patients who were censored within the first 8 weeks after randomization, 0 patients in the tivozanib arm were censored due to receiving a new anti-cancer therapy without evidence of progression of disease, compared to 13 of 26 patients in the sorafenib arm. This suggests informative censoring in the sorafenib arm (e.g., patients or physicians may have believed that sorafenib was an inferior treatment). The impact of this imbalanced early censoring on efficacy results was not clear and thus evaluated in a sensitivity analysis (see the FDA's sensitivity analyses section).

L387: ## FDA's Analysis of Discordance between BIRC and INV Assessment of Disease Progression
L388: Per the study design, disease status was assessed by both BIRC and INV. The primary PFS analysis was based on the BIRC assessment, with the INV-based PFS as a supportive analysis. Discordance rates between BIRC and INV assessments are shown in Table 15. The discordance rate in terms of PFS status (event vs. censoring) was 19% in the tivozanib arm and 14% in the sorafenib arm. The discordance rates are similar between arms.
L389: **Table 15. Discordance and Concordance Rates of PFS Event Status between BIRC and INV Assessments, TIVO-3**

Censored	16 (9%)	35 (20%)	9 (5%)	37 (21%)

L392: ## FDA's sensitivity analyses of PFS
L393: - Death post study discontinuation: Three patients on the tivozanib arm died within 16 weeks post randomization, but the death occurred after study discontinuation and these 3 patients were not counted as PFS events in the primary PFS analysis. In this sensitivity analysis, the 3 patients were considered as events at the time of death, and the estimate of hazard ratio was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.58, 0.97).
- Early censoring: As shown in Table 16, more patients randomized to the sorafenib arm were censored within 8 weeks post randomization. To evaluate the impact of imbalance
L394: in the early censoring rates (3% vs. 15%), we performed a resampling analysis of PFS per BIRC assessment by imputing early censored patients (patients censored at a timepoint \le 8 weeks from randomization) using PFS data randomly sampled from patients censored or progressed after 8 weeks and repeated this procedure for 1000 times. The median of HR estimates based on the 1000 samples was 0.71 (95% CI: 0.55, 0.90) and the median of PFS median difference between arms was 1.7 months.
L395: **Table 16. Censoring and Event Distribution by Time, TIVO-3**
L396: Tivozanib
(n=175)	Sorafenib
(n=175)

L406: ## **Reviewer Comments:**
L407: *All of the subgroup analyses presented are considered exploratory or hypothesis-generating and no formal statistical inference may be drawn.*
L408: *No apparent outliers were observed in the subgroup analyses. In the subgroup of patients with poor IMDC risk level (n=62), the point estimate of hazard ratio is 1.12; however, the sample size is small and the wide confidence interval includes 1.*
L409: *The FDA notes that the patients who received prior checkpoint inhibitor therapy appear to derive consistent PFS benefit compared to those patients who did not receive prior checkpoint inhibitor therapy. While the number of patients who received prior checkpoint inhibitor is low, the results of this subgroup analysis may be considered supportive data with clinical relevance. Since the approvals of frontline combination regimens containing checkpoint inhibitor (2018-2020) by FDA, data to support use of specific second- or third-line agents in the new treatment paradigm is limited. The PFS-BIRC HR is similar to that of the overall population (0.67 95% CI, 0.39, 1.16)), reassuring the review team that tivozanib will have a role in the context of contemporary regimens.*
L410: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L411: The study statistical analysis plan did not pre-specify any multiplicity adjustment method for analyses of secondary endpoints. Although an O'Brien-Fleming boundary was used for alpha allocation at the OS interim and final analyses, no alpha was pre-specified to be allocated to OS after the primary endpoint PFS reached statistical significance. Therefore, analyses of all secondary endpoints are considered exploratory.
L412: ## Overall Survival
L413: Overall survival was one of the secondary endpoints. In the initial SAP, only one interim analysis was planned for overall survival to be conducted at the time of primary PFS analysis. After the primary PFS analysis was conducted, the SAP was amended several times, and the final version of SAP included two interim and one final analyses of OS. The first OS interim analysis was performed at the time of primary PFS analysis and results numerically favored sorafenib with a hazard ratio estimate of 1.12 (95% CI: 0.40, 1.51) based on 183 death events. The second interim analysis was conducted after all patients were on study for at least 2 years. At the second interim analysis (cutoff date: August 15, 2019), 227 death events occurred, and the hazard ratio estimate was 0.99 (95% CI: 0.76, 1.29) which numerically favored tivozanib. The final analysis was performed based on a data cutoff date of May 1st, 2020. As of that cutoff date, 251 death events have occurred. The results of the OS final analysis are summarized in Table 18 and the Kaplan-Meier plot is provided in Figure 3. The median follow-up time for overall survival was 38 months on the tivozanib arm and 40 months on the sorafenib arm as of the data cutoff date for the final analysis.

L418: ## **Reviewer Comments:**
L419: Per the applicant's communication with the FDA on 22 May 2020, the applicant found the actual date of death for one patient who was censored in the applicant's OS final analysis due to uncertainty of the death date at the time of the database lock. The review team used the actual death date of that patient in the final OS analysis as presented in Table 17 and Figure 3.
L420: As of the data cutoff date for the final OS analysis, 99 patients were censored, of whom 82 patients (46 tivozanib vs. 36 sorafenib) were still on study and under long-term follow-up for survival. All but one patient was censored within 2 months before the cutoff date for the final OS analysis. Therefore, the censoring distribution is acceptable.
L421: The review team noted that the point estimate of OS hazard ratio is close to 1 and the median estimate of the tivozanib arm is approximately 3 months shorter than that of the sorafenib arm. Therefore, possible detrimental effect of tivozanib on OS is a concern. Given that the median is only one timepoint on the survival curve and the difference at one timepoint may not reflect the overall curves, the review team also conducted a post-hoc exploratory analysis using restricted mean survival time (RMST) method to estimate the treatment effect. Using a cutoff time at month 44 for RMST estimation, the difference of RMST estimate between tivozanib and sorafenib is -0.4 months (95% CI: -3.7, 2.9). To explore RMST estimates at different cutoff timepoints, RMST difference between the two arms by cutoff timepoint is shown in Figure 2. Overall, the point estimates of RMST differences using various cutoffs are between -0.51 and 0.02 months (negative values favor sorafenib) and the confidence intervals are wide. Given that the Kaplan-Meier plot shows overlapping survival curves, a hazard ratio less than one in favor of
L422: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L423: the tivozanib arm, and a small difference of OS between the two arms observed using the RMST approach, it appears that tivozanib does not have an apparent detrimental effect on OS.
L424: The review team also performed additional sensitivity analyses on final overall survival. The unstratified OS analysis had a hazard ratio point estimate of 1.003 (95% CI: 0.78, 1.28) and the OS analysis stratified by stratification factors data collected on eCRFs had a hazard ratio point estimate of 0.97 (95% CI: 0.75, 1.25). Results are similar to the primary findings of OS.
L425: The FDA also notes that the overall survival analysis of tivozanib are vs. sorafenib, which represents an active therapeutic option in this setting. The phase 3 trial of sorafenib vs. placebo in patients with advanced RCC that led to FDA approval based on PFS later reported an OS HR estimate of 0.88 (95%CI: 0.74, 1.04), favoring sorafenib, despite crossover.[20] Thus, these results are interpreted through that perspective as well.

L435: FDA sent an Information Request to the sponsor for potential explanations for the OS HR of 1.54 among patients with baseline high sBP or dBP. The sponsor emphasized the possibility of small random sample variability. The sponsor also conducted additional analyses that suggested that
L436: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}

However, the use of subsequent therapy may be a possible confounding variable."* The FDA also notes the hazard ratio and point estimate for patients with prior CPI, which appeared similar to the overall population results. This is also reassuring and useful data as combination CPI is currently the standard initial therapy in the frontline. Furthermore, as noted in the Reviewer comment pertaining to Table 11, the number of patients who received a checkpoint inhibitor was higher in the sorafenib arm compared to the tivozanib arm, which would be expected to weight the efficacy results in favor of the sorafenib arm.

L458: ## 8.1.3 TIVO-1 (AV-951-09-301)
L459: The Applicant submitted supportive efficacy data of tivozanib in patients with KI-naïve advanced RCC based on data from a single clinical trial (TIVO-1). FDA conducted a full review of this data in NDA 204408. These results were also presented and discussed at ODAC in May 2013 (see Section 3.2 of this review).
L460: TIVO-1 was a multicenter trial that randomized 517 patients with advanced RCC without prior therapy or having received one prior systemic cytokine therapy. Patients were randomized to receive tivozanib versus sorafenib. The primary endpoint was PFS according to BIRC.
L461: The study met the primary endpoint: PFS HR 0.80 (95% CI: 0.64, 0.99), median difference of 2.8 months (11.9 vs. 9.1 months). The primary analysis used a stratified analysis based on number of prior treatments and number of metastatic sites. The reasons for censoring of patients were generally balanced between the two treatment arms.
L462: Overall survival was the first of the secondary endpoints. The final OS analysis used data
L463: collected through August 27, 2012 when all patients had been followed for a minimum of two years and when there were 219 OS events. This analysis demonstrated a decrement in OS of tivozanib as compared to sorafenib that was not statistically significant with a hazard ratio of 1.25 (95% CI: 0.95, 1.62; p=0.11). While the median difference in survival was two weeks, the review team stated that this estimate might not be reliable since few patients had died (118 patients in the tivozanib arm vs. 101 in the sorafenib arm) and the survival time estimate at the fortieth percentile was four months longer in the sorafenib arm.
L464: The review team at the time concluded that the OS analysis was greatly confounded by a significant design flaw, which was one-way crossover: patients who progressed on sorafenib could receive tivozanib through an extension study (AV-951-09-902), but patients who progressed on tivozanib were not given any similar opportunity to receive second-line therapy. Therefore, a differential use of subsequent targeted therapies in patients with disease progression between the two arms was thought to have biased the results for overall survival. One hundred and fifty-four (60%) patients randomized to sorafenib received targeted therapy, the majority having crossed over to tivozanib, as compared to only 31 (12%) patients from the tivozanib arm. While the assessment of PFS was a true comparison of the tivozanib to sorafenib, the analysis of OS was thought to essentially be a comparison of tivozanib against sequential targeted therapies. Thus, the overall survival results were considered not interpretable by FDA.
L465: The applicant presented several justifications to support their contention that the decrement in overall survival was due to differential treatment of patients following disease progression. This included the fact that the apparent toxicity of tivozanib was similar or less than sorafenib (similar rates of adverse events leading to death), the absolute overall survival for the tivozanib arm in this trial was similar to that of previous trials, and that there was not a decrement in overall survival in patients receiving tivozanib post progression. While many of the Applicant's arguments were considered plausible, they were all post hoc and were thought to not completely rule out the possibility that either sorafenib was a superior drug when it comes to overall survival or that there was some aspect of tivozanib that accelerates demise following disease progression.
L466: ## 8.1.4 Extension Study (AV-951-09-301)
L467: As noted in Section 8.1.3, patients who progressed on sorafenib could receive tivozanib through an extension study (AV-951-09-301) (see also Table 4). The extension study was a single-arm study with no primary efficacy endpoint (N=161). The Applicant provided investigator-assessed efficacy results: ORR of 18%, PFS of 11 months, OS of 21.6 months. These results represent exploratory efficacy data in patients who received second-line tivozanib. Since the extension study was never designed to assess efficacy and time-to-event endpoints are uninterpretable in single-arm trials, FDA does not consider these results demonstration of substantial evidence of effectiveness of tivozanib for the second-line indication.
L468: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L469: The sponsor submitted this data as supportive efficacy data as justification for the proposed indication statement of “patients with relapsed or refractory RCC.” As TIVO-3 did not enroll patients with RCC whose disease had progressed on only 1 prior systemic regimen, FDA revised the sponsor’s proposed indication for the approval from “patients with relapsed or refractory RCC” to “patients with relapsed or refractory RCC with disease progression on or after two or more prior lines of therapy.”
L470: ## 8.1.5 Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L471: In NDA 212904, efficacy was assessed based on data from a single clinical trial (TIVO-3) reviewed in Sections 8.1.1 and 8.1.2.

L482: ## 8.1.6 Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L483: The efficacy of tivozanib in patients with histologically or cytologically confirmed RCC that has progressed on 2 or 3 prior systemic regimens is supported by a single, multi-national, open-label randomized controlled clinical study, TIVO-3. The primary endpoint of BIRC assessed PFS was prolonged in patients randomized to receive tivozanib (median estimate 5.59 months)
L484: compared to sorafenib (median estimate 3.88 months). The FDA agrees that the observed benefit on PFS was both statistically significant (HR estimate 0.73, p-value 0.016) and clinically meaningful considering that the comparison is between tivozanib and an active control arm, sorafenib. Sorafenib's activity in this disease setting over placebo has been demonstrated in a randomized controlled trial.[4] Results of sensitivity analyses and subgroup analyses of BIRC-PFS supported the primary findings.
L485: Survival curves of the two arms are close to each other with a hazard ratio of 0.97 (95% CI: 0.75, 1.24). No apparent detrimental effect of tivozanib on survival was observed. Results of ORR are supportive, with PRs reported in 22% vs. 11% (tivozanib vs. sorafenib, respectively).
L486: Tivozanib had previously been evaluated in TIVO-1, which randomized patients with treatment-naïve advanced RCC to receive tivozanib versus sorafenib. Although TIVO-1 met its primary endpoint, a trend towards a detrimental effect on survival was observed with tivozanib. An ODAC vote on May 2, 2013 was 13:1 no:yes in response to the question "Has the Applicant demonstrated a favorable benefit to risk evaluation for the treatment of renal cell carcinoma in an adequate and well-controlled trial?". Voting members cited concerns that included the observed decrement in the HR estimate for OS and confounding factors in the trial design.
L487: Despite the negative ODAC vote for tivozanib in the KI-naïve RCC setting and the subsequent CR letter issued on June 6, 2013 for tivozanib based on review of TIVO-1, the review team felt that the totality of the TIVO-3 data warranted an approval in this case given the PFS advantage, supportive ORR, acceptable safety for the intended population, and the HR for OS that ultimately did not demonstrate a decrement in patients who were required to have had previous treatment with 2 prior lines of therapy. Although the Applicant had requested an indication that included all patients with advanced renal cell carcinoma based also on extension study AV-951-09-301, the FDA review team granted an indication that reflected only the TIVO-3 enrolled population, i.e. patients who progressed on two prior lines of therapy.
L488: In conclusion, the efficacy of tivozanib for patients with RCC that has progressed on 2 or 3 prior systemic regimens is supported by the result of the primary endpoint, secondary endpoints, subgroup analyses, and sensitivity analyses. The results supported by scientific knowledge about the effectiveness of other drugs in the same pharmacologic class, is adequate demonstration of substantial evidence of effectiveness of tivozanib, as stated by the *Demonstrating Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness for Human Drug and Biological Products* Draft Guidance for Industry (December 2019).[21] The submitted evidence meets the statutory evidentiary standard for regular approval of tivozanib for this indication.
L489: ## 8.2 Review of Safety
L490: ## 8.2.1 Safety Review Approach
L491: The focus of the safety review for this application is the 173 patients treated with tivozanib in the phase 3 TIVO-3 Study. The data cutoff for the original submission was August 15, 2019, and
L492: May 1, 2020, for the Safety Update. Supportive safety and tolerability data was obtained from 5 other trials as described in the table below, for a total of 1008 patients with advanced RCC who received tivozanib monotherapy at the proposed dose and schedule.
L493: ## 8.2.2 Review of the Safety Database
L494: ## Overall Exposure
L495: The safety of tivozanib has been evaluated in 15 Sponsor-initiated studies:
L496: - Tivozanib monotherapy in patients with RCC at the proposed dose and schedule (the 6 studies outlined in the table below)
- Tivozanib monotherapy for solid tumors (3 studies)
- Tivozanib in combination with other therapy for various solid tumors (6 studies)
L497: Data from patients with non-RCC tumors or who received combination therapy are not included in the clinical safety portion of this review.

L500: *All patients were treated for advanced RCC and received tivozanib 1.34 mg daily for 21 days on treatment followed by 7 days off treatment for a 28-day cycle. Each patient listed in this table is unique; although AV-951-09-902 is an extension study of TIVO-1, the patients who received tivozanib in the extension study received sorafenib rather than tivozanib in TIVO-1.

L502: Data from patients with non-RCC tumors or who received combination therapy are not included in the clinical safety portion of this review.
L503: In TIVO-3, 175 patients were randomized to tivozanib, and 173 patients received at least 1 dose; 176 patients were randomized to sorafenib, and 170 patients received at least 1 dose. Thus, the clinical safety portion of this review will focus on the safety population from TIVO-3: n=173 for tivozanib and n=170 for sorafenib.
L504: The median duration of exposure was longer in the tivozanib arm (197 days) than the sorafenib arm (140.5 days). The average number of cycles started (11.9 vs. 6.7) and median relative dose intensity (94.9 mg vs. 75.3 mg) were higher in the tivozanib arm compared with the sorafenib arm (Table B).

L507: 1Exposure = sum of the length of all the cycles.
L508: Note: A cycle of tivozanib was considered to be 3 weeks of the study drug followed by 1 week of no study drug administration. A cycle of sorafenib was considered to be 4 weeks of the study drug.
L509: 2Relative Dose Intensity is defined as 100% times the Actual Dose Intensity (ADI) divided by the Intended Dose Intensity (IDI). ADI for a patient is defined as the total dose received over all cycles divided by the sum of the lengths of all cycles in days where the minimum cycle length is 28 days. IDI is the total dose assigned over all cycles divided by the sum of the intended lengths of all cycles in days.
L510: **Reviewer Comment:** The higher relative dose intensity reported for tivozanib indicates that fewer patients required dose reductions. This appears to support the conclusion that tivozanib's tolerability is within range for this patient population.
L511: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
L512: A total of 343 patients out of 350 randomized patients received at least 1 dose of study drug on the TIVO-3 study. Two patients randomized to tivozanib and 5 patients randomized to sorafenib did not receive treatment. Baseline demographic and disease characteristics for the full analysis set were generally well balanced between treatment arms as seen in Tables 8 and 9.
L513: **Reviewer Comment:** Most patients had an ECOG PS of 0 in both treatment arms, and we note
L514: *that it is unlikely that the general pool of patients in their third line of therapy would be expected to have this relatively high performance status.*
L515: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L516: Overall exposure to tivozanib was adequate to support characterization of the safety profile in the proposed patient population.

L520: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L521: Adequate definitions for adverse events (AEs) were provided. All AEs were reported regardless of causality. AEs were coded using MedDRA Version 19.1 or later and graded using NCI CTCAE Version 4.03. TEAEs were defined as any AE with an onset date from the first dose of study treatment through 30 days after last dose of study treatment.

d Coagulation parameters included prothrombin time (PT) and/or international normalized ratio (INR), and partial thromboplastin time (PTT).

l For patients with stable disease or objective response at the time of treatment discontinuation, who discontinued for reasons other than radiological evidence of progressive disease, tumor assessments continued to be collected (every 2 months during the first year [beginning from the date of the last scan performed prior to treatment discontinuation], every 3 months during the second year, and every 6 months thereafter) until disease progression confirmed by independent radiology review or start of another therapy. A \pm 3 day visit window applied to tumor assessments for patients who discontinued study drug. Starting with version 5 of the protocol, all clinical response decisions were based on local radiological review.

m Two blood samples for pharmacokinetic (PK) analysis were collected at the following time points from all patients: Cycle 1, Day 1-4 hours post-dose and Cycle 1, Day 15- pre-dose (within 2 hours before dosing).

n After the 30-Day Follow-up visit, patients were contacted once every 3 months by the site to collect long-term survival data. Additional contacts may have been made to support key analyses, such as the analysis of overall survival at the time of the final PFS analysis, and the final analysis of overall survival. A \pm 7 day window applied for the scheduled long term follow-up contacts.

o A \pm 2 day window applied to all visit procedures except study drug administration.

p The duration of treatment for tivozanib is 21 of 28 days, and for sorafenib is 28 of 28 days.

q All patients had an EOT visit when they permanently discontinued any further study treatment. The EOT visit occurred up to 7 days after the last dose of study drug. Assessments performed within 3 days of the EOT visit did not need to be repeated and may have been documented as the EOT assessment (except for the response assessment which did not need to be repeated if performed within 30 days and showed progressive disease). Patients starting new anti-cancer therapy after the last dose of study drug completed the EOT Visit assessments in advance of starting the new anti-cancer therapy.

r Assessments obtained/ performed/ measured as clinically indicated.

L542: Narratives for the TEAEs, excluding "neoplasm progression," leading to death are provided below. None of the AEs leading to death were considered "related" to tivozanib by the investigator. Of the 12 TEAEs leading to death, half (6/12) occurred concurrently with documented disease progression which may have contributed to death.
L543: According to this reviewer's assessment, 4 of the remaining 6 cases may have been related to tivozanib. Patient [REDACTED](b) (6) pneumonia is unlikely to be related to the drug because 1-2 doses of a drug that is dosed daily continuously is typically not adequate exposure to cause an infection. Patient [REDACTED](b) (6) multiple organ dysfunction syndrome is also unlikely to be related to the drug because the direct cause of death was that he stopped drinking and eating, which occurred a few weeks after stopping tivozanib. Therefore, the 4 remaining patients whose deaths were due to an AE which did not occur in the context of disease progression may have been related to the study drug, as the narratives did not provide information to suggest otherwise.

hepatic failure	51-year-old woman with metastases to bones and liver and PMH of a "liver disorder since 1990" was found to have hyperammonemia with confusional state on day 132. death	Yes	74-year-old man with metastases to bone and liver, PMH of COPD since 2007; hypertrophic cardiomyopathy, HTN, and TIA 2016, had stable disease on tivozanib for the first 4 months (through day 127). (b) (6)
pneumonia	60-year-old woman with metastases to liver and lungs and PMH of DM, HTN, and hypothyroidism, had stable disease for the first 4 months (through day 114). On day 143, she had grade 2 dyspnea and fever, which led to discontinuation of tivozanib. Symptoms worsened with discontinuation. She died 3 weeks later.
Reviewer's assessment: since the patient had disease in lung and she worsened with discontinuation, the narrative suggests her symptoms were due to progression which may not have been shown radiographically.
L546: (b) (6)
pneumonia	Yes	61-year-old man with left temporal lobectomy and RT to brain and intrabronchial RT to lung had stable disease on tivozanib for the first 4 months (through day 114). Day 164: ischemic stroke which led to pneumonia 3 days later. He died 2 days later from respiratory failure and pneumonia. Reviewer's assessment: Each patient should have only one grade 5 AE. Therefore, this reviewer only counts pneumonia, because it is a more specific term and to avoid counting patients twice, when summarizing grade 5 AEs.
(b) (6)
cerebrovascular accident	62-year-old man without history of hypertension or other risk factors had well-controlled blood pressure throughout the first 5 months of tivozanib. He had progression of disease on day 113 and discontinued therapy on day 159. Day 162: CVA. Died 2 weeks later.
Reviewer's assessment: The narrative does not contain adequate information to discern the cause of CVA, but PD with hypercoagulability due to cancer is a potential cause.
(b) (6)
multiple organ dysfunction syndrome	51-year-old man with metastases to liver and pancreas was hospitalized on day 29 for hyperbilirubinemia (direct bilirubin 4.4 mg/dL), bone pain, vomiting, severe depression. Permanent discontinuation of study drug that day. He stopped drinking, eating, and taking medications a few weeks later which led to death on day 50.
Reviewer's assessment: The narrative is unclear but it appears that the patient may have been depressed, which may have been partly due to disease progression, which led to malnutrition.
(b) (6)
cardiopulmonary arrest	66-year-old man with history of CHF, COPD, and HTN was taking amiodarone, furosemide, enoxaparin for prophylaxis cardiac comorbidities. On day 74, he was hospitalized for lung infection due to PD. COPD exacerbation and delirium resulted. Tivozanib was interrupted; 20 days after last dose, he had cardio-respiratory arrest due to lung infection
(b) (6)
hepatobiliary disease	68-year-old man with metastases to liver had scans demonstrating PD (per both investigator and independent review) after 10 months of tivozanib. Tivozanib discontinued on day 310. Day 330: U/S showed stone in duct. Direct bili was 235 U/L (13.7mg/dL). Patient died the next day. Autopsy performed but results not provided.
Reviewer's assessment: The sequence of events suggests that progression of liver metastases was the cause of death, because scans unequivocally showed PD 6 weeks prior to
L547: (b) (6)	Yes	death. This is unlikely to be caused by tivozanib since it had been discontinued 20 days before the event.

L614: The denominator used to calculate the rate varied from 139 to 171 based on the number of patients with a baseline value and at least one post-treatment value. Thus, percentages among lab abnormalities may be the same while the number of patients with that lab abnormality are different (e.g., for phosphate, 165 patients were evaluable, so n=62 is 38% of the tivo arm, while for sodium, 171 patients were evaluable, so n=62 is 36% of the tivo arm).
L615: **Reviewer comment:** Laboratory abnormalities that differ by &gt;5% and that were higher in the tivozanib arm include: hemoglobin increased, creatinine increased, glucose increased, albumin decreased, sodium decreased, calcium increased, and activated partial thromboplastin time prolonged. However, for each of these laboratory parameters, the difference between the tivozanib and sorafenib arm incidence of grade 3-4 events was less than 3%, with some of them being higher in the sorafenib arm, suggesting that most of these differences between arms are not clinically meaningful.
L616: Both low and high hemoglobin were common. The upper quartile for hemoglobin values among all patients in TIVO-3 ranged from 15.6 to 21.2 mg/dL. The lower quartile ranged from 8.3 mg/dL to 13 mg/dL. Increased hemoglobin has been noted in association with VEGF inhibitors, which may be due to stimulation of erythropoietin production (https://ashpublications.org/blood/article/112/11/3453/59882/Increased-Hemoglobin-Associated-with-VEGF).
L617: ## Vital Signs
L618: Systolic blood pressure changes from baseline ranged from -14.0 mmHg to 8.2 mmHg (tivozanib arm) and -9.7 mmHg to 6.6 mmHg (sorafenib arm). Diastolic blood pressure changes from baseline ranged from -0.7 mmHg to 7.4 mmHg (tivozanib arm) and -3.0 mmHg to 7.0 mmHg (sorafenib arm).
L619: Abnormally high systolic blood pressure values (\ge 180 mmHg) were observed in 1.4% of patients in the tivozanib arm and 5.3% in the sorafenib arm (Table 14.3.4.13). Abnormally low systolic blood pressure (\le 80 mmHg) was observed in 1.2% of patients in the tivozanib arm and 1.0% of patients in the sorafenib arm.
L620: Abnormally high diastolic blood pressure values (\ge 105 mmHg) were observed in 6.3% of patients in the tivozanib arm and 8.0% of patients in the sorafenib arm. Abnormally low diastolic blood pressure (\le 50 mmHg) was observed in 5.6% (tivozanib arm) and 2.3% (sorafenib arm) of patients.
L621: Other than the effects on blood pressure, no clinically relevant trends in vital signs were evident during the study. See Section 8.2.5.1 for description and assessment of hypertension AEs.
L622: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L623: The ECG data showed no evidence of any clinically meaningful effect of treatment on heart rate or ECG morphology.
L624: ## QT
L625: The effect of tivozanib was evaluated in an open-label, multiple dose, single arm study in patients with advanced solid tumors (Study # AV-951-10-112). The highest dose evaluated was 1.5 mg (tivozanib hydrochloride) once daily for 21 days, which covers the therapeutic exposures of tivozanib. The data were analyzed using the exposure-response analysis as the primary analysis.
L626: No large (&gt;20 msec) QTc prolongation effect of tivozanib was detected. However, FDA is reluctant to draw conclusions of lack of an effect in an absence of a positive control or large exposure margin, or an integrated nonclinical safety assessment conduct according to best practices (ICH S7b Q&amp;A 1.1 and 1.2).
L627: ## Immunogenicity
L628: Not applicable.
L629: ## 8.2.5 Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L630: Discussion of the Submission-Specific Safety Issues will focus on the Warnings and Precautions of labeling. Issues are presented in the order listed in Section 5 of labeling, followed by other AEs of interest. All AEs listed in the section below are reported in other labeling for drugs targeting the VEGF pathway.
L631: ## 8.2.5.1 Hypertension and Hypertensive Crisis
L632: Hypertension was the most common grade 3-4 event in TIVO-3 in either treatment arm. In both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3, patients in the tivozanib arm had a higher incidence of hypertension compared to patients in the sorafenib arm. This was true for all-grade events as well as grade 3-4 events.

L639: 1Includes blood pressure increased, essential hypertension, hypertension, hypertensive crisis
L640: Hypertension occurred in 454/1008 (45%) of patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, with 22% of the events &gt; Grade 3. Median time to onset of hypertension was 2 weeks (range: 0 – 192 weeks).
L641: One patient in TIVO-1 inadvertently overdosed, taking 3 x 1.5 mg capsules daily for 2 days (9 mg total) of tivozanib and experienced a death due to hypertension. Information about this death due to tivozanib overdose is included in section 5.1 of the tivozanib label.
L642: Section 5.1, the Warning &amp; Precaution for Hypertension, of labeling recommends providers to “Control blood pressure prior to treatment with FOTIVDA. Monitor blood pressure after 2 weeks and at least monthly thereafter during treatment with FOTIVDA,” consistent with the TIVO-3 protocol. Additionally, Section 2.2 recommends dose interruption followed by reduction for grade 3 hypertension not controlled by anti-hypertensive therapy, and permanent discontinuation for grade 4 hypertension, which is consistent with the TIVO-3 protocol as well.
L643: ## 8.2.5.2 Cardiac Failure
L644: Cardiac failure occurred in 16/1008 (1.6%) of patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, with 10 (1.0%) events ≥ Grade 3, and 6 (0.6%) events were fatal. This made it the most common adverse event leading to death.
L645: Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 excluded patients with significant cardiovascular disease, including symptomatic heart failure, uncontrolled hypertension, history of severe angina or acute coronary syndrome within 6 months prior, or serious/uncontrolled arrhythmia. In both trials, cardiac failure occurred at comparably low rates in the tivozanib vs. sorafenib arms.

L649: ## 8.2.5.3 Cardiac Ischemia and Arterial Thromboembolic Events
L650: Cardiac ischemia occurred in 32/1008 (3.2%) of patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, with 15 (1.5%) events \ge Grade 3, and 4 (0.4%) events were fatal. Arterial thromboembolic events were reported in 20/1008 (2%) of patients, including 1 death due to ischemic stroke (0.1%).
L651: Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 excluded patients with history of thromboembolic event (including peripheral ischemia \ge grade 2), severe angina or acute coronary syndrome within 6 months prior to first dose of study drug. In TIVO-3 trial, these events occurred in a slightly higher lower proportion of patients in the tivozanib compared to sorafenib arm. In TIVO-1, the events occurred at comparable rates across arms.

L658: Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 excluded patients with history of thromboembolic event within 6 months prior to first dose of study drug. Overall, the rates of venous thromboembolic events were slightly higher in the tivozanib arm compared to sorafenib.

L662: ## 8.2.5.5 Hemorrhagic Events
L663: Hemorrhagic events occurred in 113/1008 (11%) of patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, with 19 (1.9%) events ≥ Grade 3, and 2 (0.2%) events were fatal.
L664: Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 excluded patients with history of Grade ≥ 2 bleeding (hematemesis, hematochezia, hemoptyxis, hematuria, etc.) within 6 months prior to first dose. In both trials, the proportion of patients with bleeding events was higher in the tivozanib arm than in the sorafenib arm.

L668: ## 8.2.5.6 Proteinuria
L669: Proteinuria occurred in 81/1008 (8%) of patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, with 20 (2%) Grade 3 events, and no Grade ≥ 4 events. There were no nephrotic syndrome events reported. Of the 81 patients with proteinuria, only 3 patients developed acute kidney injury (defined as acute kidney injury, anuria, azotemia, oliguria, or renal failure). Thus, there is lack of strong evidence that proteinuria is prognostic of deterioration in renal function.
L670: Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 monitored for proteinuria by requiring a urinalysis on day 1 of every cycle; if urinalysis dipstick showed protein 4+, a 24-hour urine collection was required. In TIVO-3, the proportion of patients with proteinuria was higher in the tivozanib arm than in the sorafenib arm. In TIVO-1, rates of proteinuria were comparable across treatment arms.

L676: ## 8.2.5.7 Thyroid Dysfunction
L677: Hypothyroidism occurred in 115/1008 (11%) of patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, with 3 (0.3%) Grade 3 events and no Grade \ge 4 events. Hyperthyroidism occurred in 1% of patients.
L678: Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 monitored for thyroid dysfunction by requiring thyroid function tests on day 1 of every cycle. In both trials, the proportion of patients with hypothyroidism events in the tivozanib arm compared to the sorafenib arm is more than double (24% vs. 11% and 10% vs. 4.3%). Additionally, the risk of hypothyroidism appears to increase by more than two-fold from the first-line setting to the third-line setting (from 10% to 24% among tivozanib-treated patients, and from 4% to 11% among sorafenib-treated patients). This suggests residual treatment effects from earlier lines of therapy.

L683: ## 8.2.5.8 Risk of Impaired Wound Healing
L684: Among the 1008 patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, no reports of impaired wound healing were reported. The sponsor has not conducted any formal studies to characterize the risk of impaired wound healing. However, the Office of Oncologic Diseases (OOD) has decided that all drugs claiming anti-VEGF inhibition activity should state a duration of time for wound healing to hold the drug prior to surgery and the time to resume the drug postoperatively in the Warnings &amp; Precautions section. The rationale is to provide consistent and proportional communication across drug products regarding adverse reactions related to VEGF pathway antagonism.
L685: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L686: After multi-disciplinary discussion, in the absence of clinical data, OOD's policy is to recommend withholding the drug for 5 half lives prior to scheduled surgery and for 2 weeks (and after clinical assessment of adequate wound healing) after surgery.
L687: The half-life of tivozanib is 111 hours. Therefore, the recommendation per labeling is to hold tivozanib for 24 days prior to major surgery.
L688: ## 8.2.5.9 Reversible Posterior Leukoencephalopathy Syndrome
L689: Reversible posterior leukoencephalopathy syndrome (RPLS, also known as PRES), a syndrome of subcortical vasogenic edema diagnosed by MRI, has been reported in association with drugs inhibiting the VEGF pathway.[22] It is unclear if this syndrome occurred in patients treated with tivozanib due to lack of diagnostic imaging with MRI. However, due to the potential risk of RPLS, section 6.1 of tivozanib labeling includes the sentence, "Clinically relevant adverse reactions in &lt; 15% of patients who received FOTIVDA included ... delirium."
L690: The sponsor reported all patients with "Non-Infectious Encephalopathy" as potential RPLS cases. These included Preferred Terms of delirium, encephalopathy, or toxic encephalopathy. The proportion of patients with non-infectious encephalopathy was similarly low in either treatment arm in both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 (1% in each arm in each trial).
L691: ## 8.2.5.10 Allergic Reactions to Tartrazine (FD&amp;C Yellow No.5)
L692: The 0.89 mg tivozanib (equivalent to 1.0 mg tivozanib hydrochloride) capsule contains FD&amp;C Yellow No.5 (tartrazine) as an imprint ink which may cause allergic-type reactions (including bronchial asthma) in certain susceptible patients. Although the overall incidence of FD&amp;C Yellow No.5 (tartrazine) sensitivity in the general population is low, it is frequently seen in patients who also have aspirin hypersensitivity.
L693: FDA requested the sponsor to conduct a risk assessment of "your perception of risk associated with tartrazine in tivozanib. This should include a review of your global safety database of allergic reactions to the tartrazine in tivozanib. You should also generally provide your assessment overall of this risk."
L694: The sponsor performed a comprehensive cumulative review of the global safety database representing patient exposure corresponding to 1,064 patient-years with commercial product from the date of European Union approval (August 24, 2017) through November 23, 2020 for any suspicion of allergic reactions to the tartrazine in tivozanib. Cumulatively through November 23, 2020, there were a total of 10 events, the symptoms of which, while non-specific, could be part of a hypersensitivity reaction. These events were reported as (1-2 events each) mouth swelling, chills, peripheral swelling, pruritis, rash, skin necrosis, and skin ulcer.
L695: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L696: There were no reports of anaphylaxis nor urticaria, and the events reported did not appear to suggest allergic reactions. The sponsor concluded that the risk of allergic reaction was low.
L697: FDA agrees that the risk of allergic reaction due to tartrazine sensitivity is low. Among the 1008 patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, 141 (14%) of patients potentially had an allergic reaction. AE terms related to rash comprised most of these "allergic reactions." Only 1 patient of 1008 had wheezing, which was Grade 1-2, and none had asthma. Similarly, in TIVO-3, 16% of patients had potential allergic reactions (dermatitis, dermatitis acneiform, drug eruption, erythema, erythema multiforme, photosensitivity reaction, pruritus, rash, rash erythematous, rash generalised, rash macular, rash maculo-papular, rash morbilliform, rash pruritic, skin exfoliation, swelling face, toxic skin eruption, urticaria), with two Grade 3 events and no Grade \ge 4 events. In contrast, 50% of patients in the sorafenib arm had allergic reactions (again, mostly rash), with 15% Grade 3. The only patient in TIVO-3 who had wheezing was in the sorafenib arm.
L698: See also Skin Reactions below.
L699: ## 8.2.5.11 Skin Reactions
L700: Skin reactions are not included in the Warnings and Precautions section of the tivozanib labeling, but are a known adverse effect of kinase inhibitors. The proportion of patients with skin reactions in TIVO-3 and TIVO-1 was noticeably lower in the tivozanib arms compared to the sorafenib arms. The difference was accentuated in the rate of grade 3-4 rash in TIVO-3 (15% vs. 5.1%), which may underscore the additive effects of kinase inhibitors used in sequence that have overlapping dermatologic toxicities.

L704: **Reviewer comment:** Skin reactions, especially palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia syndrome, is one of the chronic toxicities that can commonly affect patients on this class of therapy. We note
L705: *the overall lower rate of these reactions in tivozanib vs. sorafenib, and that this likely contributes to a more favorable toxicity profile in this regard.*
L706: ## 8.2.5.12 Aneurysm or Artery Dissection
L707: Aneurysm or Artery Dissection is not a Warning/Precaution in tivozanib labeling. Among the 1008 patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, aneurysm or artery dissection occurred in 3 patients. One of the events was grade 1-2 (aortic dilatation), one was grade 3 (aortic aneurysm, in a patient that was treated in TIVO-1 on the sorafenib arm and then crossed over to tivozanib) and one of the events was grade 5 (aortic aneurysm rupture). Since determination of causality was confounded in the grade 3 event, the amount of data to justify a separate Warning &amp; Precaution for Aneurysm or Artery Dissection was sparse. The review team decided to include the grade 5 event in the Hemorrhagic Events category.
L708: ## 8.2.5.13 Osteonecrosis
L709: Osteonecrosis is not a Warning/Precaution in tivozanib labeling. Among the 1008 patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, osteonecrosis occurred in 3 patients. All events were grade 1-2. However, osteonecrosis has been reported in association with drugs inhibiting the VEGF pathway, and is included in the Warnings &amp; Precautions of some drug labels. Therefore, section 6.1 of tivozanib labeling includes the sentence, "Clinically relevant adverse reactions in &lt; 15% of patients who received FOTIVDA included ... osteonecrosis..."
L710: ## 8.2.5.14 Gastrointestinal Fistula/Perforation
L711: Gastrointestinal fistula or perforation is not a Warning/Precaution in tivozanib labeling. These AEs have been reported following initiation of treatment with other VEGF-targeting drugs. Among the 1008 patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, gastrointestinal fistula occurred in 1 patient, which was grade 4. No patients had perforations. Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 excluded patients with active peptic ulcer disease, inflammatory bowel disease, ulcerative colitis, or other gastrointestinal condition with increased risk of perforation; history of abdominal fistula, gastrointestinal perforation, or intra-abdominal abscess within 4 weeks prior to first dose of study drug.
L712: ## 8.2.6 Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L713: The sponsor did not collect any patient-reported outcome (PRO) data in TIVO-3. However, the sponsor did collect PRO data in TIVO-1, which was a randomized trial with the same treatment regimens administered in TIVO-3, tivozanib versus sorafenib. FDA therefore requested the
L714: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L715: sponsor to provide PRO analyses in an effort to understand the patient experience with tivozanib in comparison to sorafenib.
L716: Specifically, FDA requested data from TIVO-1 summarizing results from the PRO questionnaire The Functional Assessment of Cancer Therapy - General (FACT-G) (FACT-G), including questionnaire completion rates and item-level analyses for the first 6 months for items assessing treatment side effects.
L717: The FDA Oncology Center of Excellence Patient-Focused Drug Development group reviewed the analyses and provided the following interpretation
L718: - Completion rate was quite good in both arms.
- No definitive conclusion could be drawn based on item GP1 results (lack of energy).
- GP5 results show that patients randomized to sorafenib reported worse overall side effect bother.

L737: ## 8.2.7.3 Race
L738: The low numbers of non-White patients enrolled in TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 precluded meaningful comparison of safety across races within trials (≤10 patients of each non-White race per arm per trial [and in TIVO-3 specifically, ≤2 patients of each non-White race]). In the overall safety population, the low numbers of non-White or non-Asian patients also precluded meaningful comparison of safety across races within patients who had received tivozanib (black, n=7; not reported/other/unknown, n=11). L745: **Reviewer comment:** Blacks were under-represented in the demographics of TIVO-3 (n=2, both in the sorafenib arm) vs the expected numbers based on the U.S. population. The incidence of kidney and renal pelvis cancers is highest among Black men (24.7 per 100,000), followed by White men (22.0 per 100,000).[23] As noted by Sims et al,[23] "In the United States, White populations have the lowest prevalence of RCC compared to other ethnic groups, while Black Americans suffer disproportionally from the adverse effects of RCC.. During the past two decades, the incidence rate of RCC has significantly declined among Caucasians but has rapidly increased among African Americans... A disparity in relative survival still persists between African Americans and Caucasians patients with kidney cancer even when controlling for treatment and other prognostic factors including stage, tumor size, and grade... The cause of this disparity in mortality is unknown." Possible factors include tumor genomic differences which may predict different responsiveness to therapy.[24]
L746: During the ODAC for TIVO-1, the underrepresentation of Black patients was discussed: "Another committee member questioned the limited exposure data in African American patients,
L747: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L748: *describing these patients as having unique needs in the areas of kidney cancer and hypertension."*
L749: *FDA has reminded sponsors that racial and ethnic minorities should be represented appropriately in clinical trials. FDA consistently advises sponsors to ensure that clinical trial sites include geographic locations with a higher concentration of racial and ethnic minorities to recruit a diverse study population, referring them to the Guidance for Industry: Clinical Trial Populations — Eligibility Criteria, Enrollment Practices, and Trial Designs at https://www.fda.gov/media/127712/download*
L750: ## 8.2.8 Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L751: The sponsor conducted a hepatic impairment study (AV-951-12-118) between in hepatically impaired patients and matched normal controls, both without cancer, using the Child-Pugh categories of hepatic impairment, between August 2012 and September 2013. After a 10-hour fast, 44 patients received 1 dose of 1.5 mg tivozanib hydrochloride. Pharmacokinetic results are described in Section 19.4 (OCP Appendices).
L752: Overall, 11/22 (50%) of patients with hepatic impairment and 5/22 (23%) of patients with normal hepatic function at baseline had TEAEs. The highest grade TEAEs were two grade 3 AEs of hypertension, one in a patient with moderate hepatic impairment and one in a patient with severe hepatic impairment. Hypertension was also the most common TEAE in the entire study, occurring in 25-37.5% (of 8 patients enrolled in each of the mild and moderate hepatic impairment groups, and 6 patients in the severe hepatic impairment group) of patients with mild, moderate, or severe hepatic impairment, and in 23% of patients with normal hepatic function.
L753: In conjunction with the pharmacokinetic results, these findings support the recommendation in labeling to adjust the dosage in patients with moderate hepatic impairment and avoid use in patients with severe hepatic impairment.

L761: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L762: No specific studies for overdose, drug abuse potential, withdrawal, or rebound are relevant for this application.
L763: Two patients in TIVO-3 overdosed on tivozanib due to patient error. The first patient took 32 days of tivozanib continuously without interruption, and suffered no adverse event related to the exposure. The second patient took two capsules on the same day in cycle 1, Day 1. The patient experienced a Grade 2 hypertension which lasted 5 days and then resumed tivozanib.
L764: One patient in TIVO-1 overdosed due to patient error, which is described in Section 8.2.5.1.
L765: ## 8.2.10 Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L766: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L767: No safety concerns have been identified through postmarket experience. FDA specifically requested the sponsor provide postmarket data regarding potential allergic reactions (described in Section 8.2.5.10) and safety data in patients with severe renal impairment.
L768: The sponsor identified no additional safety data through a search of postmarket experience in Europe from the time of EU approval (August 2017) through February 23, 2020.
L769: FDA also requested the sponsor to provide any additional postmarket safety data in patients with severe renal impairment. The sponsor stated that there were no additional postmarketing safety reports in this population. Although no patients with severe renal impairment (CrCl 15-29 mL/min) were enrolled in TIVO-3, FDA ultimately decided that no dosage modification is recommended for patients with mild to severe renal impairment. This was due to lack of change in PK/exposure in patients with severe renal impairment (see Clinical Pharmacology section), relatively low renal excretion of the drug, and lack of extraordinary toxicity in the 2 patients with borderline severe renal impairment in TIVO-1.
L770: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L771: TIVO-3 excluded patients with several baseline factors that might be expected to raise the likelihood of developing adverse events, including ECOG performance status \ge 2, serum creatinine &gt; 1.5 \times ULN, untreated brain metastases, cardiovascular disease, poorly-controlled hypertension, and history of recent thromboembolism. The use of tivozanib in these patients could constitute a safety concern in the postmarketing setting. However, risk mitigation for many of these comorbidities is addressed in labeling, and FDA has not identified a relevant
L772: scientific question that would be effectively answered using postmarket data. Thus, FDA has not required the sponsor to conduct additional postmarket safety studies.
L773: ## 8.2.11 Integrated Assessment of Safety
L774: FDA's integrated assessment of safety focused on the 1008 patients with RCC who received the proposed dose of tivozanib. The risks associated with tivozanib in this overall safety population are described in section 8.2.5, Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues. The safety profile in the overall safety population was generally similar to the safety profile in TIVO-3, with the exception of thyroid dysfunction. Patients who were not treated in one of the phase 3 trials (TIVO-1 or TIVO-3) likely were not monitored with thyroid function tests as closely and did not have as much exposure to study drug, which may explain the higher rates of thyroid dysfunction in tivozanib-treated patients in TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 compared to the other patients in the overall safety population.
L775: The safety profile of tivozanib was adequately assessed in the submitted database. At minimum, the toxicity was within range for this patient population, with adverse reactions manageable with dose reduction, temporary treatment discontinuation, and/or standard medical care. In fact, the lower rates of dose reduction, interruption, or permanent discontinuation, supported by the overall treatment bother results from analyses of TIVO-1 PRO results, demonstrate a likely overall improved tolerability over an accepted standard-of-care according to several clinically relevant metrics. Furthermore, the toxicity profile is consistent with other drugs affecting the VEGF pathway, which increases the familiarity of providers with risk management practices in this patient population.
L776: ## 8.3 Statistical Issues
L777: The FDA efficacy evaluation was based on data from the TIVO-3 study. Study TIVO-3 met its primary objective of PFS per BIRC assessment. The PFS results were consistent across sensitivity analyses, and no outliers were observed in the subgroup analyses. In addition, no apparent detrimental effect of tivozanib on survival was observed. There are no major statistical issues with this application.
L778: ## 8.4 Conclusions and Recommendations
L779: The review team recommends regular approval for tivozanib. Tivozanib is a kinase inhibitor indicated for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory advanced renal cell carcinoma (RCC) following two or more prior systemic therapies. Overall, the similar efficacy observed between tivozanib and sorafenib in TIVO-3, treatment with tivozanib at the recommended dose will provide an additional treatment option for patients with relapsed or refractory RCC following at least 2 prior lines of therapy. The benefit to patients and providers of an additional treatment alternative is augmented by the slightly different toxicity profile of
L780: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L781: tivozanib in comparison to other approved treatments for this patient population, including lower rates of diarrhea and skin reactions, potentially better overall tolerability, and the unmet need in third-line RCC.

## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

L807: ## 11.1 Patient Labeling
L808: A Patient Package Insert (PPI) is included with FOTIVDA labeling. Changes made to the proposed PPI were done for consistency with package insert. This included the order of the serious side effects (for consistency with the Warnings &amp; Precautions), adding instruction to “tell your healthcare provider about... medical conditions... including liver problems” in light of potential need for dose reduction, and adding specific symptoms associated with high blood pressure for which patients should monitor.
L809: See the FDA OPDP/DMPP Review filed under this NDA for complete details.
L810: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L811: The Division of Risk Management and review team agreed that a REMS is not necessary to ensure the benefits outweigh the risks of tivozanib. This decision was made after considering several factors: the risks associated with tivozanib are well-described in Sections 5 and 6 of labeling, the life-threatening nature of the disease of relapsed/refractory advanced RCC, the limited treatment options available, and the clinically meaningful efficacy results seen in TIVO-3.

L813: There are no postmarketing requirements or commitments for this application.

L854: ## 1 Executive Summary
L855: ## 1.1 Introduction
L856: NDA 204408 for tivozanib was submitted to the U.S. Food and Drug Administration as a New Drug Application (NDA) for the indication of the treatment of patients with advanced renal cell carcinoma (RCC). Tivozanib is a new molecular entity kinase inhibitor, which inhibits multiple kinases including vascular endothelial growth factor receptor (VEGFR)-1, VEGFR-2, and VEGFR-3. The recommended maximum human dose is 1.5 mg taken orally once daily, on a schedule of 3 weeks-on treatment followed by 1 week-off. Nonclinical pharmacology, pharmacokinetic and toxicology studies have been submitted to support the approval of tivozanib for the proposed indication.
L857: ## 1.2 Brief Discussion of Nonclinical Findings
L858: Tivozanib hydrochloride is an anticancer agent of the Vascular Endothelial Growth Factor Receptor (VEGFR) Tyrosine Kinase inhibitor class and is being developed for the treatment of advanced renal cell carcinoma. *In vitro* cellular kinase assays demonstrated that tivozanib is an inhibitor of VEGF receptors -1, -2, and -3 (IC_{50} 0.107, 0.081, and 0.122 ng/mL, respectively) and other kinases such as c-Kit and PDGFR\beta at higher IC_{50}'s (0.83 and 0.88 ng/mL, respectively). The IC_{50} of these kinases is well below the C_{min} value achieved in RCC patients (16 to 30.9 ng/mL) following repeat dosing at the recommended dose at 1.5 mg/day, suggesting continuous inhibition of these kinases throughout the treatment period.
L859: *In vivo* pharmacodynamic studies in mice and rats bearing human or murine xenograft tumors demonstrated that tivozanib inhibited tumor growth at a dose of 0.2 mg/kg in rats, via inhibition of angiogenesis and vascular permeability. This dose is associated with a plasma levels of tivozanib in rodents (C_{max} of 70 - 108 ng/mL; AUC of 1640 ng*hr/mL), which were within range of the C_{max} and AUC achieved in RCC patients following repeat dosing at the recommended dose of 1.5 mg/day (C_{max} was 67.5 - 94.3 ng/mL; AUC0-24 was 1180 -1641 ng*hr/mL).
L860: The selection of animal models to be used in toxicology and safety pharmacology studies (rats and monkeys) was based on PK and/or metabolic profiles of tivozanib. The oral route was utilized in nonclinical studies as this is the intended clinical route of exposure. The tivozanib toxicokinetic profile following repeat oral administration was approximately linear in rats and monkeys, with C_{max} and AUC increasing in an approximately dose proportional manner. No gender differences were shown in the rat or monkey. Interspecies differences in the rate of absorption (T_{max}) and elimination half-life (t_{1/2}) were reported. The elimination t_{1/2} in rats (9-45 hours) was greater than that reported in monkeys (8-12 hours), but was considerably shorter than that reported for humans (4.5-5.1 days). To account for these differences, the dosing regimen in the nonclinical toxicology studies was daily as compared to the clinical regimen (once daily, 3 weeks on and 1 week off). *In vitro* distribution studies conducted using animal
L861: or human plasma, it was shown that tivozanib bound to plasma proteins at &gt; 97.6%, and protein binding was predominantly to albumin, with low binding to \alpha-1-acid glycoprotein. Tivozanib mainly partitioned into the plasma/serum component of blood. *In vivo* metabolism studies demonstrated that the parent compound (tivozanib) was the major component in serum and feces of rats and monkeys. Tivozanib appeared to be metabolized mainly by oxidation, demethylation and glucuronidation. All prominent human metabolites (M1 through M24) were present in both rat and monkey, with the exception of M11, which was unique to human hepatocytes (minor &gt;0.5%), and M10, which was not observed in rat hepatocytes. M14 was present at &gt;10% and was designated as a major metabolite in human, monkey and rat. Comparison of the metabolic profile of tivozanib in liver microsomes showed that monkey most closely resembled the human, followed by the rat. These data provided support for species selection. Excretion studies in rats and monkeys showed that tivozanib was mainly excreted in feces in both species, and through the bile in rats.
L862: In safety pharmacology studies, no effects on respiration rate, tidal volume, neurobehavioral endpoints or physiological parameters were reported in rat studies. In single and repeat-dose cardiovascular studies in telemetered cynomolgus monkeys, a dose of 3 mg/kg (HED= 58 mg/day; 39-fold of the recommended human dose) resulted in increases in systolic, diastolic and mean arterial blood pressure. At doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day in the monkey (HED=19.5 mg/day; 13-fold of the recommended human dose), decreases in heart rate, cardiac output, renal arterial blood flow and cardiac arterial blood flow were reported. A decrease in vascular relaxation response and decrease in sensitivity to acetylcholine were also reported at doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day, which may have played a role in the reported hypertension response in monkeys.
L863: Using the core battery of genetic toxicology studies recommended in ICH guidances, tivozanib was not shown to be mutagenic, aneugenic or clastogenic; however, tivozanib produced changes in chromosomal number (polyploidy, hyperdiploidy) at \ge 2 \mug/mL (1.2-fold times the plasma level at recommended human dose).
L864: In a male and female fertility study in rats, doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day resulted in a decrease in animal mating (HED=0.97 mg/day; 0.65-fold of the recommended human dose) and doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day produced infertility (HED=2.9 mg/day; 1.95-fold of the recommended human dose). In an embryo-fetal developmental study in rats, tivozanib was administered during the period of organogenesis. Tivozanib at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED=0.29 mg/day; 0.19-fold of the recommended human dose) resulted in maternal toxicity, increases in early and late resorptions, an increase in fetal external malformations (body edema, short tail and kinked tail) and an increase in skeletal developmental delays. In an embryo-fetal developmental study in rabbits, tivozanib at 1 mg/kg/day (HED=19.5 mg/day; 13-fold of the recommended human dose) administered orally during the period of organogenesis resulted in fetal malformations including ventricular septal defects, constricted pulmonary artery vessels and distended aorta. No maternal toxicity was reported.
L865: In repeat-dose general toxicology studies, the toxicity profiles of tivozanib in the rat and monkey showed some overlap. The predominant toxicities in both species were associated with the following systems/organs: adrenal gland, gastrointestinal system (stomach, duodenum, and pancreas), kidney, bone (femur and tibia), liver, brain, pituitary gland, heart, incisors and reproductive system (ovaries and uterus). Clinical pathology changes and body weight decreases were also reported in both species.
L866: Oral administration of tivozanib to rats for 13 weeks resulted in mortalities and severe clinical observations (clinical signs typically associated with morbidity, malocclusion, missing teeth and white teeth) at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=0.97 mg/day; 0.65-fold of the recommended human dose). The observed dental toxicity correlated with microscopic findings in the incisors of the rats which may have contributed to the reported decreases in body weights and food consumption. The effect on incisors is unlikely to be relevant to targeted patient population (adults) due to the difference in incisor growth patterns in rats and humans. Clinical pathology changes were reported at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED=0.29; 0.19-fold the recommended human dose) and included changes in hematology (increases in RBC and WBC parameters), clinical chemistry (increases in iron metabolism, liver parameters, LDH/creatinine, triglycerides and renal parameters and decrease in protein parameters) and urine parameters (increases in protein, urobilinogen, bilirubin and hyaline casts). At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=0.97; 0.65-fold the recommended human dose) the target organs with significant toxicity were the adrenal glands, kidney, bone-femur, liver/ bile duct, brain, pituitary, incisors, pancreas and duodenum. Less pronounced (lower incidence and/or severity) microscopic findings were reported in the heart, reproductive organs, bone marrow, adrenal medulla and stomach. Partial recovery of most effects was reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, although renal effects were not recoverable at these doses. Toxicities at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day overlapped with those at the lower doses, but were more severe, occurred at a higher incidence and were for the most part not recoverable. NOAEL was considered to be 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED = 0.29 g/day; 0.2-fold of the recommended human dose) based on adverse microscopic findings.
L867: Oral administration of tivozanib in rats for 26 weeks produced non-adverse decreases in body weights, abnormal whitening of the teeth and changes in hematology/clinical chemistry at 0.08 mg/kg/day. No test article-related findings were reported by the end of the treatment or the recovery periods. The NOAEL was established to be the highest dose tested, 0.08 mg/kg/day (HED = 0.78g/day; 0.5-fold of the recommended human dose). The doses assessed in this study were low, and given the lack of appreciable toxicity, were considered insufficient to enable an adequate determination of dose-response relationships.
L868: Oral administration of tivozanib in monkeys for 13 weeks produced microscopic findings in the brain and adrenal cortex at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=1.9 mg/day; 1.3-fold of the recommended human dose). At doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day (HED=5.8 mg/day; 3.9-fold of the recommended human dose), adverse clinical signs, decrease in body weight and changes in hematology (increases in RBC, decreases and in WBC parameters), clinical and urine chemistry parameters (increases in CK, SELA-\alpha &amp; \beta, iron, phospholipids; decreases in ALP, SDH, total bile
L869: acid, amylase, UIBC, U LDH and U creatinine) were reported. The predominant test article-related microscopic findings evident at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day included bone (femur and tibia), gastric system (stomach, duodenum and pancreas), reproductive system (ovary and corpora lutea) and lymphoid organs (lymphoid depletion of thymus, lymph nodes and spleen. The majority of the findings were recoverable after a 2- or 5-week recovery period. The NOAEL was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (1.3-fold of the recommended human dose). In a 39-week oral toxicity study in monkeys, the highest dose tested 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=1.95 g/day; 1.3-fold of the recommended human dose) was well tolerated and considered to the established NOAEL. As with the 26-week rat study, the doses assessed in the 39-week monkey study were low, and given the lack of appreciable toxicity, were considered insufficient to enable an adequate determination of dose-response relationships.
L870: Ocular and cutaneous phototoxicity studies with tivozanib in rats did not show skin or ocular phototoxicity.
L871: ## 1.3 Recommendations
L872: ## 1.3.1 *Approvability*
L873: There are no non-clinical findings that would preclude the approval of tivozanib for the proposed indication.

L897: ## 2.5 Comments on Impurities/Degradants of Concern
L898: *Impurities above the ICH qualification threshold:*
L899: There are two impurities above the ICH qualification threshold: (b) (4) in Drug Substance and (b) (4) in Drug Product). (b) (4) has a genotoxic concern (Ames positive). The impurity levels as specified below are considered acceptable from the pharmacology/toxicology perspective.
L900: In addition, (b)(4) is also a degradant in both the drug substance and the drug product (NMT (b)(4)%). The degradant level is considered qualified based on the qualification criteria established by the ICH Q3B(R2) guideline.
L901: (b)(4)
L902: ## **Reviewer Comments:**
L903: - The maximum daily dose of tivozanib in the clinic is 1.5 mg.
- The specification limit for (b)(4) is NMT (b)(4)%, and for (b)(4) is NMT (b)(4)%. In the drug product these limits translate to a maximum daily dose of (b)(4) µg of (b)(4) and (b)(4) µg of (b)(4).
- Lot CDM-001 had the highest impurity levels of (b)(4) used for nonclinical studies. As such, it will be used to qualify these impurities.
- Lot CDM-001 was used in a GLP 13-Week gavage toxicity and toxicokinetic study with a 5-Week recovery period with KRN951 in cynomolgus monkeys (Study # 6691-159).
- In study # 6691-159, the dose of 1.0 mg/kg/day (HED 19.5 mg/day) was considered the highest non-severely toxic dose (HNSTD) in monkey.
L904: - At a dose of 1.0 mg/kg/day (HED 19.5 mg/day), the daily dose of impurities a monkey received was [REDACTED](b) (4). These levels of impurities per day in the nonclinical study are higher than the level of impurities per day that would be administered at the clinical dose.
L905: (b) (4)
L906: *Conclusion:* The daily impurity levels of [REDACTED](b) (4) (a genotoxic impurity) in the nonclinical studies exceed the daily impurity levels at the recommended clinical doses. Given that no severe toxicity was reported at these impurity levels in nonclinical studies, the impurities levels as specified are considered qualified by the nonclinical data. In addition, the maximum daily dose of the genotoxic impurity [REDACTED](b) (4) µg/day in patients receiving 1.5 mg/day of tivozanib. As levels of genotoxic impurities in anticancer pharmaceuticals may exceed the threshold of toxicologic concern based on lifetime daily exposures as per ICH S9 recommendations, the specification for [REDACTED](b) (4) in the tivozanib DP is acceptable based on the indicated patient population.
L907: ## 2.6 Proposed Clinical Population and Dosing Regimen
L908: The proposed tivozanib hydrochloride regimen for the treatment of patients with advanced RCC is 1.5 mg daily for 3 weeks (21 days) followed by a 1-week (7 days) rest period. When dose reduction is needed, the dose may be reduced to 1.0 mg daily for 3 weeks followed by a 1-week rest period.
L909: ## 2.7 Regulatory Background
L910: Original IND [REDACTED](b) (4) was submitted to the FDA on April 30, 2007.
