L927: ## 4 Pharmacology
L928: ## 4.1 Primary Pharmacology
L929: ## 4.1.1 Mechanism of action:
L930: Tivozanib hydrochloride (referred to as KRN951, AV-951 or tivozanib) is an anticancer agent of the vascular endothelial growth factor receptor (VEGFR) Tyrosine Kinase inhibitor class which is being developed for the treatment of advanced renal cell carcinoma (RCC). *In vitro* biochemical and cellular based kinase assays demonstrated that tivozanib inhibits phosphorylation of vascular endothelial growth factor receptors (VEGFR) -1, -2 and -3 (IC_{50} of 0.21, 0.16 and 0.24 nM, respectively), and inhibited C-kit and PDGFR \beta at higher concentrations (IC_{50} of 1.63 and 1.72 nM, respectively). Other kinases were inhibited at IC_{50} \ge 229 nmol/L. Tivozanib in *in vivo* tumor model studies was shown to inhibit angiogenesis and vascular permeability in tumor tissues at doses of 0.2 mg/kg/day and 5 mg/kg/day in rats and mice, respectively. Tivozanib administration resulted in anti-tumor effects, including tumor necrosis, growth inhibition, hypoxia and decreased angiogenesis.
L931: ## Overall summary:
L932: *In vitro* studies demonstrated that tivozanib is an inhibitor of VEGFRs and other kinases (e.g., c-Kit and PDGFR \beta).
L933: - Tivozanib was tested in an *in vitro* biochemical kinase assay using a panel of 26 recombinant kinases at concentrations ranging from 0.3 nM to 10 \muM. Tivozanib inhibited the phosphorylation of VEGF receptors -1, -2, and -3, with IC_{50} values of 30, 6, and 15 nM, respectively.
L934: - Tivozanib at concentrations ranging from 0.1 to 100 nM, was screened in an *in vitro* biochemical kinase assay using a panel of 129 recombinant kinases. At concentration of 100 nM, 39 kinases were inhibited by \ge 50\%. VEGFR-1, -2, and -3 were inhibited, with 18%, 0, and 3% activity remaining relative to control, respectively.
- In an *in vitro* cellular kinase assay performed in cultured human umbilical vein endothelial cells (HUVEC), tivozanib inhibited the activation of VEGF pathway by blocking the phosphorylation of VEGFR-1 (IC_{50} of 0.21 nmol/L) and VEGFR-2 (IC_{50} of 0.16 nmol/L). Tivozanib also inhibited VEGFR-3 autophosphorylation via VEGF with IC_{50} of 0.24 nmol/L in the murine 3T3 cell line. Activity against other kinases was also reported, as tivozanib inhibited c-Kit and PDGFR \beta with IC_{50}'s of 1.63 nmol/L and 1.72 nmol/L, respectively. In other cell types, kinases including FGFR-1, Flt3, c-Met, EGFR and IGF-I R were inhibited with IC_{50} values (\ge 229 nmol/L) \sim 1000\times higher than values reported for VEGFRs.
L935: In *in vivo* tumor xenograft model studies in mice and rats, it was demonstrated that tivozanib inhibited tumor growth of various tumor cell types via inhibition of angiogenesis and vascular permeability.
L936: - The inhibitory activity of tivozanib on tumor growth through VEGFR inhibition was supported by data from an *in vivo* study in which the inhibition of human tumor cell growth (A549, Calu-6, HT29, Colo205, SW620, DU145, LNCaP and PC-3) by tivozanib was evident, but at much higher concentrations (IC_{50} \ge 1 \mumol/L) relative to what was reported for VEGF-induced vascular endothelial cell growth (IC_{50} of 0.67 nmol/L). These data suggest that the antitumor activity of tivozanib on human tumors is a result of anti-angiogenic activity rather than direct growth suppression of cancer cells.
- Oral treatment with tivozanib at 5 and 20 mg/kg/day for 14 days resulted in tumor growth inhibition of a panel of 8 human cancer cell lines (lung, prostate, colon and renal) implanted in nude or ICR/SCID (renal model) mice. Tumor growth regression was reported in tivozanib-treated SCID mice xenografted with murine lung tumor cells and mice xenografted with human prostate carcinoma (DU145) tumor cells.
- Oral treatment for up to 14 consecutive days with tivozanib (0.2 and 1 mg/kg/day) in nude rat xenograft models bearing 15 different human cancer cell lines (pancreas, kidney, lung, glioma, prostate, colon, breast, ovary and liver) resulted in inhibition of tumors in a dose-dependent manner. At 0.2 mg/kg/day, tumor growth inhibition (TGI) was evident in 11 out 15 of the cell line models (TGI\ge 50\%); and at 1 mg/kg/day in 14 out 15 models (TGI \ge 86\%).
- In a human lung carcinoma (A549) nude rat model treated orally for 7 days at 1 mg/kg/day and immunostained with a vascular endothelial cell-specific marker, intratumor angiogenesis inhibition was evident by the reduction in intratumor vascular endothelial cells. In another study using a similar rat model, the quantity of Evans blue dye (identifies vasculature permeability) in tumor tissues was evaluated. It was demonstrated that tivozanib at 0.2 and 1 mg/kg/day, inhibited angiogenesis and vascular permeability.
- In a repeat-dose study (up to 5 days) in nude mice bearing murine breast HER2 xenograft tumors, tivozanib at 5 and 20 mg/kg/day was reported to result in a dose-dependent
L937: decrease in viable tumor area, reduction in total number of proliferating cells, decrease in vessel cross-section area, decrease in microvascular density and increase in tumor hypoxia as measured by HIF1-\alpha protein.
L938: - A combined treatment of tivozanib and rapamycin in mice bearing human BH504R3 or BH469 murine breast HER2 tumors in a xenograft tumor model resulted in an inhibition of tumor cell proliferation and with a decrease in viable tumor areas.
L939: These studies are presented below:
L940: Study #: 1172	
Title: IC50 profiling of 2 compounds using 26 protein kinases
GLP: No
Objectives: The IC50 profile of KRN-633 and KRN-951 (tivozanib), was determined using 26 protein kinases. IC50 values were measured by testing 10 concentrations of each compound. Only results from KRN-951 will be discussed in this review.
Methods:
Protein kinases: EGF-R, EPHB4, IGF1-R, VEGF-R1, VEGF-R3, BRK, FGF-R1, FGF-R3, ABL1, ERBB4, FLT3, EPHB2, INS-R, JAK2, MET, MUSK, PDGFR-alpha, PDGFR-beta, KIT, WEE1, SRC, TIE2, VEGF-R2, ERBB2, FGF-R4 and FAK.
Tivozanib was tested over a concentration range of 0.3 nM to 10 \muM.
Results:
Tivozanib inhibited the kinases activity in decreasing order: VEGFR-2 (IC50 = 6 nM), VEGFR-3 (IC50 = 15 nM), Eph-B2 (IC50 = 24 nM), VEGFR-1 (IC50 = 30 nM), PDGFR-\alpha (IC50 = 40 nM), PDGFR-\beta (IC50 = 49 nM), BRK (IC50 = 48 nM), Tie-2 (IC50 = 78 nM), and c-Kit (IC50 = 78 nM). Other kinases tested demonstrated IC50 values < 475 nM (Eph-B4).
Conclusion:
The Applicant concluded that tivozanib inhibited the activity of all 3 VEGF receptors at low concentrations with IC50 of 30, 6, and 15 nM for VEGFR-1 VEGFR-2, and VEGFR-3, respectively. Additionally, tivozanib inhibited several other kinases in these assays with IC50 values ranging from 24 to 618 nM. The provided data supported this conclusion.

Study #: KBC008, KBC009, and KBC010	
Three in vitro studies were designed to assess the selectivity of tivozanib for kinases. Tivozanib was screened against an expanded panel of 129 recombinant kinases in vitro at concentrations ranging from 0.1 to 100 nM to determine its inhibitory activity. At concentrations of 0.1 and 10 nM minimal inhibition was reported for any kinase. At a concentration of 10 nM, 12 kinases showed inhibition of \ge 50%. (JAK-3, c-Kit, CSF1R, BRK, and Abl-1). At a concentration of 100 nM, 39 kinases (including 4 mutations of mast/stem cell growth factor receptor [c-Kit] and 2 alleles of platelet-derived growth factor receptor-\alpha [PDGFR-\alpha]) were inhibited by \ge 50%. VEGFR -1,
L941: VEGFR-2, and VEGFR-3 were inhibited with 18%, 0, and 3% activity remaining relative to control, respectively.
Study #: 951-03, 951-09 and 951-41	
Study numbers 951-03, 951-09 and 951-41 (combined review)
Title: Assessment of VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 tyrosine kinase inhibiting effect of tivozanib.
GLP: No
Objectives: To evaluate the autophosphorylation inhibitory activity of tivozanib on vascular endothelial growth factor receptor-1 (VEGFR-1), receptor-2 (VEGFR-2) and receptor-3 (VEGFR-3).
Methods:
Lot Number: CDM-001
Cell used for VEGFR-1: Transfect Mouse Fibroblast (NIH3T3-Flt-1).
Cell used for VEGFR-2 and VEGFR-3: Human Umbilical Vein Endothelial Cells (HUVEC)
Tivozanib DMSO solution, was added to cell culture solution for final tivozanib concentrations of 0.01 nmol/L to 300 nmol/L.
After stimulation for 5 minutes with VEGF at a concentration of 50 ng/mL, cell extract was prepared and VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 were immunoprecipitated using anti-VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 antibodies, respectively. Immunoblotting was performed with anti-phosphorylated tyrosine antibodies to the immunoprecipitated fraction.
The levels of VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 tyrosine phosphorylation, and the IC50 of the inhibitory activity of tivozanib on VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 autophosphorylation in this system were determined.
Results:
Tivozanib inhibited tyrosine-phosphorylation of VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 in a concentration-dependent manner.
IC50 for tivozanib VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 autophosphorylation was determined to be 0.21 nmol/L, 0.16 nmol/L and 0.24 nmol/L, respectively.
95% confidence intervals were determined to be from 0.16 to 0.30 nmol/L, 0.13 to 0.20 nmol/L and 0.16 to 0.30 nmol/L, for VEGFR-1, -2 and -3, respectively.
Conclusion:
The Applicant concluded that tivozanib inhibited VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 autophosphorylation via VEGF with IC50 of 0.21 nmol/L, 0.16 nmol/L and 0.24 nmol/L, respectively. The provided data supported this conclusion.

Study #: 951-14, 951-33	
Study numbers 951-14, 951-33 and 951-27 (combined review)
L942: and 951-27	
Title: Evaluation of Inhibitory Activity of tivozanib hydrochloride on PDGFR \beta, FGFR-1 and Flt3 Receptor Tyrosine Kinase
GLP: No
Objectives: To evaluate the autophosphorylation inhibitory activity of tivozanib on platelet-derived growth factor receptor \beta (PDGFR \beta), fibroblast growth factor receptor-1 (FGFR-1) and fms-like tyrosine kinase 3 (Flt3) in order to investigate specificity of tivozanib on receptor tyrosine kinase.
Methods:
Lot Number: CDM-001
Cell Name: Human Fibroblasts.
Tivozanib DMSO solution or DMSO was added to cell culture solution for final concentrations of 0.01 nmol/L to 300 nmol/L.
After stimulation with PDGF-BB, bFGF and FL for 5-10 minutes at concentrations of 25-100 ng/mL, cell extract was prepared and PDGF-BB was immunoprecipitated using anti-PDGFR \beta, anti-FGFR-1 and anti-Flt3 antibodies. Immunoblotting was performed with anti-phosphorylated tyrosine antibodies to the immunoprecipitated fraction.
The level of tyrosine phosphorylation, and the IC_{50} of the inhibitory activity of tivozanib on PDGFR \beta, FGFR-1 and Flt3 autophosphorylation in this system were determined.
Results:
Tivozanib inhibited tyrosine-phosphorylation of human fibroblasts PDGFR \beta via PDGF-BB, human fibroblasts cells via bFGF, and Flt3 via FL in a concentration-dependent manner.
IC_{50} for tivozanib PDGFR \beta, FGFR-1 and Flt3 autophosphorylation was determined to be 1.72 nmol/L, 299 nmol/L and 422 nmol/L, respectively.
95% confidence interval was determined to be from 1.39 to 2.13 nmol/L, from 214 to 417 nmol/L and from 342 to 522 nmol/L for PDGFR \beta, FGFR-1 and Flt3, respectively.
Conclusion:
The Applicant concluded that tivozanib inhibited PDGFR \beta, FGFR-1 and Flt3 autophosphorylation via PDGF-BB, bFGF and FL with IC_{50} of 1.72 nmol/L, IC_{50} of 299 nmol/L and IC_{50} of 422 nmol/L, respectively. The provided data supported this conclusion.

Study #:
951-23,
951-34
and 951-36	
Study numbers 951-23, 951-34 and 951-36 (combined review).
Title: Evaluation of Inhibitory Activity of Tivozanib Hydrochloride on c-Met, EGFR and IGF-I R Receptor Tyrosine Kinase
GLP: No
L943: Objectives: To evaluate the autophosphorylation inhibitory activity of tivozanib on c-Met, EGFR and IGF-I R in order to investigate specificity of tivozanib on receptor tyrosine kinase.
Methods:
Lot Number: CDM-001
Cell Name: A431 (Human Squamous Carcinoma Cells) or HT29 (Human colorectal adenocarcinoma).
Tivozanib DMSO solution or DMSO was added to culture medium for final concentrations of 3 nmol/L to 100 µmol/L.
HGF, EGF and IGF-I was added and cells were stimulated for 5-10 minutes at a final concentration of 20-50 ng/mL.
After stimulation, cells were immunoprecipitated using anti-c-Met, anti-EGFR or anti-IGF-I R antibodies.
Immunoblotting was performed with anti-phosphorylated tyrosine antibodies to the immunoprecipitated fraction.
The level of tyrosine phosphorylation, and the IC_{50} of the inhibitory activity of tivozanib on c-Met, EGFR and IGF-I R autophosphorylation in this system were determined.
Results:
Study # 951-23:
Tivozanib inhibited tyrosine-phosphorylation of c-Met via HGF in a concentration-dependent manner. The IC_{50} for tivozanib c-Met autophosphorylation was determined to be 1.36 µmol/L and 95% confidence interval was determined to be from 0.73 to 2.54 µmol/L.
Study # 951-34 and 951-36:
Tivozanib did not reach 50% or greater inhibition of EGFR or IGF-I R autophosphorylation at the highest concentrations used in these studies. Precipitation in culture medium was reported with tivozanib at doses \ge 10 µmol/L. A sigmoid concentration-inhibition curve could not be generated, therefore, IC_{50} calculations were not established.
Conclusion:
The Applicant concluded:
Tivozanib inhibited c-Met autophosphorylation via HGF with IC_{50} of 1.36 µmol/L.
Tivozanib inhibitory activity of EGFR and of IGF-I R autophosphorylation was weak and therefore, IC_{50} were not calculated.
The provided data supported this conclusion.

Study #:
951-04	
Title: Evaluation of tivozanib hydrochloride Vascular Endothelial Cell Antiproliferative Activity
L944: GLP: No
Objectives: To evaluate the inhibitory activity of tivozanib on vascular endothelial cell proliferation via VEGF and basic fibroblast growth factor (bFGF)
Methods:
Lot Number: CDM-001
Cell Name: human umbilical vein endothelial cells
Tivozanib DMSO solution or DMSO was added to cell suspension for final concentrations of 0.01 to 300 nmol/L.
VEGF or bFGF was added to human umbilical vein endothelial cells and cells were stimulated for 72 hours. [^{3}H] thymidine was added and cultured for about 12 hours. Proliferation suppression rates were determined for each tivozanib concentration, and the IC_{50} on vascular endothelial cell proliferation due to VEGF or bFGF in this system was established.
Results:
Tivozanib resulted in inhibition of the vascular endothelial cell proliferation via VEGF at IC_{50} determined to be 0.67 nmol/L.
95% confidence interval was determined to be from 0.58 to 0.77 nmol/L.
IC_{50} for tivozanib bFGF was determined to be \ge 300 nmol/L.
Conclusion:
The Applicant concluded that, tivozanib inhibitory activity of the vascular endothelial cell proliferation via VEGF with IC_{50} of 0.67 nmol/L. The provided data supported this conclusion.

Study #:
951-28	
Title: Study of Cell Growth Inhibition of tivozanib hydrochloride on Human Tumor Cells (WST-1 Assay)
GLP: No
Objectives: To evaluate the inhibitory activity of tivozanib on 8 human tumor cell strains with an antitumor nude mouse panel.
Methods:
Lot Number: CDM-001
Cell Name: Human Lung Carcinoma A549 and Calu-6; Human Colon Carcinoma HT29, Colo205 and SW620, Human Prostate Carcinoma DU145, LNCaP and PC-3.
Tivozanib was added to the culture medium. Cell proliferation reagent WST-1 was added and cells were cultured for additional time. Optical density of each well was measured using a microplate spectrophotometer. Cell growth rate was established at each tivozanib concentration using optical density measurements.
L945: Results:
Tivozanib did not affect cell tumor growth at concentrations \le 300 nmol/L. However, concentrations \ge 1 \mumol/L resulted in inhibition of cell tumor growth.
The IC_{50} for the different cells were established to be:
A549	Calu-6	HT29	Colo205	SW620	DU145	LNCaP	PC-3
IC_{50} (\mumol/L)	\ge 10	\ge 10	\ge 3	\ge 3	\ge 3	\ge 10	\ge 1	\ge 3
Conclusion:
The Applicant concluded that tivozanib at concentrations \ge 1 \mumol/L resulted in inhibition of cell tumor growth at 8 human tumor cell strains. The data provided support this conclusion.

Study #:
951-16	
Title: Evaluation of inhibitory activity of tivozanib hydrochloride on c-Kit receptor Tyrosine Kinase
GLP: No
Objectives: To evaluate the autophosphorylation inhibitory activity of tivozanib on c-Kit receptor Tyrosine Kinase. c- Kit tyrosine kinase, which belongs to the PDGFR family, in which structure of the tyrosine kinase domain is analogous to that of VEGFR, was evaluated in order to investigate specificity of tivozanib on VEGFR tyrosine kinase.
Methods:
Lot Number: CDM-001
Cell Name: Ku812-F (Chronic myelogeneous leukemia)
Tivozanib DMSO solution or DMSO was added to cell culture solution for final concentrations of 0.01 to 300 nmol/L.
Recombinant Human Stem Cell Factor was added and cells were stimulated for 15 minutes at a final concentration of 100 ng/mL.
After stimulation, cell extract was prepared and c-Kit was immunoprecipitated using anti-c-Kit antibodies. Immunoblotting was performed with anti-phosphorylated tyrosine antibodies to the immunoprecipitated fraction. The level of c-Kit tyrosine phosphorylation accompanying stimulation was analyzed.
Results:
Tivozanib inhibited tyrosine-phosphorylation of c-Kit via SCF in a concentration-dependent manner and IC_{50} for tivozanib c-Kit autophosphorylation was determined to be 1.63 nmol/L and the 95% confidence interval was determined to be from 1.13 to 2.35 nmol/L.
Conclusion:
L946: The Applicant concluded that, tivozanib inhibited c-Kit autophosphorylation via SCF with IC_{50} of 1.63 nmol/L. The provided data supported this conclusion.

Study #:
AV951-08008	
Title: Tivozanib hydrochloride PD Analysis in Nude Mice Bearing Murine Breast HER2 Xenograft Tumors after Continuous Daily Oral Dosing of 5 mg/kg or 20 mg/kg
GLP: No
Objectives: To determine the effects of tivozanib on tumor microvasculature, cell proliferation, and hypoxia in the mouse breast HER2 tumor model.
Methods:
BH505 mouse breast HER2 tumor cells were implanted by subcutaneous injection to female nude mice (NCr nu/nu).
Treatment: orally administered once daily; control (0.5% methylcellulose), 5, and 20 mg/kg/day (Lot# CDM-003) for a continuous 2 to 5 day period. Twelve mice per group were used.
Endpoints evaluated: Tumors were measured at 48, 72, 96 and 120 hours post initiation of dosing. H&E staining was used for histopathologically evaluation; immunohistochemistry staining (CD31 stain) was used to determine the % of viable tissue area microvasculature density to determine vessel number per viable tumor area; and cell proliferation rate (KI67) and tumor hypoxia (HIF1-\alpha protein level) were determined using ELISA.
Results:
Doses \ge 5 mg/kg/day, resulted in a significant decrease in viable tumor area following 2 days of treatment. Viable tumor areas were significantly smaller at 20 mg/kg/day in comparison with 5 mg/kg/day, at all time points examined.
At 20 mg/kg/day, a decrease in cell proliferation rate was reported at 72 and 96 hours time points.
L948: At doses \ge 5 mg/kg/day, the total number of proliferating cells was significantly lower at all time points examined.
Doses \ge 5 mg/kg/day resulted in significant decreases in vessel cross-section area and microvascular density at all time points examined.
Doses \ge 5 mg/kg/day resulted in an increase in tumor hypoxia as measured by HIF1-\alpha protein. The highest levels were reported at the 120-hour time point.
Conclusion:
The Applicant concluded that tivozanib administered in a repeat-dose manner resulted in inhibition of tumor angiogenesis and tumor cell proliferation. The data provided supported Applicant's conclusion.

Study Design:

AV-951 (5 mg/kg/day)

Animals: male BALB/cAJcl-nu mice
Tumor cells were implanted by subcutaneous injection.
Treatment: Orally.
Dosing schedule:
L953: Group	Dose Amount	Dose Period	Mice
A	Media	10 weeks	8
B	KRN951 2 mg / kg	10 weeks	8
C	KRN951 20 mg / kg	10 weeks	8
D	KRN951 2 mg / kg	2 weeks dose + 2 weeks no dose x 3	8
E	KRN951 20 mg / kg	2 weeks dose + 2 weeks no dose x 3	8
F	KRN951 2 mg / kg	4 weeks dose + 2 weeks no dose x 2	8
G	KRN951 20 mg / kg	4 weeks dose + 2 weeks no dose x 2	8
(excerpted from Applicant's report)
Schedule #1 was a 2 and 20 mg/kg dose of KRN951 per day for 10 weeks.
Schedule #2 was a cycle of 2 and 20 mg/kg dose of KRN951 per day for two weeks, then no dosing for two weeks, repeated three times.
Schedule #3 was a cycle of 2 and 20 mg/kg dose of KRN951 per day for four weeks, then no dosing for two weeks, repeated two times.
Endpoints evaluated: Body weight, tumor volume, tumor weight, antitumor efficacy, tumor antiproliferative activity and tumor regression.

At 2 and 20 mg/kg/day, %TGI was greater than 150%.
Schedule #1: KRN951 at 2 and 20 mg/kg/day sustained regression of the tumor and on the day after the final dose, the relative tumor volume weight (RVx) was less than 1 (0.64 and 0.23).
Schedule # 2 and Schedule #3 (had no dosing periods): tumor growth was reported during the periods when dosing was stopped but TGI were reported when dosing was started again.
On day 70 the control group had a relative tumor volume weight (RVx) of 7.15, in contrast all other dose groups with the different schedules had values of less than 1 (RVx 0.23 ~ 0.99).
Conclusion:
The Applicant concludes that doses of 2 and 20 mg/kg/day after a repeat dosing (up to 10 weeks) resulted in TGI. Even after a dosing rest period (2 weeks), tumor inhibition was reported once dosing was initiated for the next treatment cycle. The data suggest that no tolerance for KRN951 was developed. The data provided supported this conclusion.

L956: ## 4.2 Safety Pharmacology
L957: ## Overall Summary:
L958: A total of five safety pharmacology studies were performed, one *in vitro* hERG study, two *in vivo* cardiovascular studies in monkeys, one *in vivo* respiratory study in rats, and one central nervous system study in rats.
L959: The *in vitro* hERG Tail Current study for tivozanib hydrochloride was designed with a single concentration. As such, a concentration response curve was not established (per ICHS7B), and the study provided no information regarding IC50. Accordingly, this was not considered a valid
L960: study. An *in vivo* telemetry study was performed in male cynomolgus monkeys that received a single oral dose of KN951 (0.015, 0.3 or 3 mg/kg). A dose of 3 mg/kg resulted in increases in systolic, diastolic and mean arterial blood pressure 90 minutes post-dose. There were no test article-related effects on heart rates, RR, PR, QT, QTcF, QTcQ intervals and the QRS duration at any dose level. In an *in vivo* repeat-dose telemetry study (1 or 3 mg/kg/day for 28 or 14 days, respectively) in male cynomolgus monkeys, increases in systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure were evident at 3 mg/kg/day. As such the single- and repeat-dose telemetry study findings in monkeys at 3 mg/kg were consistent. At doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day in the monkey, decreases in heart rate, cardiac output, renal arterial blood flow and cardiac arterial blood flow were reported. A decrease in vascular relaxation response and decrease in sensitivity to acetylcholine were also reported at doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day (HED 19 mg/day).
L961: An *in vivo* respiration study in male rats receiving a single oral dose of tivozanib (0.3, 3, 30, and 400 mg/kg) showed no effects on respiration rate, tidal volume, neurobehavioral endpoints or physiological parameters (Irwin test).
L962: - The *in vivo* cardiovascular system (monkey) NOAEL: 0.3 mg/kg/day (HED 5.8 mg/day).
- The *in vivo* central nervous system (rat) NOAEL: 0.3 mg/kg/day (HED 2.9 mg/day).
- The *in vivo* respiratory system (rat) NOAEL: 0.3 mg/kg/day (HED 2.9 mg/day).
L963: Title: Effect of KRN951 on hERG Tail Current Recorded from Stably Transfected HEK293 Cells
Conducting Laboratory:
Date of study initiation: 07 July, 2003
GLP compliance: Yes
QA statement: Yes
Drug and lot #: KRN951, CDM-001

Study #:
DRZZ1020
Objective and Methods
The objective of this study was to examine the effect of the KRN951 (3 \mug/mL) on the hERG tail current recorded from stably transfected HEK293 cells with hERG cDNA.
KRN951 was tested at a single concentration. The concentration tested (3 \mug/mL) was the maximum soluble concentration of KRN951 as determined in solubility testing. E-4031 (100 nM) was used as a positive control and tested in two cells of the vehicle and test article. The effect of KRN951 (3 \mug/mL) was investigated in 5-6 cells and the vehicle (0.1% DMSO) was investigated in 5-6 cells.
L964: ResultsAnalysis of formulation: The final concentration was determined to be 27.3% of the intended concentration (3 µg/mL). Therefore, a concentration of 0.82 µg/mL was the actual concentration of the solution applied to cells in the recording chamber.
The positive control at 100 nM inhibited hERG tail current by 88.8% (expected outcome).
KRN951 inhibited hERG tail current by 28% versus 18.9% at the control group. The difference was not statistically significant.
Conclusion
KRN951 at a concentration of 0.82 µg/mL, produced no inhibition of hERG tail current.
Reviewer comment:Only one concentration was tested (at maximum soluble concentration). Ascending concentrations should have been tested to establish a concentration response curve (per ICHS7B, section 3.1.2).
The final concentration used was 27.3% (0.82 µg/mL) of the nominal concentration and the concentration did not produce inhibition of hERG tail current.
Given the lack of concentration response curve this study provides no information regarding IC50 and therefore the study was not considered to be valid.

L987: Study title: Cardiovascular Effects of KRN951 in Conscious, Telemetered Cynomolgus Monkeys

L988: ## **Key Findings:**
L989: - At 3 mg/kg, significant increases in systolic (increases of 20%, 16% and 5% at 90, 120 and 240 minutes post-dose, respectively), diastolic (increase of 21% at 90 minutes post-dose) and mean arterial blood pressure (increases of 19% and 18% at 90 and 120 minutes post-dose, respectively) were reported.

L990: ## **Methods:**
L991: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}

L996: Dose volume: 1 mL/kg
L997: Formulation/Vehicle: Vehicle, 0.5% methylcellulose
L998: Sex/Species/Strain: Male cynomolgus monkeys
L999: Age: 3 years and 11 months to 5 years and 1 month old
L1000: Device used: Animals were implanted with telemetry devices
L1001: Endpoints evaluated:

- Data was collected at least 30 minutes before dosing and ended approximately 6 hours post-dose.
- Measurements of systolic blood pressure (SBP), diastolic blood pressure (DBP), heart rate (HR) and the lead II ECG variables (measuring PR interval, RR interval, QRS duration and QT interval) were acquired continuously before and after dosing.
- Mean arterial blood pressure (MAP), QTcF and QTcQ.
- A visual inspection of all of the acquired ECG waveforms was performed for all monkeys following administration of vehicle and 3.0 mg/kg KRN951.
L1002: Deviation from study protocol: None of the deviations affected the integrity or interpretability of the study.
L1003: ## Results:
L1004: - No clinical observations were reported at any dose level.
- At 3 mg/kg, significant increases in systolic (increases of 20%, 16% and 5% at 90, 120 and 240 minutes post-dose, respectively), diastolic (increase of 21% at 90 minutes post-dose) and mean arterial blood pressure (increases of 19% and 18% at 90 and 120 minutes post-dose, respectively) were reported.
- There were no test article-related effects reported at any dose level in heart rates, RR, PR, QT, QTcF, QTcQ intervals and the QRS duration.
L1005: ## Conclusion:
L1006: Male cynomolgus monkeys received a single oral dose of KN951 (0.015, 0.3 or 3 mg/kg). A single dose of 3 mg/kg resulted in increases in systolic, diastolic and mean arterial blood pressure. There were no test article-related effects on heart rates, RR, PR, QT, QTcF, QTcQ intervals and the QRS duration at any dose level.
L1007: Study title: KRN951 Multiple Oral Dosing in Crab Eating Monkeys (Cynomolgus monkey)
Toxicity Study
Study no.:	4655
Objective:	To examine the effect of a 2 or 4 week multiple oral dosing regimen of KRN951 on the heart beat pulse rate and blood flow of crab eating monkeys.
Sponsor:	Pharmaceutical Development Laboratories
Kirin Brewery Co., Ltd.
Maebashi-shi, Gunma 528-0853
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
GLP compliance and QA statement:	Yes according to Japan GLPs
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	KRN951, CDM-001 and 98.8%
L1008: ## **Key Findings:**
L1009: - At 3 mg/kg/day (HED 58 mg/day), there were test article-related increases in systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure by up to 28%, 22% and 28% during the dosing period (days 1 to 14), respectively.
- At 1 mg/kg/day, there were no test article-related effects on systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure.
- At 1 and 3 mg/kg/day, there were test article-related decreases in heart rate by up to 44% and 35%, respectively, during the dosing period (animals at 3 and 1 mg/kg/day were dosed for 14 or 28 days, respectively).
- Decreases of 34% and 38% in cardiac output were reported at 1 and 3 mg/kg/day, respectively. A decrease in renal arterial blood flow was evident at 1 (-67%) and 3 mg/kg/day (-65%) and a decrease in cardiac arterial blood flow was evident at 1 (-59%) and 3 mg/kg/day (-46%).
- Test article-related decrease in vascular relaxation response was observed at doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day (HED 19 mg/day). The EC50 (-log M) value for ACh was lower at 1 mg/kg/day (7.12) and 3 mg/kg/day (6.84) than the control group (7.19).

L1021: ## Endpoints evaluated:
L1022: - Daily observations (twice/day) during the dosing period.
- Body weights were measured on Day 1 and weekly thereafter.
- Systolic blood pressure, diastolic blood pressure, average blood pressure and heart beat rate were measured 3 days prior to first dose and daily during the dosing period. In the 1 mg/kg/day group, measurements were taken starting on day 14.
- Blood pressure was measured for 1.5 hours prior to dose administration and for 4.5 hours post-dose.
- Blood sample was taken once every three or four days to assess blood plasma for renin activation and Creatine phosphokinase (CPK).
- At the end of dosing cardiac output, carotid artery blood flow volume and renal artery blood flow volume were measured.
- Vascular endothelial function was performed by measuring an endothelium-dependent, nitric oxide-mediated vasodilatory response (ACh-induced) from extract samples taken from the right carotid artery. In one experiment, Acetylcholine (ACh) was added to the samples at concentrations ranging from 10^{-9}M to 10^{-6}M, followed by Papaverine at 10^{-4}M, to achieve a maximum relaxation effect (baseline). In a second experiment, a nitric oxide inhibitor, L-NAME, 3 \times 10^{-5} M was added to samples treated with ACh at concentrations ranging from 10^{-9}M to 10^{-6}M, and Papaverine at 10^{-4}M. The relaxation percentage at each ACh concentration was calculated against the baseline (100%)

protocol: Ophthalmological examinations was performed.

L1025: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L1026: - At 3 mg/kg/day, there were test article-related increases in systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure by up to 28%, 22% and 28% during the dosing period (day 1 to 14), respectively. This data is in agreement with the findings reported in prior cardiovascular study (#DRZZ1019), in which produced increases in systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure by up to 20%, 21% and 19%, respectively.

L1030: ## **Reviewer comment:**
L1031: *In contrast to the Applicant's conclusion stating the minor rise after dosing in systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure was not of a large enough magnitude to be considered a test article-related, this reviewer concludes that the observed increases in systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure at 3 mg/kg/day were of sufficient magnitude to be considered a safety signal and test article-related. In addition, these results are in agreement with the findings reported in a prior cardiovascular study (#DRZZ1019), in which the test article produced increases in systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure by up to 20%, 21% and 19%, respectively. The Applicant based their conclusion on standards established in the protocol, where an effect was considered only if there was a rise of more than 15 mm Hg at the end of dosing (3 day period), as compared to the average value of the 3 day period before the start of dosing. But using the control group for comparison the magnitudes are large enough to be test article-related.*
L1032: - At 1 and 3 mg/kg/day, there were test article-related decreases in heart rate by up to 44% and 35%, respectively, during the dosing period.

L1045: ## Reviewer's comments:
L1046: - In contrast to the Applicant's conclusion stating that there were no effects on cardiac output observed at any dose level, this reviewer concludes that a decrease in cardiac output was evident at 1 and 3 mg/kg/day. The decrease in cardiac output agrees with the decrease in heart rate reported at both dose levels.

The decrease at 3 mg/kg/day was statistically significant, while the decrease at 1 mg/kg/day was not. However, the decrease at 1 mg/kg/day was considered to be biologically significant due to the magnitude of effect (-67%).
L1048: - In contrast to the Applicant's conclusion stating that no effects were observed in cardiac arterial blood flow, this reviewer concluded that a decrease in cardiac
L1049: *arterial blood flow was evident at 1 and 3 mg/kg/day. The decrease at 1 mg/kg/day was statistically significant, while the decrease at 3 mg/kg/day was not. The decrease at 3 mg/kg/day was considered to be biologically significant due to the magnitude of effect (-46%).*
L1050: - There were no test article-related effects on rennin and CPK values.
- There was a test article-related decrease in the ACh-induced vascular relaxation response (% relaxation rate) at 3 mg/kg/day KRN951, at all ACh concentrations assessed. This effect was suppressed when L-NAME was added to samples, thus indicating that the potential mechanism of blood pressure change may be due to an inhibitory effect on vascular endothelial-derived nitric oxide production, causing a rise in peripheral vascular resistance and blood pressure
L1051: ## Conclusion:
L1052: Cynomolgus monkey dosed with KRN951 at 1 or 3 mg/kg/day for 28 or 14 days, respectively. At 3 mg/kg/day (HED 58 mg/day), increases in systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure were evident. At doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day, decreases in heart rate, cardiac output, renal arterial blood flow and in cardiac arterial blood flow were evident. A decrease in vascular relaxation response was evident at doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day (HED 19 mg/day).
L1053: ## 5 Pharmacokinetics/ADME/Toxicokinetics
L1054: ## Overall Summary:
L1055: Tivozanib TK profile following oral administration was approximately linear in mice, rats and monkeys, with C_{max} and AUC increasing in an approximately dose proportional manner. No significant gender differences in exposure were reported. The rate of absorption was moderate and unaffected by dose level or duration of treatment in rats and monkeys, while, the rate of absorption (T_{max}) and elimination half-life (t_{1/2}) were different between species.
L1056: In *in vitro* distribution studies conducted using animal or human plasma, it was shown that the test article bound to plasma proteins at &gt; 97.6%, and protein binding was predominantly to albumin, with low binding to \alpha-1-acid glycoprotein. KRN951 mainly partitioned into the plasma/serum component of blood.
L1057: Quantitative data on the tissue distribution of test article-related radioactivity in whole body of rats showed that the distribution of radioactivity was maximal at 4 hours post-dose. The highest radioactivity tissue concentrations were measured in the preputial gland, harderian gland, white fat and the liver. The concentrations in both the ovary and uterus were similar to the blood levels, and those in testes were significantly less than in the blood. The majority of the radiation was eliminated from tissues at 24 hours post-dose, and by 72 hours post-dose the radiation levels were below the limit of quantification. In pigmented rats, melanin binding to
L1058: the choroid layer of the eye was apparent and levels decreased steadily over 120 hours post-dose.
L1059: *In vitro* and *in vivo* metabolism studies with KRN951 were performed and showed that in serum, the parent compound (KRN951) was the predominant circulating component (91% and 78% of the total radioactivity exposure in rat and monkey serum, respectively) with the metabolites M4 (mono-oxidation)/M5 (N-oxide of tivozanib) co-eluting and M63 in rats only. The parent compound was not detected in the urine, and metabolites detected in urine represented less than 0.5% of the administered radioactive dose. In feces, the parent compound was the main component and other metabolites detected represented less than 5% of the administered radioactive dose. *In vitro* metabolism studies showed that KRN951 metabolism was low in all species studied, as was evident by the low hepatic clearance, and that KRN951 appeared to be metabolized mainly by oxidation and demethylation. All prominent human metabolites (M1 through M24) were present in both rat and monkey with the exception of M11 (glucuronide), which was unique to human hepatocytes (minor &gt;0.5%) and M10 which was not observed with rat hepatocytes. M14 was present at &gt;10% of total radioactivity and was designated as a major metabolite in human, monkey and rat. The metabolic profile of the monkey was the closest to human, followed by the rat. Comparison of the metabolic profile of tivozanib in human, monkey, dog and rat liver microsomes showed that monkey is the closest to human followed by the rat and dog is the most different. CYP3A4 and CYP1A1 are the major human hepatic CYP isoform involved in the metabolism of tivozanib.
L1060: In rats and monkeys the main excretion route was through the feces, and to a smaller extent (2-3%) through urine.
L1061: Overall the data supported the use of rats and monkey in nonclinical studies, as these species would be expected to provide adequate coverage for potential human metabolites and associated toxicities.

As such, the data from these studies cannot be used to support this application, and were not reviewed.*

L1074: ## **Excretion studies:**
L1075: - In rats administered an oral or IV dose of [14C] KRN951, the majority of the test article was excreted in feces (91%) by 72 hours post-dose, suggesting that the main excretion
L1076: route of the test article was through the feces. A limited amount of radioactivity (&lt; 2%) was excreted into the urine (not the parent compound).
L1077: - Data generated from the bile duct-cannulated rats, suggested that [^{14}\text{C}]KRN951 is also excreted through the bile (up to 37% of the total radioactivity). Another non-biliary route of excretion of tivozanib and/or its metabolites from the systemic circulation was the gastrointestinal tract which showed approximately 40% of the radioactivity.
- In monkeys, after an oral dose of [^{14}\text{C}] KRN951, the radioactivity excreted in the urine and feces, were 3.40% and 27.4%, respectively, within 24 hours postdose. These data confirm that the main excretion route of the test article, through the feces, was indeed similar in the rat and monkey.
L1078: ## ***Distribution studies:***
L1079: - In an *in vitro* study conducted using animal or human plasma, it was shown that the test article bound to plasma proteins of the rat (99.3%), human (99.7%) and monkey (97.6%), with no apparent gender differences.
- In human plasma, the protein binding was mainly to albumin, with low binding to \alpha-1-acid glycoprotein.
- *In vitro* studies conducted with human blood indicated that KRN951 was mainly partitioned into the plasma/serum component of blood. Similarly, the reported data from the rat study suggested limited association of KRN951 within red blood cells.
- Quantitative data on the tissue distribution of test article-related radioactivity in whole body of male and female albino and male pigmented rats showed that the distribution of radioactivity was similar between the genders and strains. The extent of the tissue penetrance was low, with tissue/blood ratios of less than 1 in the majority of tissues. Maximal tissue concentrations were observed at 4 hours post-dose. The highest radioactivity tissue concentrations were measured in the preputial gland, harderian gland, white fat and liver. The lowest radiation levels were in the spinal cord and brain. The concentrations in both the ovary and uterus were similar to the blood levels, and those in testes were significantly less than in the blood. In albino rats, the majority of the radiation was eliminated from tissues at 24 hours post-dose, and by 72 hours post-dose the radiation levels were below the limit of quantification. In pigmented rats, melanin binding to the choroid layer of the eye was apparent and levels decreased steadily over 120 hours post-dose.
L1080: ## ***Metabolism studies:***
L1081: *In vitro* and *in vivo* metabolism studies with KRN951 were performed:
L1082: - The *in vivo* metabolic profiles were assessed in rat and monkey serum, urine and feces. In serum, the parent compound (KRN951) was the predominant circulating component (91% and 78% of the total radioactivity exposure in rat and monkey serum, respectively) with the metabolites M4 (mono-oxidation)/M5 (*N*-oxide of tivozanib) co-eluting and M63 in rats only. The parent compound was not detected in the urine, and none of the metabolites detected in urine represented an average of greater than 0.5% of the
L1083: administered radioactive dose. In feces, the parent compound was the main component and each of the other metabolites detected, M37/57 co-eluting, M42, and M7, represented an average of less than 1% and &lt; 4.99% in monkey and rat, respectively. KRN951 was not detected in rat bile, but the metabolites were evident at approximately 0.388% of the administered radioactive dose.
L1084: - *In vitro* metabolism studies showed that KRN951 metabolism was low in all species studied (rats, dogs, cynomolgus monkeys, and humans), as was evident by the low hepatic clearance (&lt; 25% of blood flow). KRN951 appeared to be metabolized by oxidation, demethylation, glucuronidation, sulfation, or a combination of these pathways.
- Twenty-two different metabolites (M1-M24) were detected in rat, monkey and human hepatocyte incubations with AV-951 with the exception of M11 (glucuronide), which was unique to human hepatocytes (minor &gt;0.5%) and M10, which was not observed with rat hepatocytes. M14 (desmethyl glucuronide) was present at &gt;10% of total radioactivity and was designated as a major metabolite in human, monkey and rat.
- Comparison of the metabolic profile of KRN951 in human, primate, dog and rat liver microsomes showed that the metabolic profile of the monkey was the closest to human, followed by the rat and dog. Metabolites formed by rat, dog and monkey were the same as those produced in human liver microsomes with the exception of one metabolite in monkey which eluted earlier than any of the four metabolites observed in the other species. Overall the data supported the use of rats and monkey in nonclinical studies, as these species would provide adequate coverage for potential human metabolites and potential associated toxicities.
- CYP3A4 and CYP1A1 were the major human hepatic CYP isoform involved in the metabolism of KRN951.
- Substantial levels of ^{14}\text{C}-KRN951 desmethyl glucuronide metabolites were formed by UGT isoenzymes. Metabolite M14, a desmethyl glucuronide metabolite, was the major metabolite formed by six UGT enzymes. Other observed glucuronide metabolites were M10 and M25. Since the major metabolite (M14) was formed by six different UGT isoenzymes, inhibition of any one of these enzymes is not likely to cause substantial drug-drug interactions.
- KRN951 inhibited glucuronidation of UGT substrates 4-MU and p-NP to a small extent. Therefore, tivozanib is not a potent inhibitor of UGT activities.
L1085: ## 5.1 PK/ADME
L1086: ## Studies Conducted:
L1087: Study #:
DRZZ1028	Title: The Disposition of Total Radioactivity in the Rat Following Single Oral and Single Intravenous Administration of [^{14}\text{C}] KRN951
GLP: Yes
Objectives: To evaluate the excretion of total radioactivity into urine, feces and expired air following a single oral and a single IV administration of [^{14}\text{C}]-tivozanib
L1088: hydrochloride to male rats. In addition, this study evaluated the PK and distribution of tivozanib hydrochloride to red blood cells.
L1089: ## **Methods:**
L1090: Male (treated orally and intravenously (IV)) and female (treated IV) rats were treated based on the schedule listed in the table (below) and were housed in individual glass metabolism cages. Urine, feces and expired air were collected:
L1091: **Study design:** This study was conducted in five phases.
L1092: Phase	Investigation	Route	Number of animals male (female)	Target Dose Level (mg/kg)	Sample collection
1	Excretion	Oral	3	1	Urinea, fecesa
Expired air: 48, 96 hr
2	Excretion	Intravenous	4	0.5	Urinea, fecesa
Expired air: 48, 96 hr
3	PK	Oral	3(3)	1	Blood (plasma)b
4	PK	Intravenous	3(3)	0.5	Blood (plasma)b
5	Blood cell transfer	Oral	3	1	Blood
(4, 24, 72, 120 hr post-dose)
L1093: a: Collected at selected time points through 168 hours post-dose
L1094: b: Collected at selected time points through 120 hours post-dose
L1095: (excerpted from Applicant's report)
L1096: ## **Results:**
L1097: - The concentrations of radioactivity in the expired air trap were below the limit of quantification at all collection time points.
- Following oral administration in male rats, the majority of the test article was excreted in feces and urine by 72 hours post-dose as was evident by the total radioactivity recovered (87.8% and 1.70%, respectively).
- IV administration of the test article to male and female rats resulted in similar outcome to the oral administration, suggesting that the main excretion route of the test article is fecal excretion.
- At the end of the collection period (168 hours postdose) for male and female rats, a mean total of 95.7% and 93.9% of the dose was recovered in urine, feces, cage wash, expired air traps and carcass following oral and IV administration, respectively.
- Limited association of radioactivity with red blood cells was suggested based on the data that following oral dose of the test article, the transfer of total radioactivity to red blood cells was evident at the later time points when radioactivity concentrations were low and the blood cell transfer ratios increased with time.
- Following oral dose the mean Cmax and AUC were 1764 ng equiv/mL and 2279 ng equiv/mL and 29243 and 38732 ng equiv.h/mL for male and female rats, respectively.
- Following IV administration, mean AUCinf values were 26019 and 29933 ng equiv.h/mL in male and female rats, respectively.
L1098: Conclusion:
In rats administered an oral or IV dose of [^{14}\text{C}] KRN951, the majority of test article was excreted in feces by 72 hours post-dose, indicating that the main excretion route of the test article is fecal excretion.

Study #:
Pk0403	
Title: Pharmacokinetic Study of AV951 – Biliary Excretion of [^{14}\text{C}] AV951 in Rats
GLP: No
Objectives:
To evaluate the amount of [^{14}\text{C}]KRN951 eliminated through the bile, after a single intravenous administration, by measuring the amount of radioactivity in the urine, feces and bile.
Methods:
Bile duct cannulated male rats were dosed intravenously once at 0.5 mg/kg with [^{14}\text{C}]KRN951 and radioactivity in urine and feces and bile were measured up to 72 hours post-dose.
Results:
At seventy two hours post-dose, greater than 80% of the radiation was excreted, with the percentage of excretion into the feces (via gastrointestinal tract) and bile being about the same at 39.8% and 36.6%, respectively and less than 5% excreted in urine.
Conclusion:
The data suggested that in rats, [^{14}\text{C}]KRN951 was excreted through the bile and feces.

Study #:
8255110	
Title: Metabolism of ^{14}\text{C}-Tivozanib Following Oral Administration to Rats
GLP: Yes
Objectives: The purpose of this study was to profile and identify the metabolites of ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib following oral administration to rats.
Methods:
Eleven male bile duct-intact Sprague Dawley rats and two bile duct-cannulated (BDC) male rats were used.
Study design:
L1099: Group	Number of Male Animals	Dose Route	Target Dose Level (mg/kg)	Target Dose Volume (mL/kg)	Samples Collected
1	9	Oral	5	10	Blood
2	2	Oral	5	10	Urine and Feces
3	2 BDC	Oral	5	10	Urine, Feces, and Bile
BDC Bile duct-cannulated.
Notes: The radioactive dose was approximately 200 \mu\text{Ci/kg}.
(excerpted from Applicant's report)
Rats were dosed orally with 5 mg/kg (\sim200 \mu\text{Ci/kg}) [^{14}\text{C}]-tivozanib hydrochloride.
Urine, feces and blood were collected during the pre-dose period through 24 hours post-dose to measure concentration of radioactivity.
Results:
An average of 1.26%, 22% and 53.7% of the administered [^{14}\text{C}]-tivozanib hydrochloride dose was eliminated in the urine, bile and feces, respectively, indicating that the main route of excretion is through the digestive system (feces).
Tivozanib (parent compound) was not detected in urine and bile, but the metabolites were evident at approximately 0.5% and 0.388% of the administered radioactive dose, respectively. While in feces, parent compound was the predominant radioactive component (representing 32.2 and 33.3% of the administered radioactive dose in intact and bile duct-cannulated rats, respectively). The main metabolites detected (M7, M42, M37/M57) represented < 4.99% of the administered radioactive dose.
The parent compound was the major circulating component in serum, representing approximately 91% of the total radioactivity exposure in serum, along with metabolites M4 (mono-oxidation)/M5 (N-oxide of tivozanib)/M63 (reduction of the isoxazole ring of tivozanib) co-eluting, which represented approximately 9% of the total radioactivity exposure in serum.
In bile, the main metabolites detected were M55 (glutathione conjugation)/M56 (N-oxidation couple with glutathione conjugation followed by cleavage of the conjugate) co-eluting, and M15 (mono-oxidation coupled with glucuronidation)/M60 (O-demethylation coupled with sulfation; co-eluting), representing 6.42 and 8.22% of the administered radioactive dose, respectively. Each of the other unknown metabolites detected in bile represented less than 1.5% of the administered radioactive dose.
C_{\text{max}} and AUC for tivozanib were 8560 ng equiv [^{14}\text{C}]-tivozanib /g and 125000 ng equiv [^{14}\text{C}]-tivozanib. hour/g
L1100: Conclusion:
Tivozanib (parent compound) was the major circulating component representing approximately 91% of the total radioactivity exposure in rat serum. Tivozanib was not detected in urine and bile but the metabolites were evident at approximately 0.5% and 0.388% of the administered radioactive dose. While in feces, tivozanib was the predominant radioactive component and the main metabolites detected (M7, M42, M37/M57) represented < 4.99% of the administered radioactive dose.

Study #:
8255111	
Title: Metabolism of ^{14}\text{C}-Tivozanib Following Oral Administration to Monkeys
GLP: Yes
Objectives: The purpose of this study was to profile and identify the metabolites of ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib following oral administration to monkeys.
Methods:
Two male Cynomolgus monkeys were used on study.
Following a single oral dosing (^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib), blood, urine and feces were collected pre-dose and up to 24 hours post-dose.
Aliquots of urine supernatants, and the serum and fecal extracts were analyzed by HPLC, with fractions collected and analyzed by TopCount solid scintillation counting.
LC-MS techniques were used to determine the metabolite composition in urine, feces, and serum samples.
Study design:
Group	Number of Male Animals	Dose Route	Target Dose Level (mg/kg)	Target Dose Volume (mL/kg)	Samples Collected
1	2	Oral	3	5	Blood, Urine, and Feces
Note: The radioactive dose was approximately 60 \mu\text{Ci}/\text{kg}.
(excerpted from Applicant's report)
Results:
C_{\text{max}} and AUC observed in serum were 1820 ng equivalents ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib/g and 20900 ng equivalents ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib\cdothour/g.
The radio-HPLC analysis showed that the oral dose was absorbed and the parent compound (tivozanib) was the predominant circulating component representing an average of approximately 78% of the total radioactivity exposure in serum. Major metabolites M4 (mono-oxidation)/M5 (N-oxide of tivozanib) co-eluting, represented an average of approximately 17% of the total radioactivity exposure in serum. Minor unknown metabolite M44 represented 1% of the total radioactivity exposure in serum.
L1101: In urine, an average of 3.40% of the administered radioactive dose was eliminated within 24 hours postdose. Parent compound was not detected in the urine. None of the metabolites detected in urine represented an average of greater than 0.5% of the administered radioactive dose. Except for M4/M5 co-eluting, all the other metabolites observed in urine could not be identified.
In feces, an average of 27.4% of the administered radioactive dose was eliminated within 24 hours postdose. Parent compound was the predominant radioactive component (average of 20.6%) of the administered radioactive dose. Each of the other metabolites detected [M37/57 co-eluting, M42, and M7 (O-demethylation)] represented an average of less than 1% of the administered radioactive dose.
Conclusion:
In monkey serum, the parent compound (tivozanib) was the predominant circulating component (78% of the total radioactivity exposure in serum) with the metabolites M4 (mono-oxidation)/M5 (N-oxide of tivozanib) co-eluting. The parent compound was not detected in the urine and none of the metabolites detected in urine represented an average of greater than 0.5% of the administered radioactive dose. In feces, the parent compound was the main component and each of the other metabolites detected [M37/57 co-eluting, M42, and M7] represented an average of less than 1% of the administered radioactive dose. In regard to excretion, the average radioactivity recovery in urine and feces was 3.40 and 27.4% of the administered radioactive dose, respectively.

Study
#:8201725	
Title: In Vitro Metabolism of AV-951 in Rat, Monkey, and Human Primary Hepatocytes, and the In Vitro Metabolism of 14C-AV-951 in Human Primary Hepatocytes
GLP: No
Objectives: To determine the rate of metabolism, to profile and identify metabolites of AV-951, in vitro, using rat, monkey, and human primary hepatocytes, and to determine the metabolism of 14C-AV-951 in human primary hepatocytes.
Methods:
AV-951 was incubated with hepatocytes for 0, 30, 60, and 120 minutes at 0.5 and 5 µM concentrations at 37°C.
Samples were analyzed by LC-MS/MS for remaining AV-951 and by qualitative LC-MS/MS to identify the metabolites formed. Additional incubations with human hepatocytes were conducted with 14C-AV-951 for assessment of human metabolites.
L1102: ## **Results:**
L1103: - AV-951 was metabolized to a small extent by rat and two human donor hepatocytes and to a moderate extent by monkey and one human donor hepatocytes.
- The hepatic clearance was reported to be, &lt;0.4%, &lt;1.2%, &lt;1% of hepatic blood flow for rat, monkey and human based on &gt;99% protein binding of AV-951. This represents low (&lt; 25% of blood flow) hepatic clearance in hepatocyte incubations.
- AV-951 was metabolized mainly by the addition of oxygen, demethylation, glucuronidation, sulfation, or a combination of these pathways.
- Twenty-two different metabolites (M1-M24) were detected in rat, monkey and human hepatocyte incubations with AV-951, with the exception of M11 which was evident only in human (minor &gt;0.5%) and M10 (Desmethyl, glucuronide) was not evident in the rat. Metabolites M10, M11, M14, M4/M5, M23 and M24 were also observed in incubations of human hepatocyte with ^{14}\text{C}-AV-951. As such these metabolites were considered to be the more prominent human metabolites and of these only M14 (desmethyl glucuronide) was present at &gt;10% of total radioactivity and was designated as a major metabolite. M14 was also a considered major metabolite in the rat and monkey.

L1108: ## **Conclusion:**
L1109: All prominent human metabolites (M1 through M24) were present in both rat and monkey with the exception of M11 (glucuronide), which was unique to human hepatocytes and M10 which was not observed with rat hepatocytes. M14 (desmethyl glucuronide) was present at &gt;10% of total radioactivity and was designated as a major metabolite in human, monkey and rat. The hepatic clearance was low (&lt; 25% of blood flow) in rat, monkey and human. AV-951 appeared to be metabolized mainly by oxidation and demethylation.

#:DRZZ102

L1111: **Title:** Comparison of the metabolic profile of KRN951 in human, primate, dog and rat liver microsomes and in Supersomes™ over expressing individual human cytochrome P450 isoforms
L1112: **GLP:** Yes
L1113: ## **Objectives:**
L1114: To investigate species differences in the metabolic profile of KRN951 in human, monkey, dog and rat liver microsomes and to gain information on which human
L1115: cytochrome P450 (CYP) isoform(s) may be involved in the metabolism of KRN951 to its metabolite(s).
Methods:
[^{14}\text{C}] KRN951 (0.500 \mu\text{M} and 5.00 \mu\text{M}) was incubated in the presence of Sprague-Dawley rat, beagle dog, cynomolgus monkey and human liver microsomes for 0, 20, 30, 40, 50 and 60 minutes at a protein concentration of 1.00 mg/mL. Samples were analyzed using HPLC-RAD.
To investigate the involvement of CYP isoforms in the metabolism of KRN951 (0.500 \mu\text{M} and 5.00 \mu\text{M}), metabolite formation was assessed in the presence of heterologously expressed human CYP isoforms (Gentest Supersomes).
Results:
Cytochrome P450 was involved in the metabolism of KRN951.
The disappearance of the KRN951 was NADPH-dependent.
Based on radio-chromatograms, the metabolic profile of the monkey was shown to be the closest to human, and the dog was shown to be the most different. The dog had the fewest metabolites formed and the lowest rate of disappearance of KRN951.
The metabolites formed by rat, dog and monkey were the same as those produced in human liver microsomes with the exception of one metabolite formed by monkey liver microsomes which eluted earlier than any of the four metabolites observed following incubation with human liver microsomes.
The major human hepatic CYP isoforms that were involved in the metabolism of KRN951 were CYP3A4 and CYP1A1.

Conclusion:
CYP3A4 and CYP1A1 are the major human hepatic CYP isoforms involved in the metabolism of KRN951. Title: In Vitro Metabolism of AV-951 in Human Hepatic Microsomes
GLP: No
Objectives: To determine the rate of metabolism and to profile and identify metabolites of AV-951, in vitro, using human hepatic microsomes.

Title: Evaluation of AV-951 on Cytochrome P450 Induction using Primary Cultures of Human Hepatocytes
GLP: No
Objectives: To measure the extent of induction of specific P450 marker enzymes (for CYP1A, 2B6, 2C9, 2C19, and 3A) and relative mRNA concentrations following exposure of human hepatocytes to AV-951, and to compare the effects of AV-951 with those of prototypical inducers.
Methods:
Primary hepatocyte cultures were exposed to AV-951 at 0.1, 0.5, or 5 µM, prototypical CYP inducers, or representative solvent controls for 72 hours. CYP isoenzyme activities were evaluated by determining the rate of production of relevant metabolites utilizing LC-MS/MS detection.
Results/Conclusion:
Exposure to 0.1, 0.5, and 5 µM AV-951 did not cause substantial increases (<40%) in CYP1A, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, CYP2C19 or CYP3A enzyme activities. The data suggested that drug to drug interaction resulting from induction of CYPs enzyme activities by AV-951 was unlikely.

Study #:
8242516	
Title: Identification of Human UGT Isoenzymes Involved in the Metabolism of ^{14}\text{C}-Tivozanib and Associated Inhibitory Interactions
GLP: No
Objectives:
To determine the involvement of specific uridine diphosphate-glucuronosyltransferase (UGT) isoenzymes in the metabolism of ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib using cDNA expressed human UGTs, and to determine the general inhibitory potential of tivozanib towards microsomal UGT activity.
Methods:
The identification of UGT isoenzymes involved in the metabolism of ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib were determined by incubation of the test article with cDNA expressed human UGTs. The rate of disappearance of ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib or metabolite formation was determined.
The inhibitory effects of tivozanib on human hepatic UGT activity were determined by measuring the effect of the test article on the metabolism of UGT marker substrates, 4-methylumbelliferone and para-nitrophenol.
Results/Conclusion:
Substantial levels of ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib desmethyl glucuronide metabolites were formed by UGT isoenzymes.
Metabolite M14, a desmethyl glucuronide metabolite, was the major metabolite formed by six UGT enzymes. Other observed glucuronide metabolites were M10 and M25. Since the major metabolite (M14) was formed by six different UGT isoenzymes, inhibition of any one of these enzymes was not likely to cause substantial drug-drug interactions.
Tivozanib inhibited glucuronidation of UGT substrates 4-MU and p-NP to a small extent. Therefore, tivozanib is not a potent inhibitor of UGT activities.

Study #:
AV951-08274	
Title: In vitro Biotransformation of Tivozanib (AV-951)
GLP: No
Objectives: To determine the formation of metabolic products of tivozanib (AV-951) by human liver microsomes in vitro, and the extent to which the biotransformation is inhibited by ketoconazole, temsirolimus, and everolimus.
L1119: Methods:
A fixed concentration of triazolam (250 \muM - CYP3A4 substrate) was added to varying concentrations of tivozanib hydrochloride (0 to 500 \muM). Ketoconazole (0.1 \muM) and troleandomycin (TAO) (5 \muM) were used as index inhibitors.
Incubations were performed without preincubation of inhibitors with the substrate, followed by incubation with preincubation of inhibitors with microsomes prior to addition of the substrate. HPLC analysis was used to evaluate the metabolites.
Results/Conclusion:
Tivozanib was biotransformed to at least four metabolic products by human liver microsomes.
Tivozanib inhibited biotransformation, with IC50 values of 1.1- 2.0 \muM for Ketoconazole, and > 50 \muM for temsirolimus, and everolimus.
Tivozanib was shown to be a weak inhibitor of CYP3A.

Study #:
DRZZ1040
Title: Whole Body Phosphor Imaging in Male and Female Rats Following a Single Oral Administration of [14C] KRN951
GLP: Yes
Objectives: To provide quantitative data on the tissue distribution of compound related radioactivity in male and female albino and male pigmented rats following a single oral administration of [14C] KRN951 at a target dose level of 1 mg/kg.
Methods:
Twelve male albino, 4 female albino and 6 male pigmented rats were used.
Rats were dosed orally once with 1 mg/kg of [14C] KRN951 (10 mL/kg).
Tissue concentrations of radioactivity were quantified from each of the rats at different time points.
An image representing the distribution of radioactivity across whole body sections at each time point was taken.
A whole blood sample collected from each rat immediately prior to sacrifice was analyzed for radioactivity by liquid scintillation counting.
Results/Conclusion:
The highest concentrations of radioactivity were observed in the contents of the gastrointestinal tract.
Distribution of radioactivity was similar between sexes and strains of rats.
L1120: The extent of tissue penetrance was low, with tissue/blood ratios of less than 1 in the majority of tissues.
Maximal tissue concentrations were observed at 4 hours post-dose.
Highest radioactivity tissue concentrations were measured in the preputial gland, Harderian gland, white fat and liver.
Lowest radiation levels were in the spinal cord and brain.
In 24 hours post-dose the majority of radiation was eliminated from tissues, and by 72 hours post-dose the radiation levels were below the limit of quantification.
No sex differences were reported in the majority of tissues, with concentrations in both the ovary and uterus being very similar to blood levels, and those in testes being significantly less than that in blood.
Melanin binding was apparent and levels decreased over 120 hours post-dose.

GLP: Yes
Objectives: To provide information on the in vitro binding of [^{14}C] KRN951 to the plasma proteins of male and female rat, monkey and human.

Title: The In Vitro Protein Binding of ^{14}C-Tivozanib to Human Plasma and Serum, Albumin and Human \alpha1-Acid Glycoprotein, Protein Binding Interactions, and Human Blood to Plasma Partitioning
L1121: GLP: Yes
Objectives:
To determine, in vitro: 1) the extent of binding of ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib to plasma and serum proteins from human; 2) the potential for serum protein binding interactions between ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib and warfarin in human serum; 3) the partitioning of ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib between blood and plasma; and 4) the extent of binding of ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib to isolated human plasma proteins, i.e., human serum albumin (HSA) and \alpha 1-acid glycoprotein (AAG).

The blood-to-plasma partitioning of ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib was determined at concentrations of 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3 and 5 \mu\text{M} in human blood. ^{14}\text{C}-tivozanib partitions mainly into the plasma/serum compartment based on the mean blood-to-plasma ratio ranged from 0.53 to 0.62 across human donors.

L1136: **Study #:** Title: Pharmacokinetics in Female Ncr Nu/Nu Mice After a Single Oral Dose of 5 or 20 mg/kg Tivozanib Hydrochloride
