L1137: ## 6 General Toxicology
L1138: ## **Overall Summary:**
L1139: Single-oral and intravenous (IV) dose toxicity studies in CD-1 mice and Sprague-Dawley (SD) rats were conducted followed by oral repeat-dose studies in SD rats (up to 26 weeks) and cynomolgus monkeys (up to 39 weeks).
L1140: *IV single-dose:* the maximum tolerated dose in mice and rats following a single IV administration was established to be greater than the highest dose tested of 30 mg/kg, which also is the maximum feasible dose.
L1141: Given that the oral route is the proposed clinical route, the studies used to assess the safety of the compound in support of the current NDA application were performed using the oral route as follows.
L1142: *Oral single-dose studies:* In mice, a single-oral dose of tivozanib at 524 mg/kg (1700-fold of the recommended human dose) was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose. A dose of 524 mg/kg, resulted in a decrease in body weight gain, reduced lymphocyte count, total white blood cell count and increases in ALT, AST, ALP. In rats, a single-oral dose of tivozanib at 276 mg/kg (1790-fold of the recommended human dose) was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose. A dose of 276 mg/kg, resulted in a decrease in body weight, increases in RBC parameters, ALT, AST and urobilinogen.
L1143: *Oral repeat-dose studies in rats:* In the repeat-dose toxicology studies, rats were orally dosed with tivozanib for 13 weeks (0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, or 1.0 mg/kg/day) and 26 weeks (0.005, 0.02 or 0.08 mg/kg/day). In the 13-week rat study, doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day resulted in mortalities and severe clinical observations (clinical signs typically associated with morbidity, malocclusion, missing teeth and white teeth) with effects more pronounced at 1 mg/kg/day, resulting in cessation of dosing at 1 mg/kg/day after 4 weeks of dosing. Body weights and food consumption were decreased throughout the treatment period at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day. These decreases were accompanied by test article-related changes in clinical pathology parameters. The hematology parameters reported at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day included decreases in red blood cell parameters and at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day increases in white blood cell parameters (lymphocytes, monocytes and neutrophils). Clinical chemistry changes included increase in iron metabolism at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day; decrease in protein parameters and increase in liver parameters at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day; increases in LDH/creatinine, triglycerides and renal parameters at 1 mg/kg/day. Test article-related urinalysis changes were reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (increased protein); at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day (increased urobilinogen and bilirubin) and at 1 mg/kg/day (increased hyaline casts). Test article-related macroscopic findings and organ weights were reported in multiple organs in both sexes at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day and doses \ge 0.01 mg/kg/day, respectively. Macroscopic findings associated with test article-related microscopic findings were reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day in both sexes. In general, the incidence and/or severity of the findings were dose-dependent. At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day the target organs with significant toxicity observed were adrenal glands, kidney, bone femur, liver and bile duct, brain, pituitary, incisors, pancreas and duodenum. Less pronounced (lower incidence and/or severity) microscopic findings were reported in the heart, reproductive organs, bone marrow, adrenal medulla and stomach. The NOAEL was considered to be 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED = 0.29g/day, or 0.2X the recommended human dose) based on adverse microscopic findings. Toxicities at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day were significant and many were not recoverable. Partial recovery of most effects was reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, although renal effects were not recoverable at these doses. In the 26-week rat study, findings of white teeth (doses \ge 0.005 mg/kg/day), decreases in body weights, hematology findings (increases in leukocytes, hemoglobin, hematocrit and erythrocytes) and clinical chemistry findings (decreases in total protein, albumin and globulins
L1144: in both sexes) were reported at 0.08 mg/kg/day. The above observed effects were not considered to be adverse, and no additional test article-related findings were reported by the end of the treatment or the recovery periods. The NOAEL was established to be the highest dose tested, 0.08 mg/kg/day (HED = 0.78g/day; or 0.5-fold the recommended human dose).
L1145: *Oral repeat-dose studies in monkeys:* In the repeat-dose toxicology studies, monkeys were orally dosed with tivozanib for 13 weeks (0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/day) and 39 weeks (0.005, 0.01, 0.02, 0.03 or 0.08 mg/kg/day). In the 13-week monkey study, a dose of 1.0 mg/kg was considered severely toxic, and therefore, dosing was ceased after approximately 4 weeks of dosing. Doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day resulted in clinical signs and decrease in body weights. Test article-related hematological changes were evident at 1 mg/kg/day (increases in RBC parameters, fibrin and M/E ratio; decreases in WBC, RETIC, NEUT, MCV and PT). Test article-related clinical and urine chemistry changes were evident at 1 mg/kg/day (increases in CK, SELA-\alpha &amp; \beta, iron, phospholipids; decreases in ALP, SDH, total bile acid, amylase, UIBC, U LDH, U creatinine). The clinical chemistry changes reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (increases in SELA-\alpha, CK, and phospholipids) were considered test article-related, but changes were of lesser magnitude. Some of clinical pathology changes were attributed to the reported body weight changes. At doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day, significant decreases in absolute and relative thymus weights was reported. Macroscopic findings were only evident at 1 mg/kg/day. The predominant test article-related microscopic findings were evident at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day and included bone (femur and tibia), gastric system (stomach, duodenum and pancreas), reproductive system (ovary and corpora lutea), adrenal cortex, lymphoid depletion of lymphoid organs (thymus, lymph nodes and spleen) and brain. At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, brain and adrenal cortex were affected. The majority of the findings were recoverable after a 2-week recovery period at 1 mg/kg. All of the findings at 0.1 and 0.3 mg/kg/day were recoverable during the 5-week recovery period. The NOAEL was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED = 1.95 g/day; 1.3-fold of the recommended human dose).
L1146: In a 39-week monkey study, the highest dose tested (0.1 mg/kg/day) was well tolerated. No test article-related clinical observations were reported. There were no effects on body weight, clinical pathology, echocardiographic, ECG, macro and microscopic findings reported at all dose levels. The NOAEL was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=1.95 g/day; 1.3-fold of the maximum recommended human dose).
L1147: Overall, both the 26-week rat and 39-week monkey toxicology studies were insufficient, as the highest dose(s) assessed produced no/minimal toxicity and dose-response relationships could not be evaluated. Given the low safety margin noted for this study in regard to the clinical dose, it is uncertain why the Applicant did not explore a more robust range of doses in these studies. Given the lack of substantial toxicity in these studies, the Applicant proposed to use the data from the 13-week rat and monkey studies to inform the package insert regarding toxicities in nonclinical studies.
L1148: ## 6.1 Single-Dose Toxicity
L1149: ## **Key findings:**
L1150: Single oral and intravenous dose toxicity studies were performed in CD-1 mice and Sprague-Dawley rats to establish the maximum tolerated dose for tivozanib.
L1151: - In mice, a single oral dose of 524 mg/kg (1700-fold the recommended human dose) was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose. At doses \ge 750 mg/kg, mortality and clinical observations were reported. At doses \ge 524 mg/kg, a decrease in body weight gain, reduced lymphocyte count, total white blood cell count and increases in ALT, AST, ALP were reported.
- In rats, a single oral dose of 276 mg/kg (1790-fold the recommended human dose) was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose. Higher doses resulted in mortalities (369 mg/kg and 2000 mg/kg) and clinical observations. A decrease in body weight was observed in all dose groups. At 276 mg/kg, increases in RBC parameters, ALT, AST and urobilinogen were reported.
- An IV dose &gt; 30 mg/kg (the maximal feasible dose) was established to be the maximum tolerated dose in mice and rats (97- and 195-fold the recommended human dose for mice and rats, respectively).
L1152: **Reviewer comment:** The Toxicokinetic data was not reviewed and discussed in this section for the following reason. The bioanalyses and toxicokinetic data supporting the single-dose toxicology studies (RZZ1008, RZZ 1009, RZZ 1010 and RZZ 1011) were compromised due to data integrity issues and non-compliance with the GLP regulations. As such, the data were not considered reliable, and the Applicant amended the final reports and excluded the data.
L1153: Study #:
RZZ1008	
Title: Acute Intravenous Toxicity Study in the Rat with KRN951 Including Toxicokinetic Sampling
GLP: Yes, with the exception of bioanalytical and toxicokinetic (TK) analysis.
Objectives: to evaluate the maximum tolerated dose and to assess the acute toxicity of KRN951, an angiogenesis inhibitor in the Sprague Dawley rats following a single dose by intravenous (IV) injection.
Methods: All rats (5/sex/group) received a single dose of the test article at 30 mg/kg (the highest soluble dose of KRN951 in vehicle) or the vehicle (40% Dimethylacetamide) by the IV route. Rats were observed up to 14 days post-dose. Endpoints: body weights, clinical pathology, urine, necropsy and TK.
Results:
Clinical observations were evident only during the first 15 minutes post-dose, which included subdued behavior, agitation and abnormal gait.
No effects were reported on body weights. On Day 4, red blood cells, hemoglobin, hematocrit and mean hemoglobin concentration levels were increased, while platelet counts, neutrophils and reticulocytes were reduced in males and females. By Day 15 the changes in red cell
L1154: parameters and platelets were not evident. Clinical chemistry parameters were increased on Day 4 (ALT, AST, Fe, cholesterol and phospholipids), while unsaturated iron binding capacity was decreased. By Day 15 the changes were not evident. A decrease in osmolality of the urine was evident on Day 1, but not on Day 14.
Conclusion:
A dose > 30 mg/kg was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose in rats treated IV with a single dose of KRN951.

Study #:
RZZ1010	
Title: Acute Intravenous Toxicity Study in the Mouse with KRN951 Including Toxicokinetic Sampling
GLP: Yes, with the exception of bioanalytical and TK analysis
Objectives: to evaluate the maximum tolerated dose and to assess the acute toxicity of KRN951 in the CD-1 mouse following a single dose by IV injection.
Methods: Mice received a single dose of KRN951 (15/sex/group) at 30 mg/kg (the highest soluble dose of KRN951 in vehicle) or the vehicle (40% Dimethylacetamide) by the IV route. Mice were observed for up to 14 Days post-dose.
Endpoints: body weights, clinical pathology, necropsy and TK.
Results:No clinical observations or effects on body weights were reported.
Transient changes in hematology (decrease in reticulocytes) were reported on Day 4. No effects on clinical chemistry parameters were reported.

Conclusion:
A dose of > 30 mg/kg was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose in CD-1 mice treated IV with a single dose of KRN951.

Study #:
RZZ1011	
Title: Acute Oral Toxicity Study in the Mouse with KRN951 Including Toxicokinetic (TK) Sampling
GLP: Yes, with the exception of bioanalytical and TK analysis
Objectives: to evaluate the maximum tolerated dose and to assess the acute toxicity of KRN951 in the CD-1 mouse following a single dose by oral gavage.
Methods: Mice received a single oral dose of the test article.
In phase 1: Doses of 369, 750 and 1000 mg/kg were administered to 2 mice/sex/group. Mice were observed for up to 7 days post-dose.
In phase 2: 15 mice/sex/group were dosed with 0.5% methylcellulose or 524 mg/kg KRN951. Mice were observed for up to 15 days post-dose. Blood was
L1155: collected for clinical pathology and urine for urinalysis. Necropsy was performed at the end of the observation period.
In phase 3: 14 mice/sex were dosed orally with 524 mg/kg and bled for TK analysis.
Results:
One male mouse at 750 mg/kg and one at 1000 mg/kg were terminated due to severe signs of toxicity at approximately 7 and 4 hours post-dose on Day 1, respectively.
At doses \ge 750 mg/kg clinical observations were reported that included tremors, subdued behavior, piloerection, cold, dilated pupils, abnormal gait and/or pale ears. No clinical observations were reported at doses \le 524 mg/kg.
At doses \ge 524 mg/kg a decrease of up to 13% in body weight gain was reported.
At 524 mg/kg lymphocyte count and total white blood cell count were reduced on Day 2.
ALT, AST and ALP were increased at 524 mg/kg.
Conclusion:
A dose of 524 mg/kg was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose in CD-1 mice treated orally with a single dose of KRN951.

Study #:
RZZ1009	
Title: Acute Oral Toxicity Study in the Rat with KRN951 Including Toxicokinetic Sampling
GLP: Yes, with the exception of bioanalytical and TK analysis
Objectives: To evaluate the maximum tolerated dose and to assess the acute toxicity of KRN951 in Sprague Dawley rat following a single dose by oral gavage.
Methods: Rats received a single oral dose of the test article.
In phase 1: Doses of 369, 1000 or 2000 mg/kg were given orally to 2 rats/sex/group. Rats were observed for up to 7 Days post-dose.
In phase 2: 5 rats/sex/group were dosed with 0.5% methylcellulose or 276 mg/kg KRN951. Rats were observed for up to 14 Days post-dose. Blood was collected for clinical pathology and urine for urinalysis. Necropsy was performed at the end of the observation period.
In phase 3: 14 rats/sex were dosed orally with 400 mg/kg and bled for TK analysis.
Results:
One male at 369 mg/kg was found dead on Day 8 and one female at 2000 mg/kg was terminated on Day 6.
Clinical observations were reported at 369 mg/kg (hunched posture and piloerection), 1000 mg/kg (piloerection and distended abdomen) and at
L1156: 2000 mg/kg (distended abdomen, irregular breathing, subdued behavior, piloerection, abnormal gait, emaciation, weight loss, hunched posture, tremors (male only), restless behavior, pale ears and lack of coordination).A decrease in body weight was reported at all dose groups (up to 10% at doses \le 1000 mg/kg and up to 24% at 2000 mg/kg).
At 276 mg/kg hematology parameters (red blood cells, hemoglobin and hematocrit) were increased on Day 2 and decreased on Day 15. Lymphocyte count (Lymph) and total white blood cell count (WBC) were reduced while neutrophils were increased at Day 2.
At 276 mg/kg ALT and AST were increased on Day 2.
At 276 mg/kg urobilinogen was increased on Day 3.
Conclusion:
A dose of 276 mg/kg was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose in rats treated orally with a single dose of KRN951.
L1157: ## 6.2 Repeat-Dose Toxicity
L1158: **Reviewer comment:** The Toxicokinetic data for study 6691-158 was not reviewed and discussed in this section for the following reason: the bioanalyses and toxicokinetic data was compromised due to data integrity issues and non-compliance with the GLP regulations. As such, the data was not considered reliable, and the Applicant amended the final report and excluded the data.
L1159: Study title: 13-Week Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study with a 5-Week Recovery Period with KRN951 in Rats
Study no.:	6691-158
Sponsor:	Kyowa Hakko Kirin Co., Ltd.,
(formerly Kirin Brewery Co., Ltd),
Tokyo, Japan
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
Date of study initiation:	07 July, 2003
GLP compliance/QA statement:	Yes, with the exception of bioanalytical and TK analyses
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	KRN951, CDM-001 and 98.8%
L1160: ## Key Findings:
L1161: - The NOAEL was considered to be 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED = 0.29g/day, or 0.2X the recommended human dose) based on adverse microscopic findings.
- Test article-related **mortalities** were reported at doses \ge0.1 mg/kg/day in male and female rats.
L1162: - Test article-related **clinical observations** were reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day in male and female rats with effects more pronounced at 1 mg/kg/day, resulting in cessation of dosing at 1 mg/kg/day after 4 weeks of dosing.Clinical observations included clinical signs typically associated with morbidity, malocclusion, missing teeth and white teeth. Treatment-related white teeth and malocclusion persisted throughout the recovery period.
- Test article-related decrease in mean **body weight** was reported at \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day (-13% to -38%) throughout the treatment period, and at \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day (up to -25%) at the end of the recovery period.
- Test article-related decrease in **food consumption** was reported (both sexes) at \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day during the treatment period. Findings persisted at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day throughout the recovery period.
- Test article-related changes in **hematology** parameters were reported at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day and included decreases in red blood cell parameters. At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day increases in white blood cell parameters (lymphocytes, monocytes and neutrophils) were reported. Most changes persisted at the end of recovery.
- **Clinical chemistry** changes included increase in iron metabolism at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day; decrease in protein parameters and increase in liver parameters at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day; increases in LDH/creatinine, triglycerides and renal parameters at 1 mg/kg/day. At the end of the recovery period, no test article-related findings were reported at doses \le 0.1 mg/kg/day, however, effects at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day persisted.
- Test article-related **urine changes** were reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (increased protein); at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day (increased urobilinogen and bilirubin) and at 1 mg/kg/day (increased hyaline casts). At the end of the recovery period, findings persisted at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day.
- Test article-related **macroscopic findings** were reported in both sexes at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day (in adrenal gland; duodenum, pancreas, testis, epididymis and seminal vesicle; missing teeth and mandibular lymph nodes). At the end of the recovery period, macroscopic findings were only reported at 1 mg/kg/day and they included pale adrenal cortex and kidney.
- Test article-related increases in a number of relative **organ weights** were reported at doses \ge 0.01 mg/kg/day. At the end of the recovery period, effects on relative organ weights persisted at \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day.
- Test article-related **microscopic findings** were reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day in both sexes. In general, the incidence and/or severity of the findings were dose-dependent.At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day the target organs with significant toxicity observed were adrenal glands (congestion/hemorrhage, degeneration and necrosis), kidney (glomerulopathy and chronic nephropathy), bone femur (hypertrophy of the physis and loss of the primary spongiosa), liver and bile duct (inflammation and biliary hypertrophy/hyperplasia), brain (vacuolated cells in choroid plexus and choroid vessel necrosis), pituitary (hypertrophic and degenerate chromophobe cells), incisors (degeneration of odontoblasts, ameloblasts and
L1163: dentin dysplasia at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day), pancreas (atrophy) and duodenum (transmural and mucosal hypertrophy).
L1164: - Less pronounced (lower incidence and/or severity) microscopic findings were reported in the heart (focal necrosis and myofiber degeneration), reproductive organs (atrophy of the ovaries and uterus), bone marrow (hypocellularity), adrenal medulla (necrosis) and stomach (congestion, hyperplasia/metaplasia of fundic epithelium).
- By the end of the recovery period, most microscopic findings at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day showed partial recovery, except for the microscopic findings in the kidney, which were not recoverable.
L1165: Tissues showing partial recovery included brain, adrenal cortex, bone marrow, bone, heart, pancreas, ovary, uterus and teeth at 1 mg/kg/day;; adrenal cortex, and duodenum at 0.3 mg/kg/day; brain, heart, liver; kidney, pancreas, uterus, bone and bone marrow at 0.1 and 0.3 mg/kg/day.
L1166: ## Methods:
L1167: **Doses/ Number/Sex/Group:** Male and female rats were assigned to treatment Groups 1-6 with 30, 10, 10, 15, 15 and 15 animals/sex/group, respectively.
Group 1 received vehicle control (0.5% MC); and Groups 2 through 6 received 0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, or 1.0 mg/kg/day, respectively.

**Route/Frequency of dosing:** Oral gavage/Daily

**Dose volume:** 10 mL/kg

**Formulation/Vehicle:** Vehicle, 0.5% methylcellulose

**Sex/Species/Strain:** Male and female Crl:CD®(SD) IGS BR rats

**Age/Weight:** 8 weeks /
238 to 285 g for the males, and
172 to 214 g for the females.

**Toxicokinetic evaluation:** 5 rats per sex per group were assigned to evaluating the Toxicokinetic (TK) at each dose level (Groups 7 through 11, respectively).

**Endpoints evaluated:** Mortality, clinical observations, ophthalmic examinations, body weight, weight gain, food consumption, organ weights, macroscopic findings, clinical pathology and microscopic findings.
Clinical pathology (hematology, Chemistry, coagulation, and urine): were collected based on the following schedule:

L1174: - Three male and three female rats at 1 mg/kg/day were found dead or moribund sacrificed during weeks 3-4.
- At 0.3 mg/kg/day, three male and three female rats were found dead or moribund sacrificed during weeks 7- 12 of the study.
- One female was found dead at 0.1 mg/kg/day during week 5.
- All found dead or moribund sacrificed rats at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day, were associated with microscopic findings of adrenal necrosis/degeneration. The animal found dead at 0.1 mg/kg/day, had urinary tract inflammation and adrenal necrosis.

L1188: ## **Body Weight**
L1189: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}  {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L1190: At 1 mg/kg/day test article-related decreases in mean body weights were reported in males (m)  and females (f) during the first 4 weeks of treatment (by up to 33% in males and 26% in  females), and at the end of the interim recovery period, body weight decreases persisted (up  38% in males and 19% in females). Throughout the period, (weeks 1 to 13), mean body weights  in males and females were lower than control by up to 15% and 9% at 0.1 mg/kg/day,  respectively, and by up to 41% and 32% at 0.3 mg/kg/day. The decreases in mean body  weights persisted throughout the recovery period at 0.3 mg/kg/day (by up to 25% in males and  21% in females). Decreases in mean body weights were also reported in females at 0.03  mg/kg/day (up to 13% in females) during weeks 8 to 13. No effect on mean body weight was  reported at the end of the recovery period at 0.03 mg/kg/day. In general, the effects were  more pronounced in males at all dose groups.
L1191: ## *Mean body weight (% Change):*

L1198: ## **Body Weight Gain **
L1199: Male rats gained up to 10%, 15% and 97% less weight at 0.1, 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day relative to  controls during the first 25 days of treatment, respectively. For the entire treatment period  (Days 1-92) male rats gained up to 10%, 7%, 17% and 63% at 0.01, 0.03, 0.1 and 0.3 mg/kg/day,  less body weight than the control animals, respectively. Female rats gained up to 19%, 19%,  18% and 99% less weight at 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day relative to controls during the first  25 days of treatment, respectively. For the entire treatment period (Days 1-92) female rats  gained up to 15%, 30%, 20% and 45% at 0.01, 0.03, 0.1 and 0.3 mg/kg/day, less body weight  than the control animals, respectively. No test article-related effects were reported at the end  of the recovery period.

L1204: ## Food Consumption
L1205: Test article-related decreases in food consumption were reported at 1 mg/kg/day in male and female rats during the first 25 days of treatment (up to 55% in males and 56% in females). Decreases in food consumption were also reported during the entire treatment period (Days 1-92) in males and females at 0.3 mg/kg/day (46% in males and 50% in females), at 0.1 mg/kg/day (up to 11% in males and 14% in females) and at 0.03 mg/kg/day in females (up to 13% in females). Food consumption values continued to be lower than the control group values at the end of the recovery period at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day (up to 1% in males and 15% in females).

L1216: ## Hematology
L1217: At 1 mg/kg/day, test article-related significant changes were reported in:
L1218: - Males, red blood cells parameters, platelet, neutrophils (NEUT), monocytes, APTT, and myeloid/erythroid (M/E) ratio during week 4. At the end of the recovery period (week 7) the following parameters were still evident: red blood cells (RBC), reticulocytes (RETIC), Absolute reticulocytes (AB RETIC), MCV and Monocytes.
- Females, red blood cells parameters, AB RETIC, platelet, and M/E ratio during week 4. At the end of the recovery period (week 7) the following parameters were still evident: RBC, mean corpuscular hemoglobin (MCH) and fibrinogen (Fibrin).
L1219: At 0.3 mg/kg/day test article-related significant changes were reported in:
L1220: - Males, red blood cells parameters RETIC, AB RETIC, platelet, white blood cell (WBC), NEUT, lympho and Mono during week 7. Monocyte values were still higher than controls at the end of the recovery period (week 19).
- Females, hemoglobin (HGB), hematocrit (HCT), WBC, NEUT, lymphocytes and monocytes during week 7. Monocyte values were still higher than controls at the end of the recovery period (week 19).
L1221: At 0.1 mg/kg/day test article-related significant changes were reported in:
L1222: - Males, red blood cells parameters during week 7/14.
- Females, red blood cells parameters during week 7/14.
L1223: Recovery: No findings in both sexes were reported at the end of the recovery period (week 19).
L1224: At 0.03 mg/kg/day test article-related significant effects were reported in:
L1225: - Females, red blood cells parameters, RETIC and AB RETIC during week 7/14.
Recovery: No findings in both sexes were reported at the end of the recovery period (week 19).
L1226: At 0.01 mg/kg/day the only test article-related change was reported in female HGB (week 14).

L1235: ## **Clinical Chemistry**
L1236: Predominant treatment-related clinical chemistry changes:
L1237: - Protein parameters (total protein, albumin, albumin/globulin ration): parameters were decreased at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day. Total protein and albumin were evident at a lesser magnitude at the end of the recovery period.
- Liver parameters: ALT was increased at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, total bilirubin, Gamma-Glutamyltransferase (U GGT), LDH and triglycerides were increased at 1 mg/kg/day. No findings were reported at the end of the recovery period.
- LDH/creatinine was increased at 1 mg/kg/day and effect on LDH/creatinine at a lesser magnitude was evident at the end of the recovery period.
- Iron metabolism parameters: U Iron binding capacity and percent saturation of transferrin (%SAT) were increased at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day. Serum iron was increased at 0.3 mg/kg/day.
- Renal parameters: Urea nitrogen was increased at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day and urea creatinine at 1 mg/kg/day. Effect on Urea nitrogen at a lesser magnitude was evident at the end of the recovery period.

L1245: ## Urinalysis
L1246: - Test article-related urine parameter changes reported:At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day was increased protein
- At doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day were increased urobilinogen and bilirubin
- At 1 mg/kg/day was hyaline casts

- At doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day, increased urobilinogen
- At 1 mg/kg/day, bilirubin
L1247: ## Macroscopic Findings
L1248: - At doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day included enlarged, dark, mottled and mass of the adrenal gland; distended bile duct, fluid lumen and material lumen of the liver; dark areas in mucosa of glandular region and distended of the stomach; thickened wall of the duodenum; gelatinous of the pancreas; soft and small testis; small epididymis and seminal vesicle; teeth missing and enlarged mandibular).
- At 1 mg/kg/day included thickened wall of the ileum; and enlarged, pale and mottled kidney)
- No macroscopic findings were reported at doses \le 0.1 mg/kg/day.
- At the end of the recovery period pale adrenal cortex (female) and pale kidney (female) persisted at 1 mg/kg/day.
L1249: ## Organ Weights
L1250: - Test article-related increases in a number of relative organ weights were reported at doses \ge 0.01 mg/kg/day.At 1 mg/kg/day, there were test article-related increases in organ weights relative to body weight during the interim sacrifice in males (brain, liver, kidney, spleen, testis, thymus, salivary gland, adrenal, and epididymis) and females (brain, uterus, thymus, pituitary, salivary gland, ovary and adrenal).At the end of the recovery period relative organ weight increases persisted at 1 mg/kg/day in males (increases in liver, lung, heart and
L1251: salivary gland) and females (increases in liver, spleen, thymus, pituitary, salivary gland and thyroid).

- At 0.3 mg/kg/day there were test article-related increases in relative organ weights during the terminal sacrifice: In males (brain, liver, testis, thymus, lung, salivary gland, adrenal gland, thyroid and epididymis) and in females (brain, liver, spleen, uterus, thymus, heart, salivary gland, ovary and adrenal gland).At the end of the recovery period at 0.3 mg/kg/day, relative organ weight increases persisted in males (kidney, testis, lung and salivary gland) and females (brain, spleen, heart, adrenal and salivary gland).
- Test article-related changes in relative organ weights were reported at 0.1 mg/kg/day (male kidney, female brain and heart), 0.03 mg/kg/day (male kidney, female lung and brain) and at 0.01 mg/kg/day (male kidney).No effects on relative organ weight were evident at the end of the recovery period at doses \le0.03 mg/kg/day.
L1252: ## Microscopic Findings
L1253: - Test article-related microscopic findings were reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day in both sexes. In general, the incidence and/or severity of the findings were dose dependent. By the end of the recovery period, a number of the microscopic findings were reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day.At doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day, severe test article-related microscopic findings were observed in the adrenal gland (moderate to severe - congestion/hemorrhage, moderate to marked-degeneration, marked to severe-necrosis), brain (slight to marked- vacuolated cells in choroid plexus, moderate - choroid vessel necrosis), kidney (moderate to marked - glomerulopathy, moderate to marked -chronic nephropathy), bone femur (marked to severe - hypertrophy of the physis and loss of the primary spongiosa), liver and bile ducts (minimal to marked - inflammation and biliary hypertrophy/hyperplasia), incisors (severe - degeneration of odontoblasts and marked - ameloblasts, marked to severe - dentin dysplasia), pancreas (marked- atrophy), duodenum (marked to severe - transmural and mucosal hypertrophy), ovary (moderate- atrophy and cyst follicle) and uterus (moderate - atrophy).
- Other microscopic findings were reported with less pronounced effects: pituitary (minimal to marked - hypertrophic and degenerate chromophobe cells), stomach (slight - congestion, moderate - hyperplasia/metaplasia of fundic epithelium), heart (slight to minimal - focal necrosis, myofiber degeneration), lymphoid organs (marked - lymphoid depletion), adrenal medulla (minimal to severe necrosis) and bone marrow (minimal to marked - hypocellularity).
- At 0.1 mg/kg/day, the test article-related microscopic findings reported were observed in the brain (slight vacuolated cells in choroid plexus, choroid vessel necrosis), bone femur (minimal hypertrophy of the physis and moderate - loss of the primary spongiosa), pituitary (slight - hypertrophic and degenerate chromophobe cells), adrenal cortex (slight degeneration of zona fasciculata, congestion/hemorrhage), liver (slight pigment,
L1254: hepatocytes, brown, granular, hypertrophy/hyperplasia, bile ducts), kidney (minimal-glomerulopathy), heart (minimal degeneration, focal inflammation).

Grades for Severity of the microscopic findings:
1 Minimal - the least amount of change observed with the light microscope
2 Slight - less than average amount of change, but readily discernible as abnormal
3 Moderate - the average amount of change expected for a lesion
4 Marked - a marked amount of change with possible loss of function of the affected cells or organs
5 Severe - a great amount of change with probable loss of function of the affected cell or organs and frequently involves large areas of the organ.
L1260: Recovery period:
L1261: - At 1 mg/kg/day, microscopic findings at the end of recovery were observed in the brain (minimal to slight - vacuolated cells, choroid plexus), adrenal cortex (minimal to slight - degeneration, congestion/hemorrhage), heart (slight to moderate - degeneration), kidney (minimal to severe - glomerulopathy, casts, tubules, protein (hyaline), nephropathy, chronic); minimal hypocellular bone marrow (sternum, femur), bone (minimal-Hypertrophy of the physis and minimal to marked -Loss of the primary spongiosa), pancreas (minimal - infiltrate, interstitial, macrophage, atrophy), ovary (minimal - atrophy), uterus (slight - atrophy) and teeth incisors and molar (slight to marked - hyperplasia, odontoblasts, dysplasia/degeneration and dentin).
- At 0.3 mg/kg/day, microscopic findings reported at the end of the recovery period were observed in the adrenal cortex (moderate to marked - atrophy, pigment, hemosiderin) and duodenum (minimal to moderate - hypertrophy, transmural).
- At 0.1 and 0.3 mg/kg/day, microscopic findings at the end of the recovery period were reported in the brain (slight - vacuolated cells, choroid plexus), heart (minimal to slight - degeneration, focal inflammation), liver (minimal to moderate - hepatocytes, hypertrophy/hyperplasia, bile ducts); kidney (slight to moderate - glomerulopathy, casts, tubules, protein (hyaline), nephropathy, chronic), pancreas (slight - atrophy, focal infiltrate mononuclear cell, inflammation), bone (minimal-Hypertrophy of the physis and minimal to marked -loss of the primary spongiosa), hypertrophy bone marrow (femur) and uterus (slight to moderate- dilatation).

Grades for Severity of the microscopic findings:
1 Minimal - the least amount of change observed with the light microscope
2 Slight - less than average amount of change, but readily discernible as abnormal
3 Moderate - the average amount of change expected for a lesion
4 Marked - a marked amount of change with possible loss of function of the affected cells or organs
5 Severe - a great amount of change with probable loss of function of the affected cell or organs and frequently involves large areas of the organ

L1268: ## Conclusion
L1269: The NOAEL was considered to be 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED = 0.29g/day, or 0.2X the recommended human dose) based on adverse microscopic findings. Toxicities at \ge 0.3mg/kg/day were significant and many were not recoverable. Partial recovery of most effects was reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, although renal effects were not recoverable at these doses.
L1270: Study title: KRN951: A 26-Week Oral Toxicity Study in Rats With a 6-Week Recovery Period
Study no.:	1458-005
Sponsor:	AVEO Pharmaceuticals, Inc.
Cambridge, Massachusetts 02139
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
Date of study initiation:	23 October, 2008
GLP compliance and QA statement:	Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	AV-951, CDM-005 and 99.53%
L1271: ## Key Findings
L1272: - No test article-related mortalities were reported.
- The only test article-related clinical observation reported was white teeth at doses \ge 0.005 mg/kg/day. The effect was not considered to be an adverse effect.
- Female body weights at 0.08 mg/kg/day, were slightly decreased (up to 6%) during the last 3 weeks of treatment. The effect was not considered to be an adverse effect.
- Test article-related increases in leukocytes, hemoglobin, hematocrit and erythrocytes were reported in male and female rats at 0.08 mg/kg/day at weeks 13 and/or 26. These findings were not considered to be adverse.
- Test article-related decreases were reported at 0.08 mg/kg/day in total protein, albumin and globulin in both sexes at week 13 and 26. These findings were not considered to be adverse, due to the magnitude of effect.
- There were no test article-related effects on organ weights, macroscopic and microscopic findings at all dose levels.
- No test article-related effects were reported at the end of the recovery period.
L1273: - The NOAEL was established to be the highest dose tested, 0.08 mg/kg/day (HED = 0.78g/day; or 0.5-fold the maximum recommended human dose).

L1294: ## **Results:**
L1295: ## **Analysis of dosing formulations**
L1296: The reported data indicated that the formulations were stable (up to 21 days) and homogenous. Analyses were performed and confirmed that formulation concentrations during weeks 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 20, and 24, were within the acceptable range (\pm 15\%) with the exception of the formulation on week 3 (18.7%) and 12 (16.8%).
L1297: ## **Mortality**
L1298: No test article-related mortalities were reported. Two male rats at 0.005 mg/kg/day and one male rat at 0.08 mg/kg/day were found dead or moribund sacrificed. The reported mortalities were not considered test article-related. One male rat was inadvertently killed as the technician was closing the cage on Day 89 (male at 0.08 mg/kg/day), the second male rat (0.005 mg/kg/day) was euthanized due to the severe clinical observations and the cause of death was the formation of multicentric malignant lymphoma; and the cause of death for the third male (0.005 mg/kg/day) is unknown. Due to lack of dose response relationship the deaths at 0.005 mg/kg/day were not considered test article-related.

L1304: The only test article-related clinical observation was white teeth at doses \ge 0.005 mg/kg/day. The reported observation was evident in all dose groups and was consistent with findings in prior study (13-week repeat dose study in rats-study # 6691-158); therefore, the effect was considered to be test article-related.

L1356: ## **Toxicokinetics**
L1357: - The toxicokinetic parameters were similar in male and female rats on days 1, 91 and 182.
- AUC0-24, Cmax and t1/2 were higher on days 91 and 182 than on day 1, but were similar on days 91 and 182.
- AUC0-24 was 1.8 to 2.6 times higher on days 91 and 182 than on day 1, indicating accumulation.
- Cmax was 1.6 to 2.9 times higher on days 91 and 182 than on day 1, indicating accumulation.
- The similarity in systemic exposure on days 91 and 182 suggests that steady state levels were reached by day 91, and that no further accumulation occurred beyond day 91.
- AUC0-24 and Cmax increased in approximate proportion to dose on days 1, 91 and 182, suggesting that systemic exposure was linear with dose.
- Tmax was unaffected by dose level or duration of treatment. The rate of absorption (Tmax) ranged from 2 to 4 hours.
- Elimination half-life (t1/2) ranged from 9 to 45 hours.

L1362: ## **Conclusion:**
L1363: There were minimal toxicities observed at all doses up to the highest dose tested (0.08 mg/kg/day). The clinical observation of white teeth was evident at all doses. Test article-related findings were evident at 0.08 mg/kg/day and included decrease in body weight, and hematological and clinical chemistry changes. The above observed effects were not considered to be adverse, and no test article-related macroscopic or microscopic findings were reported at any dose levels. The NOAEL was established to be the highest dose tested 0.08 mg/kg/day (0.5-fold the maximum recommended human dose).
L1364: Overall this repeat-dose toxicity study was insufficient, as the highest dose(s) assessed produced limited toxicity and dose-response relationships could not be evaluated. Given the low safety margin noted for this study in regard to the clinical dose, it is uncertain why the Applicant did not explore a more robust range of doses in this study. Given the lack of substantial toxicity in this study, the Applicant proposed to use the data from the 13-week rat study to inform the package insert regarding toxicities observed in rats.
L1365: **Title: 13-Week Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study with a 5-Week Recovery Period with KRN951 in Cynomolgus Monkeys**
L1366: **Study # 6691-159**
L1367: Sponsor:	AVEO Pharmaceuticals, Inc.
Cambridge, Massachusetts 02139
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
Date of study initiation:	10 December 2003
GLP compliance/QA statement:	Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	KRN951, CDM-001 and 98.8%
L1368: ## Key Findings
L1369: - The NOAEL was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (1.3-fold the maximum recommended human dose).
- No unscheduled **deaths** were reported at all dose levels.
- Dose of 1 mg/kg/day resulted in severe **clinical signs** that resulted in cessation of dosing after 4 weeks of dosing. The clinical signs included dehydrated appearance, hunched posture, thin appearance, hypoactivity, discolored, few/no/non-formed feces, low/no food consumption. In general, the observations were more pronounced in males. At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, low/no food consumption was observed and at 0.3 mg/kg/day dehydration and hunched appearance were observed in one male monkey. No remarkable clinical observations were reported during the recovery period.
- Mean **body weights** were decreased by up to 20% and 8% in males and females at 1 mg/kg/day, respectively, and by 18% and 8% in males and females at 0.3 mg/kg/day, respectively, during treatment period. No body weight changes were reported at the end of the recovery period.
- Test article-related **hematological changes** were evident at 1 mg/kg/day (increases in RBC parameters, fibrin and M/E ratio; decreases in WBC, RETIC, NEUT, MCV and PT). No hematological changes were reported at the end of the recovery period. No test article-related effects were reported at doses \le 0.3 mg/kg/day.
- Test article-related **clinical and urine chemistry** changes were evident at 1 mg/kg/day (increases in CK, SELA-\alpha &amp; \beta, iron, phospholipids; decreases in ALP, SDH, total bile acid, amylase, UIBC, U LDH, U creatinine). The clinical chemistry changes reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (increases in SELA-\alpha, CK, and phospholipids) were considered test article-related, but changes were of lesser magnitude. Other changes at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day were attributed to the reported decreases in food consumption and/or lower body weights. No changes were reported at the end of the recovery period.
- At doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day, **organ weight changes** included significant decreases in absolute and relative thymus weights.
- Test article-related **macroscopic observations** were reported at 1 mg/kg/day (dark thyroid, dark adrenals, distended gallbladder and thickened gastric mucosa and/or dark areas on the gastric mucosa in the stomach of males). The findings in the thyroid and thymus (females) were evident at the end of the recovery period.
L1370: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L1371: - Predominant test article-related **microscopic findings** included:At \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day effects in the following tissues were reported: bone (femur and tibia), gastric system (stomach, duodenum and pancreas), reproductive system (ovary and corpora lutea), adrenal cortex, lymphoid depletion of lymphoid organs (thymus, lymph nodes and spleen) and brain.
- At \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day in the following organs: brain and adrenal cortex.
- The majority of the microscopic findings were recoverable after a 2-week recovery period at 1mg/kg. All of the findings at 0.1 and 0.3 mg/kg/day were recoverable during the 5-week recovery period.
- No test article-related microscopic findings were reported at doses \le 0.03 mg/kg/day.
- Exposure to KRN951 increased with dose in both sexes. No gender differences were reported. C_{max} and AUC values were higher on days 28 and 91 than day 1, indicating accumulation.
L1372: ## Methods:
L1373: **Species/strain:** Cynomolgus monkey
L1374: **Age/weight:** 2 to 4 years old; 1.9 to 3.2 kg for males and 2.0 to 2.8 kg for females.
L1375: **Doses Administered:** 0, 0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day (Groups 1-6)
L1376: **Dose volume** - 10 mL/kg.
L1377: **Vehicle:** 0.5% methylcellulose
L1378: **Sex/Number/Route:** Males and females; 10, 3, 3, 5, 5, and 5/sex/Groups 1 through 6, respectively; oral gavage.
L1379: **Duration of dosing/recovery:** 3 weeks/ 5 weeks recovery (Groups 1-5); Group 1 and 6 were dosed for 4 weeks with 2 weeks recovery.
L1380: ## Study design:
L1381: - Three males and three females in the control and 1.0 mg/kg/day groups were sacrificed after at least 4 weeks of treatment (interim sacrifice) and two males and two females in the control and 1.0 mg/kg/day groups (after treatment for 4 weeks) were sacrificed after a 2 weeks of recovery period (recovery-1 Phase).
- Remaining monkeys were sacrificed after at least 13 weeks of treatment (terminal sacrifice) or placed on at least 5 weeks of recovery (recovery-2 Phase) after 13 weeks of treatment.

L1383: (excerpted from Applicant's report)
L1384: **Endpoints:** Mortality, clinical observations, body weights, food consumption, clinical pathology, ophthalmic examinations, electrocardiogram examinations, toxicokinetic, gross pathology, organ weight and microscopic evaluation.
L1385: **Histopathology peer review:** was performed
L1386: ## Results:
L1387: ## Mortality
L1388: No unscheduled deaths occurred during the study.
L1389: ## Clinical Signs
L1390: Dose of 1 mg/kg/day resulted in severe clinical signs that resulted in cessation of dosing after 4 weeks of dosing. The clinical signs included dehydrated appearance; hunched posture; thin appearance, hypoactivity; discolored, few, no, and/or non-formed feces; and/or low or no food consumption. The observations were more pronounced in males. At 0.3 mg/kg/day, no food consumption was reported in 2 out of 5 male monkeys and dehydration and hunched appearance reported in one male monkey. An increased frequency of low or no food consumption was also reported at 0.1 mg/kg/day during the treatment period. Unremarkable clinical observations were reported during the recovery period.
L1391: ## Body weights
L1392: Dose of 1 mg/kg/day resulted in 20% and 8% decreases in male and female mean body weights relative to the control group by the end of treatment period (day 1-29). The individual body weight gain data showed that some male and female monkeys lost up to 0.7 kg and 0.6 kg body weight, respectively, relative to the control monkeys (mean gain of 0.1 kg and 0 kg in male and females, respectively) during the treatment period (days 1-29). Dose of 0.3 mg/kg/day resulted in 18% and 8% decreases in male and female mean body weights relative to the control group by the end of treatment period (day 1-92). Body weight gain data showed that male and female monkeys at 0.3 mg/kg/day had decreased body weight gain of 0.2 kg and 0.1 kg relative to the control animals (mean gain of 0.4 kg and 0 kg in male and female, respectively) during the entire treatment period (Days 1-92). No significant body weight gain changes were reported at doses \le 0.1 mg/kg/day. No significant differences were reported at the end of the recovery period at all dose groups.
L1393: ## Clinical Pathology
L1394: ## Hematological
L1395: Test article-related hematological changes were evident at 1 mg/kg/day (increases in RBC, Hgb, Hct, Fibrin and M/E ratio; decreases in WBC, RETIC, NEUT, MCV and PT). By the end of the recovery period the majority of the values were similar to the control values. Some of the changes (RBC, Hct and HGB) can be attributed to the decrease in food consumption, lower body weights and/or dehydration. The changes reported at doses \le 0.3 mg/kg/day
L1396: (increase in Hgb, Hct and/or Fibrin) were of lesser magnitude, not statistically significant and were not considered adverse.
L1397: ## Clinical and urine chemistry
L1398: Test article-related clinical and urine chemistry changes were evident at 1 mg/kg/day (increases in AST, SELA-\alpha &amp; \beta, magnesium, creatinine, cholesterol, CK, potassium, U albumin, iron, SAT, phospholipids; decreases in alkaline phosphatase (ALP), amylase, chloride, UIBC, SDH, total bile acid, free fatty acids (FFA), U LDH, U creatinine). By the end of the recovery period the majority of the values were similar to the controls. Some of the reported changes can be attributed to the decrease in food consumption and/or lower body weights (i.e. increase in potassium, cholesterol, magnesium, creatinine, albumin, FFA, AST and decreased chloride). The decreases in amylase and ALP activity are correlated with the microscopic findings in pancreas (zymogen granules) and bone growth. The changes reported at 0.3 mg/kg/day (increases in creatinine, SELA-\alpha, CK, AST, cholesterol and phospholipids) and at 0.1 mg/kg/day (increases in AST, SELA-\beta, CK, cholesterol and phospholipids) were of lesser magnitude, but considered to be test article-related. No changes were reported at the end of the recovery period.

L1403: ## **Ophthalmoscopy**
L1404: There were no reported test article-related ophthalmological findings at all dose levels.
L1405: ## **Electrocardiographic Examination**
L1406: There were no reported test article-related ECG findings at all dose levels.
L1407: ## **Organ weights**
L1408: At 1 mg/kg/day, changes in organ weights were reported in the brain, lung, heart, liver, pituitary, thyroid and thymus. The increase in relative brain weight was correlated with microscopic changes in the brain. The significant decrease in the relative thymus weight at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day were correlated to the microscopic observation of thymic lymphocytic depletion, and as such were considered to be test article-related. The changes in lung, heart, liver, pituitary, thyroid relative organ weights at 1 mg/kg/day were not correlated with microscopic findings, therefore, toxicologic relevance is uncertain.

L1412: ## **Macroscopic observations**
L1413: Test article-related macroscopic observations were reported only at the interim sacrifice (1 mg/kg/day) and consisted of dark thyroid, dark adrenals, distended gallbladder and thickened gastric mucosa and/or dark areas on the gastric mucosa in the stomach of males. The findings in the thyroid and thymus (females) were evident at the end of the recovery period.
L1414: ## **Microscopic findings**
L1415: Test article-related histopathological changes were reported in multiple organs at the 1 mg/kg/day interim-sacrifice animals (3 monkeys/sex) and interim-recovery (2/sex). These findings consisted of the following predominant effects (#/# indicates incidences in male/female):
L1416: - Femur and tibia bone: severe atrophy of the primary spongiosa (3/3) and moderate hypertrophy of the physis in the distal femur (2/2) and the tibia (1/1) in males and females and slight hypertrophy of the femoral articular cartilage in males (2/0).
- Gastric changes in males (marked dilated gastric glands (3/2), marked hemorrhage (2/0) and edema (1/0), slight acute inflammation (1/0), moderate hyperplasia (3/0) of fundic and pyloric columnar epithelium and moderate decreased numbers of parietal and chief cells (3/0). One male also had slight arteriopathy (1/0) characterized by medial hyaline necrosis and proliferation of the vascular wall of arterioles and small muscular arteries in the submucosa of the stomach (1/0),
L1417: duodenum (1/0), and jejunum (1/0). The only finding in two females was slight dilatation of gastric glands.

- Ovary and corpora lutea: no maturing follicles were present (0) nor were active corpora lutea present (3). In the control group all animals (3) had developing follicles and active corpus lutea. Moderate calcareous bodies (3) and marked hyalinized corpora lutea were evident (3).
- Adrenal cortex: moderate degeneration of the adrenal cortex in males and, to a lesser degree, females (moderate cytoplasmic eosinophilia (3/1) and moderate decreased vacuolization (3/1), slight apoptosis of zona glomerulosa (1/1), and focal slight hemorrhage /congestion (2/0).
- Pancreas: Decreased pancreatic zymogen granules in 3 males (moderate) and 2 females (slight).
- Lymphoid depletion of lymphoid organs (spleen (2/1), thymus (3/1) and lymph nodes (3/0) in males and females (marked). The reported lymphoid depletion may be stress-related and not direct test article related effect.
- Brain: slight choroid plexus mononuclear infiltrates (3/3), thrombi (0/1), hemorrhage (2/1), thickening of vessel walls (1/2) and pigment deposition (1/1) in the cerebral ventricular choroid in males and females.
The following findings were considered less pronounced effects due to lower incidence and/or severity:

- Liver: Bile duct hyperplasia (2/1).
- Esophagus: slight muscle acute inflammation (1/0) and hyperkeratosis (2/0) in male.
- Gallbladder: subacute inflammation (0/1), hyperplasia (1/1), eosinophil infiltrate (0/1) of the gallbladder of males and females.
- Rectum: slight cell debris in rectal glands of 3 males.
- Heart: subacute inflammation of the epicardial vessel and the perivascular in the heart of females (0/1).
L1418: **Recovery (interim):** The majority of the microscopic findings were recoverable after a 2-week recovery period or showed a decrease in incidence/severity. Findings were reported in the brain (slight mononuclear cell infiltrate (2/0), pigment (2/1) and accumulation of vacuolated cells(1/0)), liver (inflammation of the foci (1/0), bile duct hyperplasia (2/1), stomach (slight cell debris and mucus, glands (1/0)), thymus (slight depletion, lymphoid (0/1)) and ovary (slight hyalinized corpora lutea (2) although maturing follicles were evident) in females.
L1419: Test article-related microscopic changes were reported in the terminal sacrifice animals (3/sex/group) at doses of 0.1 and 0.3 mg/kg/day:
L1420: The following effects were pronounced and or occurred at a high incidence:
L1421: - Femur and tibia bone: marked (male) and moderate (female) atrophy of the primary spongiosa of the metaphysis of the femur (2/1), tibia (1/2) and fibula (1/0) of animals at 0.3 mg/kg/day.
- Gastric changes: minimal stomach hemorrhage (1/0), slight duodenum arteriopathy (1/0) and enteropathy (1/0) at 0.3 mg/kg/day.
L1422: - Ovary and corpora lutea: moderate increase in atretic follicles was evident (2) and lack of active corpora lutea (0) at 0.3 mg/kg/day. Maturing follicles were evident at doses \le 0.3 mg/kg/day.
- Adrenal cortex degeneration: moderately increase cytoplasmic eosinophilia (3/0), moderately decreased vacuolization at 0.3 mg/kg/day (3/2 -minimal) and one female (minimal) at 0.1 mg/kg/day.
- Pancreas: slight decrease in pancreatic zymogen granules in male (3/2).
- Lymphoid depletion in the thymus (moderate 2/0), spleen and mandibular lymph node (moderate 2/0) of males at 0.3 mg/kg/day. The reported lymphoid depletion may be stress-related and not direct test article related effect.
- Brain: minimal mononuclear cell infiltrates (3/3), minimal thickening of the vessel walls (2/2) and slight accumulation of vacuolated cells in the choroid plexus (0/1) in animals at 0.3 mg/kg/day and mononuclear infiltrates in the choroid plexus (1/0) of a male at 0.1 mg/kg/day.
L1423: The following findings were considered less pronounced effects due to lower incidence and/or severity:
L1424: - Mandibular salivary gland: slight (male) and minimal (female) decrease in cytoplasmic granules (2/1) at 0.3 mg/kg/day.
- Heart: moderate (male #s I55542 and I55543) and slight (female) coronary groove fat atrophy (2/1). Atrophy of fat in the coronary groove of the heart finding can be attributed to the severe body weight loss reported at 0.3 mg/kg/day relative to the control group (male monkey #s I55542 and I55543 had the greatest weight loss 0.4 kg and 0.7 kg, respectively; relative to other animal at 0.3 mg/kg/day group (day 1-92))
L1425: **Recovery:** All of the findings were recoverable during the 5-week recovery period.
L1426: No test article-related microscopic findings reported at doses \le 0.03 mg/kg/day.
L1427: ## Toxicokinetics
L1428: - Exposure to tivozanib hydrochloride increased with dose at all collection days in both sexes.
- The increases in C_{max} and AUC for both sexes were generally less than dose proportional on day 28 and 91 (0.01 to 1 mg/kg/day). On day 1, the increases in C_{max} and AUC were approximately dose proportional, with the exception of the values at 1 mg/kg/day which were greater than dose proportional. At doses of 0.03 to 1 mg/kg/day, the increase in C_{max} and AUC were approximately dose proportional on all collection days.
- Mean concentrations were higher after repeat dosing at doses \le 0.3 mg/kg/day, while concentrations were similar after repeat dosing at 1 mg/kg/day.
- T_{max} values on day 1, 28 and 91 ranged from 2.6-6.7 hours, 3.3-8 hours and 2-4 hours, respectively.
- No significant (&gt;2 fold) gender differences were reported.
L1429: — C_{max} and AUC values were higher (1.1-2.9 and 1.3 to 3.8 times, respectively) on day 28 and 91 than day 1.

— Mean elimination half life (t_{1/2}) was similar across all dose levels for both sexes on all blood collection days. Elimination half-life (t_{1/2}) ranged from 7.7 to 9.2 hours.

L1433: ## **Conclusion:**
L1434: The NOAEL level after 13 weeks of dosing was 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED = 1.95 mg/day). Findings after at least 2 weeks of recovery in animals given 1.0 mg/kg/day and after at least 5 weeks of recovery in animals given 0.3 mg/kg/day indicated either full or partial recoverability.
L1435: Study title: AV-961: A 39-Week Oral Toxicity Study in Cynomolgus Monkeys With a 6-Week Recovery Period
Study no.:	1458-006
Sponsor:	AVEO Pharmaceuticals, Inc.
Cambridge, Massachusetts 02139
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)

L1436: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L1437: ## *Key Findings*
L1438: - One female at 0.03 mg/kg/day was found dead on day 37 due to gavage error. An additional male was found dead at 0.1 mg/kg/day on day 173. The cause of death was not considered to be test article-related due to the lack of toxicity/morbidity in any other animal in that group (dose longer), or any other animal receiving much higher doses in the dose range-finding study.
- No test article-related effects were reported on all end points evaluated on this study at any dose level.
- NOAEL was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (1.3-fold the maximum recommended human dose).

L1444: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L1445: Endpoints evaluated	Mortality, clinical observations, cage side observations, ophthalmic examinations, body weight and food consumption.
ECG was performed pre-test and 1 hour post-dose during week 39 and at the end of the recovery period. Additional ECG examination was performed pre-dose and 1 hour post-dose during week 29 for all animals at the control and 0.1 mg/kg/day groups.
Echocardiographic examinations were performed on all animals at the control and 0.1 mg/kg/day groups during week 29.
Clinical pathology (hematology, clinical chemistry and urine) evaluations were performed pre-test, weeks 13 and 29, from the control and 0.1 mg/kg/day animals only and prior to the terminal and recovery necropsies. Blood samples for evaluation for CPK, Troponin I and brain natriuretic peptide (BNP) were collected during week 29 from all surviving animals at the control and 0.1 mg/kg/day groups.
Pathology evaluations (macro and microscopic) and organ weights. No histopathology peer review was performed.

Termination and necropsy:	All surviving animals at the scheduled terminal and recovery necropsies. Histopathology evaluations were performed at all dose levels.
Deviation from study protocol:	None of the deviations affected the integrity or interpretability of the data.
L1446: ## *Results:*
L1447: ## *Analysis of dosing formulations*
L1448: - **Stability:** Samples collected from 0.01 and 0.1 mg/kg/day formulations from weeks 1 and 12 preparations were maintained in refrigerated conditions for 7 and 14 days. Results of the 7 and 14 day stability at both concentrations from week 1 preparation were outside the \pm 15\% acceptance criteria.The 7 and 14 day stability results for samples at 1.0 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} were 136.6% and 144.1% of targeted concentration. The 7 and 14 day stability results for samples at 10.0 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} were 115.2% and 117.7% of targeted concentration, respectively.
- Results of the 7 and 14 day stability from week 12 at both concentrations were within the \pm 15\% acceptance criteria:
- **Homogeneity:** Analysis of samples collected from 0.01 and 0.1 mg/kg/day week 1 formulation indicated that samples were homogenous.
- **Concentration:** Analysis of samples collected during weeks 1 through 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 39, 40, and 44 indicated that formulations were within the targeted concentration range (\pm 15\%). Exceptions were week 8 for 0.01 mg/kg/day (115.4% - backup samples); week 28 for 0.1 mg/kg/day (137.2%) and week 40 for 0.03 mg/kg/day (117.0%).
L1449: ## *Mortality*
L1450: - One female at 0.03 mg/kg/day (animal number 135) was found dead on day 37. Microscopic findings indicated that the primary cause of death was due to a gavage error with secondary pneumonia, subsequent septicemia and abdominal involvement. Microscopic findings provided support for this assumption.
- One male (animal number 138) at 0.1 mg/kg/day was found dead on day 173. No macroscopic or microscopic findings were reported that would indicate the cause of death. There was no evidence of gavage error reported. The reported microscopic findings were minimal infiltration of mononuclear cells in the heart, kidney and liver; minimal pigmented alveolar macrophages around bronchioles in the lung and unilateral congestion of cortical sinusoids of the adrenal glands.
L1451: The death of the male at 0.1 mg/kg/day was not considered to be test article-related for the following reasons:
L1452: - Follow up studies were performed (troponin I, CK, CPK and BMP, blood pressure, ECGs and echocardiographic evaluation) on the remaining surviving animals to understand the causes of death. The findings did not provide any additional insight regarding the cause of the reported death.
- Higher doses (0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day) administered in prior 13 week study (Study # 6691-159) showed no mortalities.
- No other study animal was reported to show severe signs of toxicity, even after 273 days of dosing.

## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

**Clinical Chemistry:** There were no reported test article-related clinical chemistry changes at all dose levels.

L1469: There were no reported test article-related macroscopic findings at all dose levels.
L1470: ## **Organ weights**
L1471: There were no reported test article-related organ weight changes at all dose levels. Although decreases in heart weights in males and females were noted, they were not associated with macro-microscopic findings, ECG or echocardiographic findings.
L1472: ## **Microscopic Observations**
L1473: There were no reported test article-related microscopic findings.
L1474: ## **Toxicokinetics**
L1475: - Males and female TK parameters were not different on days 1, 91 and 273, indicating no gender effects.
- AUC_{0-24}, AUC_{0-\text{last}} and C_{\text{max}} increased in approximate proportion to dose on days 1, 91 and 273, suggesting that systemic exposure was linear.
- Mean AUC_{0-24}, AUC_{0-\text{last}} and C_{\text{max}} were 0.823 to 1.57 times higher on days 91 and 273 than on day 1. The similarity in AUC_{0-24}, AUC_{0-\text{last}} and C_{\text{max}} on days 91 and 273 suggests that steady-state levels were achieved by day 91, and that no further accumulation occurred beyond day 91.
- T_{\text{max}} values were longer on day 1 in comparison to Days 91 and 273. Mean T_{\text{max}} on day 1 ranged from 3.60 to 4.40 hours and from 2.10 to 2.67 hours on Days 91 and 273.
- Half life (t_{1/2}) was unaffected by dose level or duration of treatment. Elimination half-life (t_{1/2}) ranged from 9.6 to 12.4 hours.

L1478: (excerpted from Applicant's report)
L1479: ## Conclusion:
L1480: The test article was well tolerated at all dose levels. Although one male was found dead at 0.1 mg/kg/day on day 173, the cause of death was not considered by the Applicant to be test article-related. The NOAEL was established to be the highest dose tested 0.1 mg/kg/day (1.3-fold the maximum recommended human dose). As such, the doses in this study were insufficient to adequately establish a dose response, and this study does not provide adequate information regarding potential toxicity associated with AV-961 following repeated oral administration for 39 weeks. Accordingly, the Applicant proposed to rely on the above GLP-compliant 13-week study to inform the package insert regarding toxicities in monkeys.
L1481: ## 7 Genetic Toxicology
L1482: ## Overall Summary:
L1483: - KRN951 was not considered mutagenic in the *in vitro* reverse mutation assay (Ames)
- KRN951 was not considered clastogenic in the *in vitro* mammalian cell cytogenetic test. In the absence of S9 mix, extended exposure to KRN951 produced changes in chromosomal number (polyploidy and hyperdiploidy) at \ge 2 \mu\text{g/mL} (1.2-fold the RCC patient exposure at the recommended clinical dose).
- KRN951 was not considered clastogenic or aneugenic in the *in vivo* clastogenicity assay in the rodent.

L1502: ## 7.2 *In Vitro Assays in Mammalian Cells*
L1503: Study title: In Vitro Mammalian Cell Cytogenetic Test: Chinese Hamster Ovary Cells	
Study no.:	RCK0002
Sponsor:	Kirin Brewery Co. Ltd., Japan
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
Date of study initiation:	17 February, 2003
GLP compliance/QA statement:	UK GLPs and OECD GLPs
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	KRN951, Lot # CDM-001 and purity 98.8%
L1504: ## **Key Findings**
L1505: KRN951 was not considered to be clastogenic under the conditions tested. In the absence of S9 mix, extended exposure produced changes in chromosomal number (polyploidy and
L1506: hyperdiploidy) at \ge 2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} (1.2-fold the RCC patient exposure at the maximum recommended clinical dose).
L1507: ## Methods
L1508: Cell line: CHO cells
L1509: Exposure and concentration in the dose-range study: Exposure was for 3 hours period and harvesting was approximately 1.5 cell cycles after initiation of exposure. The dose levels that were tested and sent for cytogenetic analysis were:
L1510: - 50, 500 and 1000 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} in the absence of S-9 mix (the maximum dose 1000 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} was limited by osmolality).
- 10, 100, 500 and 1000 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} tested in the presence of S-9 mix. (the maximum dose 1000 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} was limited by toxicity).
- 10, 100 and 500 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} were analyzed for concentration verification. The doses in the dose-range study were limited by the osmolality of the compound at 2000 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}.
- There were no changes in pH or other osmolality of the culture medium that impacted the outcome of the study.
L1511: Exposure and Concentrations in definitive study: Exposure was for 3 hours in the presence of S9 and 1.5 cell cycles in the absence of S9 mix. Two harvest times were used: 1.5 cell cycles (harvest 1) post initiation of treatment and 24 hours later (harvest 2).
L1512: The dose levels that were tested and sent for cytogenetic analysis were: 0.5 and 2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} at harvest 1 and 0.5, 2, 5 and 10 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} at harvest 2 in the absence of S-9 (the maximum dose 10 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} was limited by toxicity); and doses of 10, 100 and 500 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} at harvest 1 (the maximum dose 500 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} was limited by toxicity) and 10 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} at harvest 2 in the presence of S-9 mix. Dose of 10 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} in the absence of S-9 was limited by toxicity.
L1513: Evaluation: Following treatment, cells were harvested based on the schedule listed above. Metaphase spreads were prepared and evaluated for chromosomal damage.
L1514: Positive control: Mitomycin C in the absence of S-9 mix; and Benzo(a)Pyrene and Cyclophosphamide in the presence of S-9 mix.
L1515: Formulation/Vehicle: DMSO
L1516: ## Results:
L1517: - Samples obtained for concentration verification analysis were within the acceptable limits of \pm 10\% error (90% to 110% of nominal concentrations) with one exception at 100 \text{mg}/\text{mL} (concentration was 89.3%).
L1518: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L1519: - There was an increase in osmolality greater than 50 mOsm/kg at 2000 µg/mL, as compared to the control group, therefore, this dose level was not used in the definitive studies.
- The maximum level of KRN951 tested in the absence of S-9 mix was 1000 µg/mL in the dose-range study (limited by osmolality) and 10 µg/mL in the definitive study (limited by toxicity). The maximum level of KRN951 tested in the presence of S-9 mix, was 1000 µg/mL in the dose-range study and 500 µg/mL in the definitive study (both limited by toxicity).
- In the dose-range study:The increases in chromosome aberrations in the absence of S-9 at 1000 µg/mL including gaps only, by totals and by frequency, 100 and 10 µg/mL by totals and by frequency, were not considered test article-related, and were attributed to the biological variability of the assay.
- There were no other changes in chromosomal aberrations in the presence or absence of S9 at any other dose level.
- In the definitive study:No test article-related changes in the number of chromosome aberrations in the presence or absence of S-9 were reported after harvest 1 and 2.
- Due to the lack of metaphases in the absence of S9 at harvest 1 two out of the 4 doses were scored (0.5 and 2 µg/mL). Doses of 5 and 10 µg/mL were not scored.
- Treatment with KRN951 for the longer exposure period (which provided acceptable metaphases) in the absence of S-9 mix, provided evidence of interference with the cell cycle, as demonstrated by an increase in numerical aberrations (polyploidy and hyperdiploidy) at ≥ 2 µg/mL (1.2-fold the RCC patient exposure at the maximum recommended clinical dose).

L1585: ## 8 Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L1586: ## *Overall Summary:*
L1587: In the dose range (at 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/day) and definitive fertility and reproduction toxicity studies (at 0.01, 0.03 or 0.1 mg/kg/day), KRN951 was administered orally by gavage to male rats for up to 28 days before mating, during mating (1 to 14 days) and up to termination (approximately 49 total doses) and to female rats for up to 21 days prior to mating, through mating and on Gestation Days (GD) 0 to 7.
L1588: At 1 mg/kg/day, mortalities were evident in both sexes. Clinical observations and decreases in body weights were reported in both sexes at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day. A decrease in food consumption was reported in males at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day and in females at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day. At 1 mg/kg/day, mating was affected as evident by the increase in number of days in cohabitation, and decrease in number of male and female rats that mated. Doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day affected the fertility index (number of pregnancies per number of rats mated) as evident by the decrease in number of rats pregnant per rats in cohabitation.
L1589: Relative epididymal weights were increased at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day by up to 24%, and relative testis weights were increased by 19% at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day. There were no effects on gonadal function or pregnancy at doses \le 0.03 mg/kg/day, while embryo lethality was evident at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day. The NOAEL for mating and fertility was established at 0.1 mg/kg/day (0.64-fold the human maximum recommended clinical dose) and the NOAEL for male systemic toxicity was established to be 0.03 mg/kg/day (0.19-fold the recommended human clinical dose).
L1590: In the embryo-fetal developmental study in rats, KRN951 at doses of 0.003, 0.01, 0.03 or 0.09 mg/kg/day was administered orally by gavage throughout organogenesis. Maternal toxicity (decreases in body weight gain) was evident at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day. Increases in the mean number of dams with resorptions and in the percent of resorbed conceptuses per litter were evident at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day. Significant increases in fetal external malformations (body
L1591: edema, short tail and kinked tail) were evident at 0.09 mg/kg/day (0.58-fold the maximum human recommended clinical dose), and skeletal developmental delays were reported at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day (0.19-fold the maximum human recommended clinical dose). A NOAEL for maternal and embryo-fetal toxicity was established to be 0.01 mg/kg/day (0.06-fold the maximum human recommended clinical dose).
L1592: In the rabbit embryo-fetal developmental study, pregnant rabbits were dosed (0.01, 0.1 or 1 mg/kg/day) throughout organogenesis. There was no reported maternal toxicity at any dose. Embryo-fetal toxicity was evident at 1 mg/kg/day (VSD, constricted pulmonary artery vessels and distended aorta). The NOAEL for maternal toxicity was established to be greater than 1 mg/kg/day (13-fold the maximum recommended human clinical dose), while the NOAEL for embryo-fetal toxicity was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (1.3-fold the maximum recommended human clinical dose).
L1593: ## 8.1 Fertility and general reproduction toxicity studies
L1594: Study title:
Oral (Gavage) dose-range fertility and general reproduction toxicity study of KRN951 in rats
Study no.:	311-007P
Study report location:	eCTD 4.2.3.5.1
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
Date of study initiation:	09 May 2003
GLP compliance:	Yes
QA Statement	Yes
Drug, lot #:	KRN951, CDM-001
L1595: ## Key findings:
L1596: - At 1 mg/kg/day, test-article administration resulted in mortalities in both sexes.
- Doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day resulted in clinical observations, decreases in body weights and food consumption.
- At 1 mg/kg/day, test-article administration resulted in effects on mating as evident by the increase in number of days in cohabitation and decrease in number of male and female rats that mated.
- Doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day resulted in effects on fertility as evident by the decrease in number of pregnancies per number of rats mated and number of rats pregnant per rats in cohabitation.
- Doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day resulted in an increase in epididymis weight (up to 24%) and at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day testis weight increased by up to 19%, respectively.
- Doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, resulted in an increase in nonviable embryos per litter and number of dams with nonviable embryos (embryo lethality).
L1597: ## **Objectives:**
L1598: To provide information for the selection of dosages to be used in the fertility and general reproduction toxicity study of KRN951 administered orally via gavage to Crl:CD®(SD)IGS BR VAF/Plus® male and female rats.
L1599: ## **Methods:**
L1600: - Species and sex: male and female rats
- Strain: Crl:CD® (SD)IGS BR VAF/Plus®
- Supplier: [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L1601: ## **Study design:**
L1602: Dose (mg/kg/day)	Dose Volume (ml/kg)	Number of Rats/sex
Group 1	Vehicle	10	8
Group 2	0.03	10	8
Group 3	0.1	10	8
Group 4	0.3	10	8

L1603: - *Vehicle:* Aqueous 0.5% w/v Methylcellulose.
- Suspension of Test-Article, KRN951 was prepared weekly.
- Analysis: Concentration and homogeneity were evaluated on study. Stability data for the prepared formulation bracketing the range of concentrations used in study were provided by the Applicant.
- Route: Oral gavage.
- Dosing:Males - daily for 21 days prior to cohabitation and through the day before sacrifice. Maximum doses administered: 42 doses.
- Females - 21 days before cohabitation and through Gestation Day (GD) 7. Maximum doses administered: 42 doses.
- Cohabitation period:Treated male were cohabited with treated females from the same dose group for 14 days. Female rats not mated within the first 14 days of cohabitation were assigned alternate male rats (same dosage group) that had mated and remained in cohabitation for a maximum of five additional days.
- Female rats with spermatozoa observed in a smear of the vaginal contents and/or a copulatory plug *in situ* were considered to be GD 0.
- Cesarean section (C-section): GD 13.
- In-life endpoints:Mortalities and clinical observations.
- Daily post-dose observations (1 hour and 4 hours).
- Body weight – weekly during the pre-dose period and daily during the dosing period and post-dosing period and at necropsy.
L1604: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L1605: - Food consumption – weekly during the dosing period (males) except during the cohabitation period. For females weekly to cohabitation and on GD 0, 7, 8, and 13.
- Estrous cycling was evaluated (vaginal cytology) during the cohabitation period until spermatozoa were observed in a smear of the vaginal contents and/or a copulatory plug was observed *in situ*.
- Males were sacrificed and necropsied after cohabitation following at least 42 days of dosing. Male reproductive organs were weighed and retained for possible histological evaluation.
- Females were sacrificed on GD13 followed by C-section and gross necropsy. Number of corpora lutea was recorded. Uterus was examined for implantation sites and viable/nonviable embryos.
L1606: ## **Results:**
L1607: ## Analysis of test formulations:
L1608: Concentration (\pm 15\%) of the test-article in the formulations were within the acceptable criteria with the exception of deviations in group 4 and 5 (0.03 mg/mL and 1.0 mg/mL) for formulations prepared on 5/14/2003 (+39.5% and +24.2%, respectively) and group 3 (0.003 mg/mL) and 5 (0.1 mg/mL) for formulations prepared on 6/30/2003 (-17.6% and -17.1%, respectively). The formulation was stable for up to 15 days after preparation at (4°C) and for at least one day at room temperature.
L1609: Homogeneity (\le 5\% RSD) of the test-article in the formulations was within the acceptable criteria with the exception of deviations on 5/14/2003 in group 4 and 5 (+10.3% and +15.2%, respectively).
L1610: ## **Mortality and clinical observations (male and female):**
L1611: Found dead and moribund sacrificed were reported only at 1 mg/kg/day in males and females:
L1612: ## **Mortalities:**
L1613: Dose (mg/kg/day)	Found dead	Moribund sacrificed
