L1615: Clinical observations were reported prior to death or sacrifice, and included dehydration, chromorhinorrhea, chromodacryorrhea, cold to touch, lacrimation, decreased motor activity, pale extremities, miosis, emaciation, hyperpnea, ungroomed coat, urine-stained abdominal fur, mucoid feces, soft feces, liquid feces, scant feces, no feces in the cage pan, hunched posture, emaciation, impaired righting reflex and/or labored breathing.
L1616: Surviving male and female rats to scheduled termination at 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day, exhibited adverse clinical signs that included:
L1617: ungroomed coat, dehydration, chromorhinorrhea, soft or liquid feces, chromodacryorrhea and lacrimation. Other clinical signs reported only at 1 mg/kg/day were scant feces, urine-stained abdominal fur, decreased motor activity, cold to touch, pale extremities, hunched posture, emaciation, impaired righting reflex, brown or red per-oral substance, ptosis, hyperpnea, labored breathing, mucoid feces and/or no feces in cage pan.
L1618: ## ***Body weight (male and female):***
L1619: - At 1 mg/kg/day, a decrease ranging between 6% to 43%, in male mean body weight was reported during SD 11 to 42. The severity of the effect was more pronounced at latter study days.
- Decreases in male body weight gains were reported at 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day by up to 20%, 36%, 50% and 252%, respectively.
- At 1 mg/kg/day, a decrease in female mean body weight (up to 8%) was reported during study days 18 to 21 (pre-cohabitation period) and a decrease in body weight gain (up to 101%) during the entire pre-cohabitation period.
L1620: ## ***Food consumption (male and female):***
L1621: - Male mean absolute and relative feed consumption values were reduced at 0.1, 0.3 and 1.0 mg/kg/day by up to 6%, 8% and 39% during the entire study period.
- Female mean absolute and relative feed consumption values were reduced at 0.3 and 1.0 mg/kg/day during the entire pre-cohabitation period (study day 1 to 21) by 10% and 24%, respectively.
L1622: ## ***Mating and fertility:***
L1623: ## ***Males***
L1624: The number of days in cohabitation (number of days required to inseminate) was increased for the male rats at 1.0 mg/kg/day, in comparison with the control group (3.7 days versus 2.7 days, respectively). The number of male rats that mated (83.3%) was also reduced at 1.0 mg/kg/day, compared to the control group (100%). The fertility index (number of pregnancies per number of rats mated) and the number of rats pregnant per rats in cohabitation were 0.0% at 0.3 and 1.0 mg/kg/day.
L1625: ## ***Females***
L1626: The number of days in cohabitation was increased for the female rats at 1.0 mg/kg/day, in comparison with the control group (5.1 days versus 2.7 days, respectively). The number of female rats that mated was also reduced at 1.0 mg/kg/day, in comparison with the control group (71.4% versus 100%, respectively). The fertility index (number of pregnancies per number of rats mated) and the number of rats pregnant per rats in cohabitation were 0.0% (none mated) at 0.3 and 1.0 mg/kg/day dosage groups.
L1627: All mating and fertility parameters for male and female rats were unaffected at \le 0.1 mg/kg/day.
L1628: ## *Male necropsy and organ weights:*
L1629: Several gross lesions were reported at necropsy. At 0.3 mg/kg/day, one male rat had a mass on the right cauda epididymis.
L1630: At 1 mg/kg/day, two male rats had adrenals that appeared large and red (bilateral), kidneys that appeared pale (bilateral), and air-filled stomach; one additional rat had a liver mass.
L1631: Relative epididymis weights were increased 13%, 18%, 24% and 19% at 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day, respectively. Relative testis weights were increased by 10% and 19% at 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day.
L1632: ## *Female Cesarean section and necropsy:*
L1633: No gross lesions were reported at necropsy of rats survived to C-section.
L1634: None of the surviving female rats at either the 0.3 or 1.0 mg/kg/day were found pregnant.
L1635: At 0.1 mg/kg/day, an increases in the number of dams with any nonviable embryos (62.5% vs 28.6% at the control, respectively) and percent nonviable embryos per litter (5.8% vs. 2.8% at the control, respectively) were reported.
L1636: ## **Conclusion:**
L1637: — KRN951 (0.03, 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/day) was administered orally by gavage to male rats for 21 days before mating, during mating (1 to 14 days) and up to termination and to female rats for 21 days prior to mating, through mating and on GD 0 to 7. Doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day resulted in mortalities, clinical observations, decreases in body weights, decrease in food consumption, reduced mating and fertility. Relative epididymis weights were increased at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day and relative testis weights were increased at 0.3 mg/kg/day. An increase in nonviable embryos per litter and number of dams with nonviable embryos was evident at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day.
L1638: ## **Study title:**
L1639: Oral (Gavage) fertility and general reproduction toxicity study of KRN951 in rats
L1640: Study no.:	311-007
Study report location:	eCTD 4.2.3.5.1
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
Date of study initiation:	11 August 2003
GLP compliance:	Yes
QA Statement	Yes
Drug, lot #:	KRN951, CDM-001
L1641: ## **Key findings:**
L1642: In males, there were no reported test article-related clinical observations, body weight and food consumption effects at all dose levels. Mating, reproductive organ weights, sperm count and motility were not affected at all dose levels.
L1643: In females, there were no reported test article-related clinical observations and body weight effects at all dose levels. However, relative food consumption was statistically significantly decreased during the gestation period (GD 8-13) at 0.1 mg/kg/day. Estrous cycle and mating incidence, days needed for mating or pregnancy incidence were not affected by the test article treatment. The number of litters with nonviable embryos was not affected by treatment at all dose groups. Placentae appeared normal at all dose groups.
L1644: ## **Objectives:**
L1645: To determine the potential effects of KRN951, when administered orally to male and female rats, on mating, fertility and gonadal function and on pregnancy and early embryonic period.
L1646: ## **Methods:**
L1647: - Species and sex: Male and female rats
- Strain: Crl:CD® (SD)IGS BR VAF/Plus®
- Supplier: (b) (4)
- Age at arrival: Males 63 days; females 63 days
- Weight at study assignment: Males 314-365 g; females 219-286 g
- After the acclimation period and estrous cycle evaluation, female rats were selected for study on the basis of physical appearance, body weights recorded during the predosage period and evidence of normal estrous cycling.
L1648: ## **Study design:**
L1649: Dose (mg/kg/day)	Dose Volume (ml/kg)	Number of Rats/sex
Group 1	Vehicle	10	25
L1650: Group 2	0.01	10	25
Group 3	0.03	10	25
Group 4	0.1	10	25
L1651: - Vehicle: Aqueous 0.5% w/v Methylcellulose.
- Suspension of Test Article, KRN951 was prepared weekly.
- Analysis: Concentration and homogeneity were evaluated on study. Stability data for the prepared formulation bracketing the range of concentrations used in study were provided by the sponsor to the contact laboratory.
- Route: Oral gavage.
- Dosing:Males - Daily for 28 days before cohabitation and through a 14-day cohabitation period, until the day before sacrifice (up to 49 days). Maximum doses administered: 47 doses.
- Females - 15 days before cohabitation through Gestation Day (GD) 7. Maximum doses administered: 47 doses.
- Cohabitation period: Treated male were cohabited with treated females from the same dose group for 14 days. Female rats not mated within the first 14 days of cohabitation were assigned alternate male rats (same dosage group) that had mated, and the pair remained in cohabitation for a maximum of five additional days.
- Female rats with spermatozoa observed in a smear of the vaginal contents and/or a copulatory plug *in situ* were considered to be at GD 0.
- Cesarean section (C-section): GD 13.
- In-life endpoints:Mortalities and clinical observations.
- Daily post-dose observations (1 hour and 4 hours).
- Body weight – weekly during the pre-dose period and daily during the dosing period and post-dosing period (females) and at necropsy.
- Food consumption – weekly during the dosing period (males) except during the cohabitation period. For females weekly to cohabitation and on GD 0, 7, 8, 10 and 13.
- Estrous cycling was evaluated (vaginal cytology) for 14 days pre-dose, for 14 days during dosing and then until spermatozoa was observed in a smear of the vaginal contents and/or a copulatory plug was observed *in situ* during the cohabitation period.
- Males were sacrificed and necropsied after cohabitation. Male reproductive organs were weighed and retained for possible histological evaluation. Sperm concentration and motility were evaluated. Motility was evaluated by the Hamilton Thorne IVOS using samples collected from the left vas deferens. A homogenate was prepared from the left cauda epididymis for evaluation by the Hamilton Thorne IVOS to determine sperm concentration (sperm per gram of tissue weight).
L1652: - Females were sacrificed on GD 13 followed by caesarean section and gross necropsy. Number of corpora lutea was recorded. Uterus was examined for implantation sites and viable and nonviable embryos.
L1653: ## Results:
L1654: ## Analysis of test formulations:
L1655: Concentration (\pm 15\%) and homogeneity (\le 5\% RSD) of the test article in the formulations were within the acceptable criteria with the exception of deviations in group 2 (0.001 mg/mL) for formulations prepared on 10/6/2003. The formulation was stable for up to 15 days after preparation at 4^\circ\text{C} and for at least one day at room temperature.
L1656: ## *Mortality and clinical observations (male and female):*
L1657: All rats survived to scheduled termination. There were no test article-related clinical observations.
L1658: ## *Body weight and food consumption (male and female):*
L1659: There were no reported test article effects on body weights at all dose levels in both sexes. Male food consumption was not affected by the test article treatment at all dose levels, while female relative food consumption was decrease by 6% during GD 8 to 13 at 0.1 mg/kg/day.
L1660: ## *Male mating, necropsy, organ weights, sperm count and motility:*
L1661: There were no test article-related effects reported on mating incidence, days taken for mating or pregnancy incidence. There were no test article-related changes reported during necropsy and no effect on reproductive organ weights. There were no test article-related effects on the number and percent motile sperm, number of non-motile sperm and total sperm count from the vas deferens and caudal epididymal (sperm count and density).
L1662: ## *Female estrous cycle and mating, cesarean section and necropsy:*
L1663: There were no test article-related necropsy observations. There were no test article-related effects on the estrous cycle, mating incidence, days needed for mating or pregnancy incidence. There were no test article-related effects on numbers of corpora lutea, implantations, litter size, viability of embryos at all dose levels. Placentae appeared normal at all dose groups.
L1664: ## Conclusion:
L1665: KRN951 (0.01, 0.03 or 0.1 mg/kg/day) was administered orally by gavage to male rats for 28 days before mating, during mating (1 to 14 days) and up to termination (approximately 49 total doses) and to female rats for 14 days prior to mating, through mating and on GD 0 to 7. There were no effects on mating, fertility, gonadal function, or embryo viability at all dose levels.
L1666: ## 8.2 *Embryo-fetal developmental toxicity studies*
L1667: Study title:	Oral (gavage) developmental toxicity study of KRN951 in rats
Study no.:	311-005
Study report location:	eCTD 4.2.3.5.2
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
Date of study initiation:	11 August 2003
GLP compliance:	Yes
QA Statement	Yes
Drug, lot #:	KRN951, CDM-001
L1668: ## **Key Findings:**
L1669: Maternal toxicity (decreases in body weight gain) was evident at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day. An increase in mean number of dams with resorptions and in percent of resorbed conceptuses per litter was evident at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day. Significant increases in external malformations (body edema, short tail and kinked tail) were evident at 0.09 mg/kg/day, and skeletal developmental delays were reported at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day.
L1670: ## **Objectives:**
L1671: The purpose of the study was to evaluate the adverse effects of KRN951 on Crl:CD® (SD)IGS BR VAF/Plus® presumed pregnant female rats and development of the embryo and fetus consequent to exposure of the dam from implantation to closure of the hard palate. This study was designed to evaluate ICH Harmonised Tripartite Guideline stage C and D of the reproductive process.
L1672: ## **Methods:**
L1673: - Species: Male and Female Rats (males used only for breeding)
- Strain: Crl:CD® (SD)IGS BR VAF/Plus
- Supplier: (b) (4)
- Age at arrival: 62 days
- Weight at study assignment: 218-246 g
L1674: ## **Study design:**
L1675: Dose (mg/kg/day)	Dose Volume (ml/kg)	Number of Rats
Group 1	Vehicle	10	25
Group 2	0.003	10	25
Group 3	0.01	10	25
Group 4	0.03	10	25
Group 5	0.09	10	25
L1676: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L1677: - Vehicle: Aqueous 0.5% w/v Methylcellulose.
- Suspension of KRN951 was prepared weekly.
- Analysis: Concentration and homogeneity were evaluated on study. Stability data for the prepared formulation bracketing the range of concentrations used in study were provided by the sponsor to the contract laboratory.
- Females with evidence of a sperm positive vaginal smear were randomized and assigned to study groups.
- Dosing:Females: Daily from Gestation Day (GD) 7 to 17 (Day 0= Day 1 of gestation).
- Males: were not dosed.
- Route: Oral gavage.
- Cesarean section (C-section): GD 21.
- In-life endpoints:Mortalities and clinical observations.
- Daily post-dose observations (1 hour and 4 hours).
- Body weight - Daily.
- Food consumption - GD 1, 7, 10, 12, 15, 18 and 21.
- Litter endpoints: Uterus weight, number of corpora lutea, number of live fetuses, number of dead fetuses and resorptions.
- Fetal end points: Sex, fetal weights, external, visceral and skeletal examination.
L1678: ## Results:
L1679: ## Analysis of test formulations:
L1680: Concentration and homogeneity of the test article in the formulations were within the acceptable criteria with the exception of deviations in group 2 (0.003 mg/kg/day) for formulations prepared on 4 days (-15.8% to 70.4% for concentration and 33.4% on one day for homogeneity). The formulation was stable for up to 15 days after preparation at (4°C) and for at least one day at room temperature.
L1681: ## Maternal data:
L1682: ## Mortality and clinical observations:
L1683: All rats survived to scheduled termination. There were no treatment-related clinical observations.
L1684: ## Body weight and Food consumption:
L1685: - A decrease in body weight gain was reported at 0.09 mg/kg/day group in comparison to the control group, during GD 15-18 (17%) and during the entire dosing period (11% at GD 7-17). Mean body weights were not affected by the treatment at all dose levels.
- Body weight gain at 0.03 mg/kg/day, was decreased by 8% during the entire dosing period (GD 7-17).
- There was no drug-related effect on mean food consumption at all dose levels.
L1686: ## *Litter data:*
L1687: - Mean number of dams with resorptions at 0.09 mg/kg/day, was reported to be 64% higher than the control group. The reported increases in total resorptions were attributed to the increases in mean number of early resorptions (not statistically significant) at 0.09 mg/kg/day.
- The percent of resorbed conceptuses per litter was higher at 0.03 mg/kg/day (5.3%) and 0.09 mg/kg/day (7.4%), relative to control (3.5%).
- There were no effects on fetal weights.
L1688: ## *Fetal Data:*
L1689: - 100%, 58%, 25% and 48% of the litters at 0.09, 0.03, 0.01, and 0.003 mg/kg/day, respectively, were reported to have fetuses with anatomical abnormalities (malformations and/or variations) in comparison to 8% of the fetuses at the control group.
- The percent of fetuses with any anatomical abnormalities per litter was higher at 0.09 mg/kg/day (70%) in comparison to the control group (0.5%).
- The percent of litters with fetuses with any malformation was higher at 0.09 mg/kg/day (96%) in comparison to the control group (0%). The percent of fetuses with malformation per litter at 0.09 mg/kg/day was 59%, in comparison to 0% at the control group.
- The percent of fetuses with any variations per litter was higher at 0.09 mg/kg/day (11.7%) and 0.03 mg/kg/day (4.2%) in comparison to the control group (0.5%).
- Significant increases in external malformations were reported at 0.09 mg/kg/day and included, body edema, short tail and kinked tail.
- Significant increases in skeletal variations were reported at 0.09 mg/kg/day. The variations were reported to be in the thoracic vertebra (centrum unilateral ossification and centrum bifid), lumber vertebrae (bifid centrum), caudal vertebrae (centrum unilateral ossification), sternal centra (incompletely ossified). At 0.03 mg/kg/day, the only toxicologically important variation report was in the thoracic vertebra (centrum bifid).
- The mean number of ossification sites per fetus per litter (caudal vertebrae, forelimb phalanges, metatarsals and hindlimb phalanges) were decreased at 0.09 mg/kg/day in comparison to the control group. Additionally, a decrease in the number of caudal vertebrae ossification sites per fetus per litter was reported at 0.03 mg/kg/day in comparison to the control group. The decrease in number of ossifications may be an indication of developmental delay.
L1690: ## **Conclusion:**
L1691: In the embryo-fetal developmental study, KRN951 at doses of 0.003, 0.01, 0.03 or 0.09 mg/kg/day was administered orally by gavage to pregnant rats throughout organogenesis. Maternal toxicity was evident at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day. Increases in mean number of dams with resorptions and in percent of resorbed conceptuses per litter were evident at doses \ge 0.03. At 0.09 mg/kg/day, external malformations,
L1692: and fetal developmental delay (skeletal variations) were apparent at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day. NOAEL was established at 0.01 mg/kg/day.
L1693: Title: Oral (Stomach Tube) Dosage-Range Developmental Toxicity Study of KRN951 in Rabbits
Conducting Laboratory:
Date of study initiation: 09 May, 2003
GLP compliance: Yes
QA statement: Yes
Objective and Methods:
The objective of the dose range study was to establish doses for the pivotal embryo-fetal developmental study in rabbits.
Study #: 311-006p
Timed-mated pregnant New Zealand White rabbits (n=24) were randomly assigned to four dose groups (0, 0.01, 0.1 and 1.0 mg/kg/day). Test article and vehicle were administered orally once daily on Gestation Days (GD) 6 through 18. The dosage volume was 10 ml/kg. Viability, clinical observations, body weights, food consumption, corpora lutea, implantations, resorptions, live and dead fetuses, fetal weight, fetal sex and external (all dosage groups), visceral (control and 1 mg/kg/day) and skeletal (control and 1 mg/kg/day) findings were recorded.
Results:
At 1 mg/kg/day, mean body weights and food consumption values were decreased (GD 12 to 29).
At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, mean numbers of early resorptions and the percentage of dead or resorbed conceptuses per litter were increased.
At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, fetal body weights were reduced. Reductions in fetal weights were greatest for male fetuses.
At 1 mg/kg/day, the following fetal external findings were reported: one fetus with meningocele, open eyelids of the right eye. One fetus had an inwardly flexed left forepaw.
At 1 mg/kg/day, the following fetal visceral findings were reported: one fetus had corneal opacity of the right eye. Interventricular septal defect (VSD) was reported in two fetus from two different litters. One of the two
L1694: fetuses with the VSD also had distended aorta and constricted pulmonary artery.At 1 mg/kg/day, external and skeletal variations were reported.
Conclusion
Doses of 0.01, 0.1 and 1 mg/kg/day were recommended for the pivotal study.

L1710: ## *Maternal data:*
L1711: ## *Mortality and clinical observations:*

L1717: ## *Fetal Data:*
L1718: - There were no reported test article-related variations at all dose groups.

L1719: ## **Conclusion:**
L1720: In contrast to the Applicant's conclusion stating that no fetal toxicity was observed at any dose level, this reviewer concludes that teratogenicity was evident at the highest dose assessed, 1 mg/kg/day. The reported visceral malformations at 1 mg/kg/day occurred in one fetus and were: interventricular septal defect (VSD), constricted pulmonary artery vessels and distended aorta. These findings were considered to be test article-related since in the dose-range study (Study number 311-006P) at 1 mg/kg/day dose, two fetuses from two different litters (2615-3 and 2616-2) were reported to have VSD's and fetus 2616-2 showed distended aorta and constricted pulmonary artery vessels.
L1721: Therefore, the NOAEL for embryo-fetal toxicity was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day and the NOAEL for maternal toxicity was established to be greater than 1 mg/kg/day (no effects reported at the highest dose tested).
L1722: ## 9 Special Toxicology Studies
L1723: ## 9.1 Phototoxicity Studies
L1724: Study
#: 311-
003	
Title: Ultraviolet Radiation and Visible Light Absorption Profile Study
GLP: Yes
Objectives: "The purpose of this study was to evaluate the ultraviolet radiation (UVR) and visible light absorption profile of tivozanib hydrochloride".
Methods:
Tivozanib was suspended in 0.5% MC at concentrations of 0, 0.004, 0.04, 0.4, 4 and 40 mg/mL. These dilutions were evaluated spectrophotometrically in duplicate to determine which concentrations provided the best spectra. The test plate was scanned spectrophotometrically from 290 to 700 nm to determine if the vehicle possessed any absorption peaks. Isolation of the major peak was conducted at the 0.4 mg/mL concentration.
Results:
The majority of the light did not go through both suspensions of tivozanib (4 and 40 mg/mL). The 0.4 mg/mL dilution provided an average wave length of 329.5 nm (range 290 and 350 nm) and the highest absorption differential of 2.990. The 0.004 and 0.04 mg/mL were clear, however, the peak isolated at the 0.04 mg/mL concentration showed three peaks at approximately 293, 315 and 328 nm.
Conclusion:
The Applicant reported that the UVR and visible light absorption profile of tivozanib revealed strong absorbance in the UVR range from 290 through 340 nm with minimal absorption at wave lengths above 360. The peak absorbance occurred at approximately 329.5 nm. The peak isolated at the 0.04 mg/mL concentration showed three peaks at approximately 293, 315 and 328 nm.

Study
#: 311-
004	
Title: Single-Dose Phototoxicity Study to Determine the Effects on Eyes and Skin in Pigmented Rats
GLP: Yes
Objectives: "The purpose of this study was to determine the phototoxic effects on the eyes and skin of Long- Evans pigmented rats, after a single oral administration of tivozanib hydrochloride followed by exposures to radiation from a xenon lamp (to simulate sunlight)".
Methods:
Animals: Long- Evans pigmented rats
Study design:

4	300	5+5b	5+5b

(excerpted from Applicant's report)
One lightly and one darkly pigmented skin site was exposed to UVR approximately 4, 24, and 120 hours after formulation administration in rats in Groups 1- 4. In Group 5 rats, the eyes and the 2 skin sites were exposed to UVR approximately 1 hour after administration of 8-MOP.
Rats were examined for general appearance and general skin observations approximately 30 minutes after the completion of UVR exposure and 1, 2 and 3 days after UVR exposure. Rats were sacrificed and eyes were taken for histopathology evaluation.
Results:
A female rat that received 300 mg/kg of tivozanib hydrochloride was found dead after UVR exposure on Day 2 of study.
No test article-related skin toxicity reported at all dose levels tested.
No test article-related ocular phototoxicity reported at all dose levels tested.
There were no test article-related microscopic findings in the cornea, retina, sclera, iris, ciliary body, choroids, lens, aqueous and vitreous chambers, optic nerve and bulbar conjunctiva.
Conclusions:
A single oral administration of tivozanib followed by exposures to radiation from a xenon lamp did not elicit either skin or ocular findings indicative of phototoxicity in rats, at doses up to 300 mg/kg.
L1726: ## 10 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation
L1727: **Pharmacology:** Tivozanib hydrochloride (referred to as KRN951, AV-951 or Tivozanib) is an anticancer agent of the vascular endothelial growth factor receptor (VEGFR) Tyrosine Kinase inhibitor class and is being developed for the treatment of advanced renal cell carcinoma. *In vitro* biochemical and cellular kinase assays demonstrated that tivozanib is an inhibitor of VEGF receptors (VEGFRs). In *in vitro* biochemical kinase assays, tivozanib inhibited the phosphorylation of VEGFRs-1, -2, and -3, with IC_{50} of 30, 6, and 15 nM, respectively, and inhibited other kinases at higher concentrations (IC_{50} ranging from 40 to 618 nM). In *in vitro* cell-based kinase assays, tivozanib inhibited the phosphorylation of VEGFRs-1, -2 and -3, with IC_{50} of 0.21, 0.16 and 0.24 nM (0.107, 0.081 and 0.122 ng/ml), respectively, and inhibited other kinases (C-kit and PDGFR\beta) at higher concentrations (IC_{50} of 1.63 and 1.72 nM, or 0.83 and 0.88 ng/ml, respectively). The VEGFR, C-kit and PDGFR\beta IC_{50} values are less than the C_{min} value achieved in RCC patients (16 to 30.9 ng/mL) following repeat dosing at the recommended dose of 1.5 mg/day, indicating that continuous inhibition of VEGF-mediated angiogenesis and activity of other identified kinases would be occurring during the treatment period.
L1728: In *in vivo* pharmacodynamic studies in mice and rats bearing human or murine xenograft tumors, tivozanib inhibited tumor growth via changes in angiogenesis and vascular permeability. In an oral repeat-dose study in nude rat xenograft models bearing 15 different human cancer cell lines, treatment with tivozanib (0.2 mg/kg/day; HED 1.95 mg/day) resulted in tumor growth inhibition (TGI \ge50%) in 11 out 15 cell line models, and inhibition of intratumor angiogenesis, as was evident by the reduction in intratumor vascular endothelial cell density and vascular permeability. A similar outcome was reported in the repeat-dose studies in nude mice bearing murine breast HER2 xenograft tumors. Tivozanib treatment result in a decrease in viable tumor area, reduction in total number of proliferating cells, decrease in vessel cross-section area, decrease in microvascular density and an increase in tumor hypoxia, as measured by HIF1-\alpha protein. The anti-tumor activity dose (0.2 mg/kg) in the rat model produced a mean C_{max} of 70 ng/ml (simulated serum concentration after repeat dosing) or 108 ng/ml and AUC of 1640 ng*hr/ml following single dose. These plasma levels overlap those achieved in RCC patients following a repeat dosing at the recommended dose of 1.5 mg/day (C_{max} was 67.5 to 94.3 ng/mL and the AUC0-24 was 1180 to 1641 ng*hr/mL, respectively).
L1729: In *safety pharmacology* studies, no effects on respiration rate, tidal volume, neurobehavioral endpoints or physiological parameters (Irwin test) were reported at 0.3 mg/kg in rat studies (HED=2.9 mg/day; 1.9-fold of the recommended human dose). The *in vitro* hERG tail-current study for tivozanib was designed with only a single concentration. As such, a concentration-response curve was not established (per ICHS7B), and the study provided no information regarding IC_{50}. Accordingly, this was not considered a valid study. An *in vivo* telemetry study was performed in male cynomolgus monkeys administered a single oral dose of tivozanib. The highest dose tested, 3 mg/kg (HED=54.4 mg/day; 38.9-fold of the recommended human dose) resulted in increases in systolic, diastolic and mean arterial blood pressure 90 minutes post dose. There were no test article-related effects on heart rates, RR, PR, QT, QTcF, QTcQ intervals and the QRS duration at any dose level. In an *in vivo* repeat-dose telemetry study in male
L1730: cynomolgus monkeys at doses of 1 or 3 mg/kg/day for 28 or 14 days, respectively, increases in systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure were evident at 3 mg/kg/day. As such the results of the single- and repeat-dose telemetry study in monkeys at 3 mg/kg were consistent. At doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day in the monkey, decreases in heart rate, cardiac output, renal arterial blood flow and cardiac arterial blood flow were reported. A decrease in vascular relaxation response and decrease in sensitivity to acetylcholine were also reported at doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day (HED=19.5 mg/day; 13-fold of the recommended human dose), and may have played a role in the reported hypertension response in monkeys. Another possible cause of the increased blood pressure may be related to effects noted in the zona glomerulosa of the adrenal cortex (study # 6691-159; 13-week repeat-dose study in monkeys). Since the zona glomerulosa is responsible for the production of mineralocorticoids (e.g. aldosterone) which play a role in regulating blood pressure and kidney function, changes to this cell population may contribute to the observed physiological changes
L1731: *Pharmacokinetic studies:* In vitro and in vivo ADME studies were performed. Overall the data supported the use of rats and monkey in nonclinical studies, as these species would be expected to provide adequate coverage for potential human metabolites and associated toxicities.
L1732: - Tivozanib TK profile following oral administered was approximately linear in mice, rats and monkeys, with C_{max} and AUC increasing in an approximately dose proportional manner. No significant gender differences in exposure were reported. The rate of absorption was moderate and unaffected by dose level or duration of treatment in rats and monkeys, while, the rate of absorption (T_{max}) and elimination half-life (t_{1/2}) were different between species. The elimination t_{1/2} in rats (9-45 hours) was greater than that reported in monkeys (8-12 hours), but was considerably shorter than that reported for humans (4.5-5.1 days). As such, clinical regimen (QD, 3 weeks on and 1 week off) differs from the daily nonclinical dosing regimen, to account for these differences.*Reviewer Note:* The toxicokinetics (TK) data supporting 10 toxicology studies was compromised due to data integrity issues and therefore are in non-compliance with the GLP regulations. The following reports were amended and data was discarded: 6691-158 (DRZZ1047), RCK0003, RZZ1008, RZZ1009, RZZ1010, RZZ1011, RZZ1012, RZZ1013, RZZ1014, and RZZ1015. As such, the data from these studies cannot be used to support this application, and were not reviewed.
- In *in vitro* distribution studies conducted using animal or human plasma, it was shown that the test article bound to plasma proteins at &gt; 97.6%, and protein binding was predominantly to albumin, with low binding to \alpha-1-acid glycoprotein. KRN951 mainly partitioned into the plasma/serum component of blood. Quantitative data on the tissue distribution of test article-related radioactivity in whole body of rats showed that the distribution of radioactivity was maximal at 4 hours post-dose. The highest radioactivity tissue concentrations were measured in the preputial gland, hardierian gland, white fat and the liver. The concentrations in both the ovary and uterus were similar to the blood
L1733: levels, and those in testes were significantly less than in the blood. The majority of the radiation was eliminated from tissues at 24 hours post-dose, and by 72 hours post-dose the radiation levels were below the limit of quantification. In pigmented rats, melanin binding to the choroid layer of the eye was apparent and levels decreased steadily over 120 hours post-dose.
L1734: - *In vitro* and *in vivo* metabolism studies with KRN951 were performed and showed that in serum, the parent compound (KRN951) was the predominant circulating component (91% and 78% of the total radioactivity exposure in rat and monkey serum, respectively) with the metabolites M4 (mono-oxidation)/M5 (N-oxide of tivozanib) co-eluting and M63 in rats only. The parent compound was mainly detected in feces and only less than 0.5% in urine. In feces, the parent compound was the main component and other metabolites detected represented less than 5% of the administered radioactive dose. *In vitro* metabolism studies showed that KRN951 metabolism was low in all species studied, as was evident by the low hepatic clearance, and that KRN951 appeared to be metabolized mainly by oxidation and demethylation. All prominent human metabolites (M1 through M24) were present in both rat and monkey with the exception of M11 (glucuronide), which was unique to human hepatocytes (minor &gt;0.5%) and M10 which was not observed with rat hepatocytes. M14 was present at &gt;10% of total radioactivity and was designated as a major metabolite in human, monkey and rat. Comparison of the metabolic profile of tivozanib in human, monkey, dog and rat liver microsomes showed that monkey is the closest to human followed by the rat, and dog was determined to be the most different. CYP3A4 and CYP1A1 are the major human hepatic CYP isoform involved in the metabolism of tivozanib.
- In rats and monkeys the main excretion route was through the feces, and to a smaller extent (2-3%) through urine. Excretion through the bile duct was identified in rats.
L1735: *In genetic toxicology studies:* Tivozanib was not considered mutagenic in the *in vitro* reverse mutation assay (Ames) or aneugenic in the *in vivo* clastogenicity assay in rodents. In addition, tivozanib was not considered clastogenic in the *in vitro* mammalian cell cytogenetic test; however, tivozanib produced changes in chromosomal number (polyploidy, hyperdiploidy) at doses \ge 2 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} (1.2-fold the plasma level at the recommended human dose).
L1736: *Impurity qualification:* The potential impurities of the tivozanib drug substance were evaluated and eight out of 39 of the compounds were found to be positive in Ames; these included (b) (4). There were two impurities identified to be above the ICH qualification threshold: (b) (4) in Drug Substance and (b) (4) in Drug Product, but given that levels of these impurities at doses that did not result in severe toxicity in nonclinical studies were higher than the maximum potential doses of these impurities in patients if they were at the levels of the specifications, the impurity specifications were considered qualified by the nonclinical data. The maximum amount of (b) (4)
L1737: a genotoxic impurity, administered to patients will be (b)^{(4)} \mug/day, which is acceptable for the indicated patient population.
L1738: **Reproductive toxicology:** In male and female fertility studies, systemic toxicity was reported (clinical observations and decreases in body weight and food consumption) at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=0.97 mg/day; 0.64-fold of the recommended human dose). At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day mating was affected as evident by the increase in number of days in cohabitation, and decrease in number of male and female rats that mated. Doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day affected the fertility index (number of pregnancies per number of rats mated) as evident by the decrease in number of rats pregnant per rats in cohabitation. As such, the NOAEL was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=0.97 mg/day; 0.43-fold of the recommended human dose). In the embryo-fetal developmental study in rats, tivozanib was administered orally throughout organogenesis. Maternal toxicity, increases in resorptions (early and late), fetal external malformations (body edema, short tail and kinked tail) and skeletal developmental delays were reported at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED=0.29 mg/day; 0.19-fold of the recommended human dose). A NOAEL for maternal and embryo-fetal toxicity was established to be 0.01 mg/kg/day (HED=0.097 mg/day; 0.06-fold of the recommended human dose). In the rabbit embryo-fetal developmental study, pregnant rabbits were dosed orally throughout organogenesis. There was no reported maternal toxicity at any dose. Embryo-fetal toxicity was evident at 1 mg/kg/day (VSD, constricted pulmonary artery vessels and distended aorta). The NOAEL for maternal toxicity was established to be greater than 1 mg/kg/day (HED=19.5 mg/day; 13-fold of the recommended human dose), while the NOAEL for embryo-fetal toxicity was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=0.19 mg/day; 1.3-fold of the recommended human dose).
L1739: **Single-dose toxicology:** The maximum tolerated dose in mice and rats following a single IV administration was established to be greater than the highest dose tested of 30 mg/kg. This dose was established as the maximum feasible dose. Given that the oral route is the proposed clinical route, the studies used to assess the safety of the compound in support of the current NDA application were conducted using the oral route as follows. The MTDs for a single oral dose in mice and rats were 524 mg/kg and 276 mg/kg, respectively (1700 and 1790-fold of the recommended human dose). These doses resulted in body weight decrease, an increases in ALT, AST and ALP, an increase in hematology parameters (red blood cells, hemoglobin, hematocrit, and neutrophils), and decreases in lymphocyte and total white blood cell counts.
L1740: **Repeat-dose toxicology:** Rats were orally dosed with tivozanib for 13 weeks (0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, or 1.0 mg/kg/day) and 26 weeks (0.005, 0.02 or 0.08 mg/kg/day). In the 13-week study, doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day resulted in mortalities and severe clinical observations (clinical signs typically associated with morbidity, malocclusion, missing teeth and white teeth) with effects more pronounced at 1 mg/kg/day, resulting in cessation of dosing at 1 mg/kg/day after 4 weeks of dosing. The malocclusion and missing teeth findings were correlated with microscopic findings in the incisors (degeneration of odontoblasts, ameloblasts and dentin dysplasia) and may have contributed to the body weight and food consumption decreases reported at doses \ge
L1741: 0.03 mg/kg/day. The dental toxicity in rats was not observed in monkeys, likely because this effect is a direct effect on growing teeth. In rats, growth of the incisors is continuous throughout the entire lifespan of the animal, which is different from monkey and human. Therefore, the effect on incisors is unlikely to be relevant to the targeted patient population (adults) for this indication. The reported decreases in body weights and food consumption were accompanied by test article-related changes in clinical pathology parameters. The hematology parameters reported at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day included decreased red blood cell parameters, and at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day included increased white blood cell parameters. Clinical chemistry changes included increase in iron metabolism at doses \ge 0.03 mg/kg/day; decrease in protein parameters and increase in liver parameters at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day; and increases in LDH/creatinine, triglycerides and renal parameters at 1 mg/kg/day. Test article-related urinalysis changes were reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (increased protein); at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day (increased urobilinogen and bilirubin) and at 1 mg/kg/day (increased hyaline casts). Test article-related macroscopic findings and organ weights were reported in multiple organs in both sexes at doses \ge 0.01 mg/kg/day. Macroscopic and microscopic findings were reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day in both sexes. In general, the incidence and/or severity of the findings were dose-dependent. At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day the target organs with significant toxicity observed were adrenal glands, kidney (correlated with an increases in urobilinogen, and other proteins), bone femur, liver (correlated with the increase in the levels of liver parameters such ALT, U GGT, total bilirubin, LDH and triglycerides) and bile duct, brain, pituitary, incisors, pancreas and duodenum. Less pronounced (lower incidence and/or severity) microscopic findings were reported in the heart, reproductive organs, bone marrow, adrenal medulla and stomach. Similar microscopic findings in bone, pancreas and incisors were reported in the 4-week repeat dose study in rats (study # RZZ1013). The NOAEL was considered to be 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED = 0.29 g/day; 0.2-fold of the recommended human dose) based on adverse microscopic findings. Toxicities at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day were severe and for the most part were not recoverable. Partial recovery of most effects was reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, although renal effects were not recoverable at these doses. In the 26-week rat study, findings of white teeth (doses \ge 0.005 mg/kg/day), decrease in body weight, hematology findings (increases in leukocytes, hemoglobin, hematocrit and erythrocytes) and clinical chemistry findings (decreases in total protein, albumin and globulins in both sexes) were reported at 0.08 mg/kg/day. The above observed effects were not considered to be adverse, and no additional test article-related findings were reported by the end of the treatment or the recovery periods. The NOAEL was established to be the highest dose tested, 0.08 mg/kg/day (HED = 0.78g/day; or 0.5-fold the recommended human dose).
L1742: In the repeat-dose toxicology studies, **monkeys** were orally dosed with tivozanib for 13 weeks (0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/day) and 39 weeks (0.005, 0.01, 0.02, 0.03 or 0.08 mg/kg/day). In the 13-week monkey study, a dose of 1.0 mg/kg was considered severally toxic, and therefore, dosing was ceased after approximately 4 weeks of dosing. Doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day resulted in clinical signs and decrease in body weights. Test article-related hematological changes were evident at 1 mg/kg/day (increases in RBC parameters, fibrin and M/E ratio; decreases in WBC, RETIC, NEUT, MCV and PT). Test article-related clinical and urine chemistry changes were evident at 1 mg/kg/day (increases in CK, SELA-\alpha &amp; \beta, iron,
L1743: phospholipids; decreases in ALP, SDH, total bile acid, amylase, UIBC, U LDH, U creatinine). The clinical chemistry changes reported at doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day (increases in SELA-\alpha, CK, and phospholipids) were considered test article-related, but changes were of lesser magnitude. Some of clinical pathology changes were attributed to the reported body weight changes. At doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day, significant decreases in absolute and relative thymus weights was reported. Macroscopic findings were only evident at 1 mg/kg/day. At doses \ge 0.1 mg/kg/day, microscopic findings in the brain and adrenal cortex were reported. The predominant test article-related microscopic findings evident at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day included bone (femur and tibia), gastric system (stomach, duodenum and pancreas), reproductive system (ovary and corpora lutea), and lymphoid depletion of lymphoid organs (thymus, lymph nodes and spleen). Similar microscopic findings in bone, pancreas and thymus were reported in the 4-week repeat dose study in monkeys (study # RZZ1015). The majority of the findings in the 13-week study were recoverable after a 2- or 5-week recovery period. The NOAEL was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED = 1.95 g/day; 1.3-fold of the recommended human dose). In a 39-week monkey study, the highest dose tested (0.1 mg/kg/day) was well tolerated. No test article-related clinical observations were reported. There were no effects on body weight, clinical pathology, echocardiographic, ECG, macro and microscopic findings reported at all dose levels. The NOAEL was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=1.95 g/day; 1.3-fold of the maximum recommended human dose).
L1744: - *Discussion of toxicities:* Of note was that in the monkey study, the ossification findings were limited to bone. Given that teeth do not grow at a constant rate in monkeys, the incisor findings in the rat study were not present in the monkey studies. The growth plate hypertrophy effect on the femur and/or tibia in the rat and monkey is an important finding, and would be a significant safety concern in young/young adult populations, as the epiphyseal plate remains active until the age of ~20 years in the human. Given the targeted age patient population for tivozanib, the bone toxicities may not be relevant. In the monkey, microscopic findings in the adrenal cortex were observed in the zona glomerulosa. This tissue is responsible for the production of mineralocorticoids (e.g. aldosterone) that plays a role in regulating blood pressure and kidney function, and as such may contribute to the blood pressure and renal function changes reported in the cardiovascular safety pharmacology study in monkeys.
L1745: Overall both the 26-week rat and 39-week monkey toxicology studies were insufficient, as the highest dose(s) assessed produced no/minimal toxicity and dose-response relationships could not be evaluated. Given the low safety margin noted for this study in regard to the clinical dose, it is uncertain why the Applicant did not explore a more robust range of doses in these studies. Given the lack of substantial toxicity in these studies, the Applicant uses the data from the 13-week rat and monkey studies to inform the label regarding acute toxicities.
L1746: *Phototoxicity studies:* Ocular and cutaneous phototoxicity studies with tivozanib in rats did not show cutaneous or ocular phototoxicity.

L1751: ## 19.4 OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L1752: As mentioned above, tivozanib has been reviewed by the Office of Clinical Pharmacology under NDA 204408 in 2012-2013. Only two more clinical studies were included in current submission, the dedicated hepatic impairment study AV-951-12-118 and the pivotal study AV-951-15-303 (TIVO-3). Therefore, the current review had an overview of the clinical pharmacology characteristics of tivozanib and focused on the intrinsic factors that may influence exposure.
L1753: ## 19.4.1 Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1754: ## Were relevant metabolite concentrations measured in the clinical pharmacology and biopharmaceutics studies?
L1755: Yes. Unchanged tivozanib is the predominate circulating product in serum with no major active metabolites. Therefore, only tivozanib was measured in the clinical pharmacology and biopharmaceutics studies.
L1756: ## For all moieties measured, is free, bound, or total measured? What is the basis for that decision, if any, and is it appropriate?
L1757: The total plasma concentration of tivozanib was measured in the clinical trials. This was appropriate as tivozanib is greater than 99% plasma protein bound over the clinically relevant concentration range studied.
L1758: ## What bioanalytical methods are used to assess concentrations?
L1759: Method TVZHSP (Validation Report: 8253837) is an LC-MS/MS method for the determination of tivozanib (also known as AV951, AV-951, and KRN951) in human serum using a liquid-liquid extraction method. KRN633 (also known as CDU) was used as the internal standard. Concentrations were calculated with a 1/x linear regression over a concentration range of 0.100
L1760: (LLOQ) to 100 ng/mL (ULOQ) with 9 calibration standards. This method was used in study AV-951-15-303 (also known as TIVO-3; Report 8333396) and study AV-951-12-118.
L1761: (b) (4)
L1762: Method TVZHSP was based on a method developed by
L1763: The method
L1764: was validated at (b) (4) as Method TVZHSP and sample analysis for studies TIVO-3 and AV-951-12-118 were completed in their entirety at (b) (4). Method validation assessments for TVZHSP included precision, accuracy, sensitivity, matrix effect, recovery, carryover, dilution integrity, and the relevant stability parameters. A summary of the results is included in the table below.

L1768: A total of 13 runs were performed using method TVZHSP during sample analysis of TIVO-3. The precision of the QC samples was -2.5 to -0.3% and the accuracy was 97.5 to 99.8%. A total of 23 samples were repeated due to the original result of tivozanib being above the limit of quantitation (100 ng/mL). Samples were diluted up to 1:10, which was within the validated dilution range. Incurred sample reanalysis for tivozanib was performed on 32 samples and passed acceptance criteria since 100% of the ISR samples had a percent difference from the original result of less than 20%.
L1769: A total of 17 runs were performed using method TVZHSP during sample analysis of AV-951-12-118. The precision of the QC samples was 1.5 to 7.3% and the accuracy was 98.9 to 110.8%. A total of 8 samples were repeated due to a positive pre-dose (2/8 samples), re-assay to confirm results (5/8 samples), and pharmacokinetic repeat (1/8 samples). Incurred sample reanalysis for tivozanib was performed on 72 samples and passed acceptance criteria since 99% of the ISR samples had a percent difference from the original result of less than 20%.
L1770: ## 19.4.2 Clinical PK
L1771: Seven clinical pharmacology Phase 1 studies have been completed to characterize the PK of oral tivozanib; six have been completed in healthy or hepatically impaired volunteers (Studies AV-951-10-111, AV-951-10-115, AV-951-11-116, AV-951-11-117, AV-951-12-118 and AV-951-09-109) and one in patients with advanced solid tumors (Study AV-951-10-112). An additional six clinical monotherapy studies conducted in patients with solid tumors also included PK endpoints (Studies AV-951-03-B01, AV-951-08-105, AV-951-07-201, AV-951-10-202, AV-951-09-301, and AV-951-15-303).
L1772: A summary of these trials and their top-line findings is provided in Table 2 below.

LSV: Mar 2011	At C_{max} predicted mean change was 8.3 ms (UCI: 12.6 ms) increase in QTcF duration using the exposure-effect model. Maximum mean change in QTcF was 9.3 ms (90% CI: 5, 13.6) occurring 2.5 hours after dose on Day 21. The central tendency change across all time points was 2.2 ms.
The slope for QTcF was 0.08464 indicating a small exposure-effect relationship, with an estimated QTcF change at C_{max} of 8.2694, (UCI of 12.6493).
The results of the ECG analysis revealed that tivozanib was correlated with a slight reduction in heart rate of no clinical meaningfulness. There was no significant effect on PR interval, a 2.1 ms increase in the QRS duration, and new morphological ST-T changes were noted in a few subjects.
AV-951-10-115	Phase 1, single center, open-label, randomized, two-period crossover food effect study of single doses of tivozanib in healthy volunteers	Healthy male and female subjects aged 18 to 55 years, inclusive; with a BMI range of 18.5 to 31.0 kg/m^{2}, inclusive	Determine the effect of food on the PK of a single dose of 1.5 mg tivozanib in healthy volunteers	30 enrolled (15 per treatment sequence)
Tivozanib 1.5 mg	One of 2 treatment sequences:
1: Treatment A \to Washout \to Treatment B
2: Treatment B \to Washout \to Treatment A
Treatment A: single oral dose of tivozanib after an approximate 10-hour fast
Treatment B: single oral dose of tivozanib following consumption of a standardized FDA high fat breakfast	Completed
FSD: Feb 2011;
LSV: May 2011	An approximately 23% (arithmetic mean) decrease in C_{max} in the fed condition compared to the fasted condition was found. The decrease in C_{max} was statistically significant with the 90% CI for C_{max} was not entirely within the 80% to 125% range, generally considered indicative of bioequivalence. Food did not have an effect on the mean AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-\infty} and the 90% CIs for AUCs were entirely contained within the 80% to 125% range. The half-life, CL/F, and apparent Vz/F were similar between fed and fasted conditions.

L1790: ## **Effect of Renal Impairment**
L1791: No dose adjustment is recommended for patients with renal impairment (creatinine clearance [CLcr] 15-89 mL/min, estimated by Cockcroft-Gault). The basis for this recommendation is listed as follows:
L1792: - The lack of relationship between tivozanib clearance and creatinine clearance. In the PopPK analysis, renal impairment, as quantified by creatinine clearance (CLcr) with median 76.2 mL/min and range from 26.8 to 210 mL/min, did not have a statistically significant effect on the PK of tivozanib.
- A small fraction (11.8%) of the administered tivozanib was excreted in urine.
- The lack of renal function relation AE observed in the clinical trials, including 2 patients with severe renal impairment. The Incidence rate of acute renal failure is low (Grade1-5, 5.2%, grade3-4, 2.3%) in the pivotal study; no increased safety signals were observed when the AE profile for the 2 patients with severe renal impairment were analyzed, and they were both on therapy for about 1.3 years, indicating tivozanib was well tolerated. Renal and urinary disorders data is summarized in table 4 below.
L1793: Given that a higher proportion of patients within the proposed indication, refractory/relapsed renal cell carcinoma, may have severe renal impairment compared to the general population, who can potentially benefit from tivozanib treatment, no dose adjustment is recommended for patients with renal impairment (creatinine clearance [CLcr] 15-89 mL/min, estimated by Cockcroft-Gault).

L1797: ## Effect of Hepatic Impairment
L1798: The effect of hepatic impairment on the PK of tivozanib was assessed in a dedicated hepatic impairment study and by population PK analysis.
L1799: In the dedicated study, the patients were originally classified according to Child-Pugh Scores, there were 8 patients with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A), 8 patients with moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B) and 6 patients with severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh C). Results showed that the exposure to tivozanib in patients with mild hepatic impairment was similar to that in patients with normal hepatic function (GMR of AUC_{0-\infty}: 1.1 ). However, tivozanib was eliminated more slowly in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment. The mean tivozanib exposure (AUC_{0-\infty}) increased 2.2-fold in patients with moderate (Child-Pugh B) hepatic impairment and 3.4-fold in patients with severe (Child-Pugh C) hepatic impairment.

L1801: After re-classifying the patients in the dedicated study by NCI-ODWG criteria, there were 12 patients with mild hepatic impairment, 5 patients with moderate hepatic impairment, 0 patients with severe hepatic impairment. Mean tivozanib exposure (AUC_{0-\infty}) increased 1.79-fold in patients with mild hepatic impairment and 3.49-fold in patients with moderate hepatic impairment. The effect of hepatic impairment on the clearance of tivozanib was larger when
L1802: patients were classified by NCI-ODWG criteria compared to Child-Pugh criteria in the dedicated study.

L1804: Therefore, in the PopPK analysis, NCI-ODWG criteria was used to classify the patients, not only because that dosing recommendation based on NCI-ODWG criteria is more conservative, given the larger fold changes in AUC observed, but also that the majority of the patients were cancer patients. In the whole PopPK dataset, there were 570 patients with normal hepatic function, 83 patients with mild hepatic impairment, 10 patients with moderate hepatic impairment, 0 patients with severe hepatic impairment. The PopPK analysis indicated that mild hepatic impairment did not have an effect on the tivozanib CL/F, while patients with moderate hepatic impairment were found to have a 38.9% lower tivozanib CL/F compared to patients with normal hepatic function or mild hepatic impairment.
L1805: The PopPK analysis, using all data with more patients for both mild hepatic impairment and moderate hepatic impairment, provided a reliable estimate for the hepatic impairment effect, given disease status was not found to be a significant covariate. Therefore, simulations were conducted based on the PK metrics generated from PopPK analysis. Simulation results (figure 1 below) indicated that 0.89 mg once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off in patients with moderate hepatic impairment is estimated to result in tivozanib exposure comparable to that in patients with normal hepatic function treated with 1.34 mg QD tivozanib for 3 weeks on and 1 week off. Therefore, in the absence of safety issues in the clinical trials, 0.89 mg once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off is recommended for patients with moderate hepatic impairment.
L1806: Figure 1, Simulated Tivozanib Cavg During Cycle 2 by Dosing Regimen
L1807: ## 19.4.3 Pharmacometrics Review
L1808: ## Population pharmacokinetics and exposure response analyses
L1809: **Aim:** 1) Incorporate data from AV-951-15-303 into the previously developed PopPK model describing the serum concentration of tivozanib over time; 2) Evaluate the impact of covariates on the PK of tivozanib; 3) Establish ER models describing efficacy (change in tumor size, PFS) and incidence of adverse events (increased blood pressure, HFS) as a function of predicted tivozanib exposure; 4) Evaluate the impact of covariates on the ER of tivozanib.
L1810: **Data:** This population pharmacokinetic (PopPK) and exposure response (ER) analyses is based on tivozanib exposure and clinical efficacy and safety data obtained from seven clinical studies (See Table 36). A total of 4351 tivozanib plasma PK concentration samples obtained from 666 patients were included in the updated PopPK model. Patient characteristics are summarized in Table 35.
L1811: Table 35. Summary of Clinical Studies used in PopPK analyses.

L1819: Model: The reference model was developed without the hepatic impairment study (AV-951-12-118) and consisted of a one-compartment structural pharmacokinetic (PK) model with a transit compartment absorption model (TCAM) and linear elimination. The structure of the model is shown in Figure 4. The model updated and re-evaluated with the dedicated hepatic impairment study (AV-951-12-118). Covariates that were evaluated include weight on V/F and sex, disease status, dose, creatinine clearance (CLcr), hepatic impairment on CL/F. Initially, hepatic impairment was included as a categorical covariate (normal versus mild or moderate impairment). Although the drop in OFV was significant (\Delta\text{OFV} -19), the effect size of mild hepatic impairment was estimated to be approximately 0. The final model includes inter-individual variability (IIV) on all structural parameters, including mean transit time (MTT), apparent clearance (CL/F), and apparent central volume of distribution unadjusted for bioavailability (V/F) and covariates body weight effect on V/F and effects of sex and moderate hepatic impairment on CL/F. The parameter estimates of the final model are in Table 37. The final model was evaluated via Goodness of Fit and Visual Predictive Check plots (see
L1820: Figure 5 and Figure 6). The T_{1/2} of tivozanib was 129 hours and 110 hours in women and men, respectively. Typical CL/F was 0.651 L/h in women and 0.862 L/h in men. Volume of distribution was found to increase nearly proportionally with body weight. Typical value of volume distribution (95% CI) is 123 (121-126) L. Population PK modelling showed slightly high inter individual variability (82.5%, square root of estimated variability in %) in mean transit time (MTT) for absorption, likely due to enterohepatic recirculation of tivozanib, but moderate variability in distribution and elimination parameters (40.5% and 28.1% for CL/F and V1/F, respectively).
L1821: Figure 4. Tivozanib Structural Model.
L1822: CL/F: Apparent clearance; V/F: Apparent volume of distribution; MTT: Mean transit time; NTR: Number of transit compartments; K_{tr}: Absorption transit rate; A_{trx}: Amount in transit compartment x.
L1823: Source: Figure 1 in the PopPK and ER report dated 7/22/2020.

L1834: **Special Populations:** The PopPK analysis indicated that mild hepatic impairment did not have an impact on the tivozanib CL/F, while patients with moderate hepatic impairment were found to have a 38.9% lower tivozanib CL/F. To further check whether the PopPK analysis using all data can capture the feature of the 12 mild and 5 moderate HI patients in dedicated study with respect to CL/F, we summarized the empirical Bayesian estimates of CL/F by studies and HI categories by NCI as in Study. The popPK results for the HI study 118 are consistent with the non-compartment analysis results with respect to hepatic effect on PK. Mild patients in the study AV-951-12-118 (referred as 118) have lower CL than patients in other studies. In addition, the popPK analysis results using 118 study only are also consistent with 118 study using NCA. Overall, the PopPK results are sufficient to make conclusions on HI dosing recommendation.

L1838: To assess hepatic impairment on tivozanib exposure and further support dose adjustment, four dosing regimens, based on either once daily (QD) or once every other day (QOD) dosing, were simulated using the final tivozanib popPK model:
L1839: - Original: 1.5 mg capsule QD for all groups;
- Regimen 1: 1.5 mg capsule QD (normal or mild hepatic impairment) or 1.0 mg capsule QD (moderate hepatic impairment);
- Regimen 2: 1.5 mg capsule QD (normal or mild hepatic impairment) or 1.5 mg capsule QOD (moderate hepatic impairment);
L1840: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
## {FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
L1841: - Regimen 3: 1.5 mg capsule QD (normal or mild hepatic impairment) or 1.0 mg capsule QOD (moderate hepatic impairment - continuous dosing).
L1842: Note that 1.5 mg capsule is equivalent to 1.34 mg tivozanib, 1.0 mg capsule in this figure is equivalent to 0.89 mg tivozanib.
L1843: For all dosing regimens, 2,000 virtual patient profiles per group were simulated and the average concentration (C_{avg}) were summarized by dosing regimen. The simulation results are summarized in Figure 7. Simulated mean tivozanib exposure (AUC_{tau}) increased 1.62-fold in patients with moderate hepatic impairment compared to patient with normal hepatic function or mild hepatic impairment. Simulation results indicated that 0.89 mg QD tivozanib (i.e., 1 mg capsule) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off in patients with moderate hepatic impairment can establish tivozanib exposure comparable to that in patients with normal hepatic function treated with 1.34 mg QD tivozanib for 3 weeks on and 1 week off. Therefore, in the absence of safety concerns, 0.89 mg once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off is recommended for patients with moderate hepatic impairment.

L1847: Renal impairment, as quantified by creatinine clearance (CLcr) with median 76.2 mL/min and range from 26.8 to 210 mL/min, did not have a statistically significant effect on the PK of tivozanib. Therefore, no dose adjustments are required for patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. It is the general expectation that V/F scales linearly with WT. In addition, females were found to have a statistically significant, 25.2% lower CL/F compared to males.
L1848: **Exposure Response Analyses:** Cavg over a four-week treatment period was identified as the best predictor for efficacy compared to other exposure metrics. Cavg in cycle 2 was derived based on individual post-hoc PK parameter estimates from the final PK model. The efficacy endpoints of interest include tumor size and progression free survival (PFS) and the safety endpoint of interest include hand and foot syndrome (HFS) and blood pressure. All ER analyses were performed on data obtained in patients treated with either tivozanib or sorafenib in studies AV-951-09-301 and AV-951-15-303. Only for HFS, data obtained in study AV-951-07-201 was included from patients with exposure metrics.
L1849: ## **Exposure Efficacy Response Analyses:**
L1850: **Tumor Size:** A tumor growth inhibition model was developed to describe the change in tumor size as a function of time, tivozanib exposure, and covariates. Covariates including Cavg, study population (AV-951-09-301 vs. AV-951-15-303), baseline ECOG, sex, prior therapy, and International metastatic renal cell carcinoma database Consortium (IMDC) status were tested via the stepwise covariate modeling (SCM). Effects of Cavg on tumor shrinkage rate (KD), study population on tumor growth rate (KL), and ECOG status on baseline tumor size was identified as the best model describing change in tumor size as a function of time and tivozanib exposure. The model structure is shown in the equation below. The final model parameter estimates are summarized in Table 39. The model was further assessed via goodness of fit and predictive check plots (
L1851: Figure 8).
L1852: \begin{aligned}\frac{dA}{dt} &amp;= K_L \cdot A - K_D \cdot e^{-\lambda t} \cdot A1 \\ K_{D_i} &amp;= \hat{K}_D \cdot (1 + C_{avgK_D} \cdot (C_{avg} - 54.53)) \\ K_{L_i} &amp;= \hat{K}_L \cdot (1 + Refractory_{K_L}) \\ A(t=0)_i &amp;= A(t=0) \cdot (1 + ECOG0_{A(t=0)})\end{aligned}
L1853: Where A represents tumor size at time t. \hat{K}_L represents population tumor growth rate and Refractory_{K_L} represents the proportional change from \hat{K}_L for advanced refractory RCC patients. Similarly, \hat{K}_D represents population tumor shrinkage rate at median C_{avg}, with proportional change of C_{avgK_D} for each unit deviation in C_{avg}. A(t=0) is population baseline tumor size if ECOG=1, and ECOG0_{A(t=0)} represents proportional change in baseline tumor size if ECOG=0.

L1861: Progression Free Survival: Cox proportional hazard models were used to assess the effects of tivozanib exposure and covariates on risk of progressive disease. ECOG, study population, and Cavg which was categorized as quartiles were selected as significant covariates in the final model. The parameter estimates were summarized in Table 40. The model indicates that the highest tivozanib exposure quartile (Q4) relative to the lowest tivozanib exposure quartile (Q1) was highly statistically significant with an estimated relative risk reduction of approximately 33%. Patients with ECOG status 1 had a 59% higher risk relative to patients with ECOG status 0. Similarly, advanced refractory RCC (AV-951-15-303) patients had a 58% higher risk relative to advanced RCC (AV-951-09-301) patients. However, these results should be interpreted with caution as it was based on data at only one dose level.

L1866: ## Exposure Safety Response Analyses:
L1867: Hand and Foot Syndrome: Logistic regression was used to evaluate the influence of tivozanib exposure data in combination with covariates on the odds of experiencing an HFS event. No significant covariate except for Cavg was selected in the final model. **Table 41** shows parameter estimated from the final model and the plot observed HFS occurrences with model predicted probability vs. Tivozanib Cavg. The result demonstrated exposure-dependent increases in rates of hand-foot syndrome (HFS, a.k.a., palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia [PPE]).
L1868: Table 41. Summary of Final Logistic Regression Model of Effect of Tivozanib Exposure on Odds of Hand and Foot Syndrome.
L1869: Source: Table 18 and Figure 24 in the PopPK and ER report dated 7/22/2020.
L1870: Blood Pressure: No exposure-response relationship was observed.
L1871: ## Review Summary
L1872: In general, the applicant's population PK analysis is considered acceptable for the purpose of supporting analyses objectives. The applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
L1873: More specifically, the developed model was used to support the current submission as outlined in **Table 42**.
L1874: Table 42. Specific Comments on Applicant's Final Population PK model
L1875: Utility of the final model	Reviewer's Comments
Support applicant's proposed labeling statements about intrinsic and extrinsic factors	Intrinsic factor	
"For moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B), dose with one 1.0 mg capsule taken orally once daily for 21 days on treatment followed by 7 days off treatment for a 28-day cycle. Closely monitor patient."
"No relationship has been found between tivozanib PK and serum creatinine and creatinine clearance as measurements of renal function."
"Females had a 24.5% lower apparent clearance compared to males independent of body weight. No dose adjustment is required for female patients."
The apparent volume of distribution of tivozanib increases nearly proportionally with body weight."
"Population pharmacokinetic analysis indicated no clinically relevant effects of age or race on the clearance of tivozanib."
For moderate hepatic impairment (NCI), we recommend that "Tivozanib should be used with caution in patients with moderate hepatic impairment and the dose reduced to one 0.89 mg tivozanib."
For the apparent volume distribution, we recommend that "The apparent volume of distribution of tivozanib increases with body weight."
Other statements are acceptable without significant changes.

L1904: Objectives: To determine the pharmacokinetic profile of KN951 administered in a single oral dose to Nude mice.
Methods:
Seven-week old female nude mice
Single dose of KRN951
KRN951 Lot # CDM-003
Oral gavage
Doses of 5 or 20 mg/kg
Blood was taken at 0, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 24, and 48 hours postdose for pharmacokinetic analysis.
Serum samples were analyzed using HPLC-MS/MS.
Endpoints: C_{max}, AUC, T_{1/2}, T_{max}

Results/conclusion:
C_{max} at the 5 and 20 mg/kg dose levels was 1361 and 7708 ng/mL, respectively.
AUC was 11513 and 68233 hr\cdotng/mL for the 5 and 20 mg/kg doses, respectively.
Both the C_{max} and AUC showed dose-dependent increases.
The t_{1/2} for 5 and 20 mg/kg was not dose dependent, being 6.11 and 4.58 hours, respectively.

Table 3: Mean Pharmacokinetic Parameters following a Single Oral Administration to Female Nude Mice (Study AV951-08028)
Dose (mg/kg)	C_{max}a (ng/mL)	T_{max} (hr)	AUC0-∞ (hr\cdotng/mL)a	t_{1/2} (hr)
5	1361	2	11513	6.11
20	7708	2	68233	4.58

aUnits for AUC and C_{max} were converted from micrograms/L. to nanograms/mL.
(excerpted from Applicant's report)
L1905: Figure showing box plots of Serum Tivozanib Cavg (ng/mL) across four regimens (Original, Regimen 1, Regimen 2, Regimen 3) for three levels of renal impairment (Normal, Mild, Moderate).
Legend: NCI-ODWG Normal (Red), Mild (Green), Moderate (Blue).
Original: 1.5 mg capsule QD for all groups.
Regimen 1: 1.5 mg capsule QD (normal or mild HI) or 1.0 mg capsule QD (moderate HI).
Regimen 2: 1.5 mg capsule QD (normal or mild HI) or 1.5 mg capsule QOD (moderate HI).
Regimen 3: 1.5 mg capsule QD (normal or mild HI) or 1.0 mg capsule QOD (moderate HI - continuous dosing).
Except for continuous dosing, all other regimens assumed 3 weeks dosing followed by 1 week without dosing.
Boxes show median and interquartile range (25% - 75%); Whiskers cover the 95% Confidence interval.
Source: Figure 6 in the sponsor's PopPK and ER report dated 7/22/2020.
Note that 1.5 mg capsule in this figure is equivalent to 1.34 mg tivozanib, 1.0 mg capsule in this figure is equivalent to 0.89 mg tivozanib.
L1906: Diagram of the Tivozanib Structural Model. The model shows a dose entering a series of transit compartments (Atr1, Atr2, ..., AtrN) connected sequentially by absorption transit rate (Ktr). The transit absorption model is defined by Ktr = (NTR+1)/MTT. The output of the transit compartments flows into the Central compartment (V/F). The Central compartment is connected to CL/F (Apparent clearance).
L1907: Figure 5 displays three scatter plots illustrating the Goodness of Fit (GoF) for the population pharmacokinetic model of tivozanib. The plots show Conditional Weighted Residuals (CWR) versus three different variables: PRED (Population predicted serum tivozanib concentrations), TAD (Time after last dose), and TIME (Time after first dose). The CWR values are scattered around the horizontal line at 0.0, indicating a good fit. The blue line and green shading represent the Loess smoothing line and 95% Confidence Interval (CI), respectively. The black dashed line represents the Line of Identity.
L1908: Figure 6 displays six Predictive Corrected Visual Predictive Check (PCVPC) plots for individual subjects (AV-951-09-109, AV-951-12-118, AV-951-07-201, AV-951-10-202, AV-951-09-301, AV-951-15-303). The plots show Serum Tivozanib Concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (Y-axis) versus Time (X-axis). The X-axis labels vary: Time after Dose (h) for the top row, and Time after first dose (h) for the bottom row. The plots compare individual observations (circles) against the population prediction (blue line) and the 95% Prediction Interval (green shading). The black dashed line represents the Line of Identity. The plots generally show that the observed concentrations fall within the predicted intervals, supporting the model's predictive performance.
L1909: Visual Predictive Check Study KRN951/03-B01 by Dose - Final PK Model (Run 22)
Three plots showing Serum Tivozanib Concentration (ng/mL) versus Time after first dose (h) for doses 1 mg, 1.5 mg, and 2 mg. The plots display observed concentrations (circles), median predicted concentrations (solid blue line), and 5th and 95th percentiles of observed concentrations (dashed lines). Shaded areas indicate the 90% CI around the median (green), and 5th and 95th percentiles of the simulated concentrations (grey). All observations and predictions were adjusted using prediction correction [9].
